| `linalg.CoMoment.Corr` divided by `sqrt(M2ii) * sqrt(M2jj)` | `Corr` divides by `sqrt(M2ii * M2jj)` (the `TEST_PEARSON_R` arithmetic), falling back to the two-root form when the product overflows or underflows, so a matrix cell can equal `TEST_PEARSON_R` bit for bit on amd64 | behaviour (last ulps) | a caller comparing `Corr` bits against stored values regenerates them; no Pulse code outside the matrix slot called it | U16 |
| `mergegate.MergeRefusal` refused any request without an aggregator | a request carrying only mergeable matrices is admitted (parallel decode, shards) | internal | none; results are bit-identical to serial | U16 |

Not shipped, by decision: `precision` on matrix cells (U17), `MaxMatrixDim` and a buckets x p^2 guard (U19), `repair: "nearest"` and non-PSD refusal by decompositions (U24), raw p-values and intervals on correlation and a matrix overlay host (U28), matrices in crosstab arms (U28), `BlockMerger` through `extend` (U30).

## Changes from U17 (response shaping core)

Complete for U17 (no release tag cut by the unit). Contract: `docs/src/contract/payload-schema.md` (Return slot); guide `docs/src/library/response-shaping.md`; skill `skills/response-shaping.md`. Additive: a request without `return`, on an instance with no default, is byte-identical in request hash, response and components, and `format_version` stays `"1.1"`.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| no way to choose response parts or float digits | `types.Request.Return` and `types.ComposedRequest.Return` (`types.Return {Preset, Include, Exclude, Precision}`; `types.ReturnPreset` with `ReturnPresetFull` / `Standard` / `Minimal`, `types.AllReturnPresets()`) | added | none; opt in per request. Compose-level `return` accepts `overlays...` paths only | U17 |
| `Response` always carried every computed part | `types.Response.Returned`, `ComposedResponse.Returned` (`types.ReturnedMarker {Preset, Digest, Precision}`), `StreamChunk.Returned` (terminal chunk only), `Returned()` and `MarshalRow` on the `ProcessStream` iterator | added | a shaped response is a projection, not schema-valid against the full payload schema; an excluded part is absent on the wire and zero / nil on the Go value, so read `Returned` before trusting an empty slot | U17 |
| `Components.Aggregations[i]` described the ungrouped run only | `types.AggregationComponents.Groups` (one entry per group, in `data` order: group key, `n`, `n_null`, weighted floor keys, operator map), on buffered, streaming and sharded runs; terminal chunk only when streaming | added | none; `DisableComponents` still yields the pre-Components wire | U17 |
| no instance default for response shape | `pulse.Options.DefaultReturn` (a `*types.Return`) and `pulse.FeatureProfile.Return` (JSON `return`); precedence request `return`, then `DefaultReturn`, then profile `return`, then `full`; a request block REPLACES the default | added | set one default per instance; a bad preset, precision or path fails `pulse.New` (`PULSE_RETURN_INVALID` / `_PATH_UNKNOWN`), a profile `return` fails with `PULSE_FEATURE_PROFILE_INVALID` reason `invalid_return` | U17 |
| `DisableComponents` with request shaping | an engine `DisableComponents` always wins over a request `return` block: components are not computed and the block gains `exclude: ["components"]` (the response carries a `returned` marker). Only an explicit request `disable_components: false` re-opens them, exactly as before U17; `DisableComponents` alone stays byte-identical with no marker | added | none | U17 |
| `descriptor.PredictResult` and the manifest had no return keys | `PredictResult.Return` (`descriptor.ReturnPlan`: expanded paths, `rp1:` digest), manifest `return_presets` (`[{name, paths}]`, expanded against the instance) | added | none | U17 |
| no return error codes | `PULSE_RETURN_INVALID`, `PULSE_RETURN_PATH_UNKNOWN`, `PULSE_RETURN_PATH_UNMATCHED` (warning, buffered runs only) | added | resolve with `pulse errors lookup CODE`; the manifest golden changed (error list only) | U17 |

Not shipped, by decision: a `pulse.ReturnedMarker` root alias (U33 decides before the freeze; use `types.ReturnedMarker`); `Response.overlays` is not yet feature-gated, so hiding overlays leaves its paths listed (U35).

## Changes from U18 (response shaping execution)

Landed in U18 (no release tag cut by the unit). The library default is unchanged: a request without `return`, on an instance with no default, stays byte-identical and `format_version` stays `"1.1"`.

> **Release note — `breaking` for MCP users.** The MCP tools now shape a request WITHOUT its own `return` block by the `standard` preset: `pulse_process`, `pulse_predict`, every `pulse_compose` slot and every `pulse_process_chain` stage drop `components` (and `matrices[*].auxiliary`, regression `credible_intervals` / `selection`, test details other than `effect_size`) and stamp `returned {preset: "standard", digest}`. An agent that needs everything sends `"return": {"preset": "full"}`; an operator restores the old output with `pulse mcp --return full` (or `gosdk.Config{DefaultReturn: types.ReturnPresetFull}` / `mcpserve.Options{DefaultReturn: ...}`). The library default stays `full`.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MCP tools returned the full Response unless a request carried `return` | MCP default preset `standard`; precedence request `return`, then host `gosdk.Config.DefaultReturn` / `mcpserve.Options.DefaultReturn` / `pulse mcp --return`, then the instance default (`pulse.Options.DefaultReturn`, else the feature profile's `return`), then `standard`. An engine `DisableComponents` still keeps components off; only a request `disable_components: false` re-opens them | behaviour change — **breaking (MCP wire)** | MCP clients that read `components` (or other non-`standard` parts) send `return: {"preset": "full"}` or include the paths; hosts wanting the old output set the host default to `full` | U18 |
| no host knob for the MCP response shape | `gosdk.Config.DefaultReturn` and `mcpserve.Options.DefaultReturn` (`types.ReturnPreset`; zero value defers to the instance default, else `standard`), CLI `pulse mcp --return PRESET` | added | none; `Register` / `Serve` / `pulse mcp` refuse an unknown preset with `PULSE_RETURN_INVALID` | U18 |
| an excluded overlay, test, post-test or regression slot still ran and could refuse or warn | an excluded part is not computed, so it raises no refusal or warning (`PULSE_OVERLAY_COMPONENTS_REQUIRED`, `PULSE_TEST_*`, `PROCESSING_REGRESSION_*`, `PULSE_WEIGHT_LOW_NEFF`); `Predict` stays the validator. Chain stages still refuse tests / regressions (`PULSE_CHAIN_NOT_MERGEABLE`); a partly skipped Go `tests` / `post_tests` slice holds nil at skipped positions | behaviour change — **only for requests with an excluding `return`** | run `Predict` to validate a request before shaping it; keep a part in `return` to have it validated and computed | U18 |
| no per-section size figure | `PredictResult.Sizes` (`descriptor.ResponseSectionSize`, on every request) and `PredictResult.Return.UnresolvedIncludes` | added | none | U18 |

## Changes from U19 (resource limits)

Landed in U19 (no release tag cut by the unit). Defaults are high enough that no embedded example or golden request trips them (`TestLimitsDefaultsNeverTripGoldens`). Guide: [Tuning Limits](../../src/library/tuning-limits.md).

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| no instance resource limits | `pulse.Limits` (alias of the internal struct; fields `RequestTimeout`, `MaxGroups`, `MaxCrosstabCells`, `MaxEstimatedMemory`, `MaxMatrixDim`, `MaxComposeSlots`, `MaxChainStages`, `MaxJoinBuildRows`), `pulse.Unlimited` (`-1`), `pulse.Default*` constants, `Options.Limits`, `(*Pulse).Limits()` | added | none unless you want to tighten. `0` = default, `-1` = unlimited, other negatives fail `pulse.New` with `PULSE_LIMIT_INVALID`; precedence per field: `Options.Limits`, then the profile `limits`, then the default | U19 |
| feature profile refused a `limits` key | `pulse.FeatureProfileLimits` (snake_case JSON; `request_timeout` is a duration string) on `FeatureProfile.Limits`; a bad value is `PULSE_FEATURE_PROFILE_INVALID` reason `invalid_limits` | added | a profile that used no `limits` key is unaffected | U19 |
| (none) | `descriptor.PredictResult.LimitFindings` (`[]descriptor.LimitFinding {limit, configured, estimated, grade}`), `descriptor.LimitGradeCertain` / `LimitGradePossible` | added (`omitempty`) | a `certain` finding also makes `Valid` false with `PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`; a `possible` one is informational | U19 |
| (none) | `descriptor.LimitMeta`, `Manifest.Limits` (`[{name, value, default, unit}]`), `Manifest.LimitsDigest` (`lim1:` + SHA-256); `pulse mcp --limit name=value` (repeatable) | added | agents read `manifest.limits` and re-fetch when `limits_digest` moves; `feature_set_digest` does not change with limits | U19 |
| (none) | error codes `PULSE_LIMIT_INVALID`, `PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` (details `{limit, configured, observed, option}`) | added | resolve with `pulse errors lookup CODE`; the manifest golden changed (error list, limits block) | U19 |
| serial per-record loops ignored the caller ctx | serial streaming, buffered materialize, fused and buffered crosstab, join build and facet loops poll ctx on the first row and every 4,096 rows | behaviour change | a cancelled or timed-out ctx now stops a run it used to ignore, with the ctx error unchanged (`context.Canceled` / `DeadlineExceeded`) | U19 |
| `ComposeParallel` with `FailFast` reported the lowest-index failure, siblings' `context.Canceled` included | reports the lowest-index failure that is NOT a sibling cancellation (the error that tripped FailFast); a done caller ctx keeps the lowest-index rule | behaviour change | none unless you matched on the old, sometimes `context.Canceled`, winner | U19 |
| `ComposeParallel` with a ctx done before every slot launched returned a "successful" `ComposedResponse` with nil slots | returns the ctx error | behaviour change (bug fix) | handle the error | U19 |
| join `Process` / joined crosstab decoded the right side before refusing a bad request | defaults, zones, field refs and the limits pre-flight run BEFORE the right side decodes; an unknown field is refused without decoding either side | behaviour change | none; error codes unchanged, refusals are earlier | U19 |
| matrix `PredictResult.Matrices[*].EstimatedBytes` for grouped requests was buckets x accumulator | blocks x buckets x accumulator, per shard (the real merge-block state) | value change (in place) | re-baseline any threshold you derived from it; it is larger | U19 |
| crosstab return-size model left a `GROUP_DATE_RANGES` axis unsized | `PredictResult.Sizes` sizes that axis through the shared estimator | value change | none | U19 |
| `Options.Limits.RequestTimeout` absent | an inner deadline on each top-level call; a trip is `PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` `{limit: "request_timeout"}`, the caller's own deadline stays `context.DeadlineExceeded` | added (opt-in) | none | U19 |

Not shipped, by decision: a `MaxOutputRows` limit (output volume is the embedder's to manage); a `GOMEMLIMIT`-derived default for `MaxEstimatedMemory` (it is opt-in and unlimited, so `MaxEstimatedMemory` never couples to the Go runtime); `MaxGroups` / `MaxCrosstabCells` as `certain` predict findings (they are `possible`: dictionary-size upper bounds); limit findings for Compose and chain validators (`ValidateCompose` / `ValidateChain`).

## Changes from U20 (observability)

Landed in U20 (`v1.0.0-alpha.6`, with the `contrib/otelpulse` and `contrib/prompulse` tags). Everything is additive and silent by default. Guide: [Observability](../../src/library/observability.md).

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| (none) | `Options.Logger *slog.Logger`, `Options.Hooks *observe.Hooks`, `Options.Metrics observe.Metrics` | added | none; nil on all three is the old behaviour, with an allocation-identical off path. Pulse never reads `slog.Default()` | U20 |
| (none) | public package `github.com/frankbardon/pulse/observe` (`Hooks`, `OperationInfo`, `OperationResult`, `PhaseTiming`, `OperationKind`, `Phase`, `Arm`, `Scope`, `AllOperationKinds`, `Metrics`, `Counter`, `Histogram`, `UpDownCounter`, `Label`); stdlib only | added | import it to write hooks or a metrics adapter; the public-API golden changed | U20 |
| (none) | separate modules `github.com/frankbardon/pulse/contrib/otelpulse` and `.../contrib/prompulse`, tagged `contrib/<module>/v1.0.0-alpha.6` in lockstep with the root | added | `go get` the one you need at the same version as Pulse; the core `go.mod` gains no OpenTelemetry or Prometheus dependency | U20 |
| `Synth`, `Profile` and `Manifest` took an unnamed (`_`) context parameter | the parameter is named `ctx` | no-op | none: no signature change, `apidiff` neutral | U20 |
| `mcpserve.ServeInfo` was `==`-comparable | `ServeInfo.DefaultReturn` and `ServeInfo.Limits` (a slice) added; see the U04 table | breaking only for `==` | compare fields | U20 |
| the `pulse mcp` startup line (stderr) named the data dir, tools and feature profile | the line is printed after `Register` and also names the effective `return` preset, tuned limits, log level and the bound metrics address | behaviour change | parse the stderr line by key if you scrape it; stdout stays the JSON-RPC stream | U20 |
| (none) | global CLI flags `--log-level off\|debug\|info\|warn\|error` and `--log-format text\|json` on every leaf (stderr only); `pulse mcp --metrics-addr HOST:PORT` serving unauthenticated `GET /metrics` | added | none; no port opens unless `--metrics-addr` is given | U20 |

Not shipped, by decision: `pulse_cache_hits_total`; a `PULSE_*` environment variable for logging; recovery of a panic raised inside an operation (hook panics were recovered here; the rest shipped in U56, see "Changes from U56").

## Changes from U24 (matrix operators)

Complete for U24 (no release tag cut by the unit). Contract: `.claude/reference/matrix-and-vectors.md`; guides `multivariate-design`, `regression-inference`, `docs/src/library/matrices.md`. Additive: a request without the new params or `vcov` is byte-identical, and `format_version` stays `"1.1"`.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `types.AllMatrixTypes()` listed `MAT_COVARIANCE`, `MAT_CORRELATION` | adds `MAT_PARTIAL_CORRELATION`, `MAT_RELIABILITY`, `MAT_PCA`, `MAT_COLLINEARITY` | added | none; a profile that lists matrix operators by name opts the new ones in | U24 |
| `MatrixKind` was `square_symmetric` only | adds `types.MatrixKindRectangular` (`MAT_PCA` loadings and eigenvectors, `MAT_COLLINEARITY` variance decomposition) | added | a consumer switching on `kind` handles `rectangular` (`row_keys` the members, `column_keys` the columns) | U24 |
| `MatrixSpec.Streamable()` was a property of the operator type | streamability and mergeability are per spec: `MatrixSpec.Mergeable()` added; a `MAT_CORRELATION` spec with `params.method` `spearman` / `kendall` is buffered and not mergeable | added / behaviour | none for built-ins; the request routes buffered and serial automatically | U24 |
| `descriptor.MatrixPredict` had no buffer figures | `Mergeable bool` (`mergeable`) and `RowBufferBytes *int64` (`row_buffer_bytes`, omitted when not buffered) | added | none | U24 |
| `TEST_KENDALL_TAU` classified pairs in an O(n^2) loop | runs the shared Knight O(n log n) kernel (`kendallCounts`), also used by `MAT_CORRELATION` `method: "kendall"` | behaviour (performance, internal) | outputs are unchanged (property-tested against the old loop on tied, zero-weighted data); a caller comparing Kendall bits against stored values on a different architecture is unaffected, the counts are integer-exact | U24 |
| `PULSE_MATRIX_NOT_PSD` was a per-matrix warning everywhere | a warning on `MAT_COVARIANCE` / `MAT_CORRELATION`; FATAL on `MAT_PARTIAL_CORRELATION`, `MAT_PCA`, `MAT_COLLINEARITY` unless `params.repair: "nearest"` (a warning then, with `frobenius_adjustment`); `MAT_RELIABILITY` keeps alpha and nulls omega | behaviour | add `params.repair: "nearest"`, or use listwise deletion; `details.repair_options` names the fix | U24 |
| `PULSE_MATRIX_SINGULAR` details were `pivot`, `reason`, `n` | adds `rank`, `condition_number`, `dependent_indices`, and `matrix` + `dependent_fields` from a matrix operator | added | none | U24 |
| no iterative-solver or identification warnings | `errors.PULSE_MATRIX_NOT_CONVERGED`, `PULSE_MATRIX_HEYWOOD`, `PULSE_MATRIX_NOT_IDENTIFIED`, `PULSE_MATRIX_PAIRWISE_N_STAR` (warnings), `PROCESSING_REGRESSION_VCOV_UNSUPPORTED` (fatal, lasso / elastic net only, `details.reason` `penalty`) | added | `pulse errors lookup CODE`; the manifest error list and golden changed | U24 |
| `RegressionSpec` and `RegressionResult` carried no covariance | `RegressionSpec.Vcov bool` (`vcov`, flat on the slot) and `RegressionResult.Vcov` / `.Correlation` (`*MatrixValues`, keyed `(intercept)` then the predictors); absent when off | added | opt in with `"vcov": true` on `REG_OLS` / `REG_GLM` / `REG_BAYES_LINEAR`; `regressions[*].vcov` / `.correlation` ride the `standard` return preset (the MCP default), not `minimal` | U24 |
| a matrices-hidden profile removed `MatrixValues` from the payload schema | `MatrixValues` stays in the schema of a matrices-hidden instance, because the ungated `regressions` slot can now return it | behaviour (schema) | none; regenerate any stored payload-schema snapshot | U24 |
| `MAT_CORRELATION` `KnownAs` | the matrix carries "rank correlation", "spearman correlation matrix", "kendall correlation matrix"; the bare `spearman` / `kendall` aliases stay on `TEST_SPEARMAN_R` / `TEST_KENDALL_TAU` | behaviour (search) | none; an owner call may move them (see the U24 review record) | U24 |

Not shipped, by decision: p-values and intervals on rank and partial correlation (U28), `MAT_FACTOR` and rotation (U25), `vcov` for lasso and elastic net fits (resample fits report the replicate covariance, selection fits the final refit's).

## Changes from U41 (derived cohort)

Complete for U41 (no release tag cut by the unit; rides the next `v1.0.0-alpha.N`). Contract: `docs/src/cli/flags.md` (`--schema` / `--ignore-extra`, `--float`, `api materialize`), skills `tool-import`, `derived-cohorts`, `tool-materialize`, `item-fan-out`, `op-win-ewma`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` format change — only the stored schema-block offsets of explicit-schema and schema-file cohorts move to the canonical layout (record payloads unchanged).

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| an explicit `ImportJob.Schema` / `ConvertJob.Schema` mapped to source columns by POSITION (`CsvColumnIdx` = schema array index), so a reordered schema silently swapped same-typed columns | matched by exact header NAME on every adapter (Arrow / Parquet / SPSS included when the explicit schema overrides the source's), on a copy of the schema; any order works. A field with no column, a column no field names, a repeated name, or an NDJSON / JSON-array key first seen after the first object is refused before any row | behaviour change | give every schema field the exact source column name; set `IgnoreExtra` when the schema deliberately names a subset of the columns (previously such columns were silently ignored) | U41 |
| (none) | `io.ImportJob.IgnoreExtra`, `io.ConvertJob.IgnoreExtra` (CLI `--ignore-extra` on `import <fmt>`, `import predict`, `convert`) | added | set it to drop unnamed source columns and late keys instead of refusing | U41 |
| (none) | `errors.PULSE_IMPORT_SCHEMA_FIELD_MISSING`, `PULSE_IMPORT_SCHEMA_COLUMN_UNMAPPED`, `PULSE_IMPORT_SCHEMA_DUPLICATE`, `PULSE_IMPORT_SCHEMA_KEY_LATE` | added | `pulse errors lookup CODE`; the manifest error list changed | U41 |
| an inferred all-float column took `f32` whenever every sampled value was inside f32's RANGE, so ordinary decimals rounded to ~7 significant digits and money sums drifted | inferred floats are `f64`; `f32` only on opt-in — `io.ImportJob.FloatWidth` / `io.ConvertJob.FloatWidth` `"f32"` (CLI `--float f32` on `import <fmt>`, `import predict`, `import schema-template`, `convert`), which restores the old rule including out-of-sample `PULSE_IMPORT_WIDTH_PROMOTED`. Any other value is `PULSE_IMPORT_FLOAT_WIDTH_INVALID` | behaviour change | re-imported cohorts carry `f64` float fields (wider rows, exact values); set `FloatWidth: "f32"` to keep the old width, or force one column with `ColumnTypeOverrides` / managed `force_type` | U41 |
| an unknown `type` name in a CLI schema file (`--schema` on `import <fmt>`, `import predict`, `convert`) silently became `f64` | refused with `PULSE_IMPORT_SCHEMA_TYPE_UNKNOWN` (`field`, `type`, `valid`) before any row is read | behaviour change | spell every `type` as a registered field type name (`pulse import schema-template INPUT` emits valid ones) | U41 |
| (none) | `io.ImportJob.FloatWidth`, `io.ConvertJob.FloatWidth`; `errors.PULSE_IMPORT_FLOAT_WIDTH_INVALID`, `PULSE_IMPORT_SCHEMA_TYPE_UNKNOWN` | added | see the two rows above | U41 |
| (none) | `pulse.ImportSpec.Schema` (`*encoding.Schema`), `.IgnoreExtra`, `.FloatWidth` — the managed import (`ImportFile`, MCP `pulse_import` `schema` / `ignore_extra` / `float`) takes an explicit schema matched by name, under the same refusals as `ImportJob`; none is persisted on the managed sidecar. A schema on a `.pulse` source is `PULSE_IMPORT_SCHEMA_INVALID` | added | set them on the spec; an unset spec imports exactly as before | U41 |
| a malformed CLI schema file (`--schema`) failed with an uncoded error (`--json` `errors[0].code` the `CLI_ERROR` placeholder); an empty array `[]` was accepted | both refused with `errors.PULSE_IMPORT_SCHEMA_INVALID` (`reason` `malformed` / `empty`) | behaviour change | match on the code; write at least one field, or omit `--schema` to infer | U41 |
| an explicit `ImportJob.Schema` / `ConvertJob.Schema` / `ImportSpec.Schema` stored the caller's `Field.ByteOffset` / `BitPosition` verbatim (all zeros when unset), so a shard `add` with diverging dictionaries could rewrite a neighbouring field's bytes, a nonzero `BitPosition` read bit-packed values back wrong through the engine, and the cohort failed shard cohesion against an inferred twin | caller-supplied `ByteOffset` / `BitPosition` are ignored: every explicit-schema import stores the canonical layout (a bit-packed field owns one whole byte at bit 0); the shard dictionary rewrite addresses bytes by that type-derived layout whatever an older shard stores. Record payloads are unchanged | behaviour change | stop setting `ByteOffset` / `BitPosition` on an explicit schema. A cohort imported before this with zero offsets keeps them and fails `shard add` cohesion against a newly imported twin; until [TODO #373](../TODO.md) makes such cohorts interoperate without re-import, re-import it | U41 |
| a CLI schema file (`--schema`) with a bit-packed field (`u4`, `packed_bool`) ahead of other fields stored every later field's offset one byte short (it summed `ByteSize()`) | stored at the canonical layout; record payloads unchanged | value change (schema block only) | a schema-file cohort of that shape written before this fails `shard add` cohesion against one written after; until [TODO #373](../TODO.md) lands, re-import the older one | U41 |
| (none) | `(*Pulse).Materialize(ctx, req, out, MaterializeOptions) (*MaterializeResult, error)`, `pulse.MaterializeOptions {Overwrite, Strict}`, `pulse.MaterializeResult {Target, Records, DerivedColumns, InvalidatedSidecars, Warnings}`; CLI `pulse api materialize`, MCP `pulse_materialize`, feature `capability:materialize` (depends on `capability:process`); `observe.OpMaterialize` | added | none. A request's derived columns become `f64` fields of a new single-file cohort; the request may carry `sort` (output row order, `Process`'s comparator, keys naming source fields or derived columns — rows move, window values do not); a feature profile that lists features opts the tool and leaf in by name. A hook or metrics consumer switching on `observe.OperationKind` sees one more kind | U41 |
| (none) | `errors.PULSE_MATERIALIZE_SLOT_UNSUPPORTED`, `PULSE_MATERIALIZE_NO_DERIVED`, `PULSE_MATERIALIZE_OUTPUT_UNDECLARED`, `PULSE_MATERIALIZE_OUTPUT_NOT_NUMERIC` (refusals before any write), `PULSE_MATERIALIZE_SIDECARS_INVALIDATED` (warning on `Overwrite`) | added | `pulse errors lookup CODE`; the manifest error list changed | U41 |
| `WIN_EWMA` took `params.alpha` only; a missing alpha was refused with "params.alpha is required" | `params.decay` beside `alpha` (`alpha = 1 - decay`, computed as an exact decimal from the JSON literal, so `decay: 0.7` is bit-identical to `alpha: 0.3`); exactly one is required, and both or neither is refused with "exactly one of params.alpha or params.decay is required (alpha = 1 - decay)" (same code) | added / message change | none for `alpha` requests; match on the code, not the old message | U41 |
| (none) | `types.ForEach {Axes, Mode}` on `types.Window`, `types.Feature`, `types.Attribute` (`for_each`), expanded in place before validation; `errors.PULSE_FOR_EACH_INVALID`; feature `capability:for_each`; `descriptor.PredictResult.ForEach` (`[]descriptor.ForEachSummary {path, axes, mode, expanded_count}`, `omitempty`) | added | none; an item without `for_each` is byte-identical on the wire and in `CanonicalHash`, and an expanded request hashes like its hand-written twin. Predict before running a large grid | U41 |
| (none) | `Limits.MaxDerivedColumns`, `FeatureProfileLimits.MaxDerivedColumns` (`max_derived_columns`), `pulse.DefaultMaxDerivedColumns` (1,000): plain plus expanded window / feature / attribute items, over it `PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` | added | none unless a request carries more than 1,000 derived items; `manifest.limits` gains an entry, so `limits_digest` moves | U41 |
| (none) | `ParamMeta.ExactlyOneOf`, `descriptor.OperatorParamMeta.ExactlyOneOf`, `descriptor.Param.ExactlyOneOf` (`exactly_one_of`, `omitempty`): params that form a one-of group (`WIN_EWMA` `alpha` / `decay`, `FEAT_BUCKETIZE` `boundaries` / `quantiles`) | added | a manifest reader treats a tagged group as "supply exactly one"; `WIN_EWMA` `alpha` is no longer marked `required`. Recommend drafts `WIN_EWMA` and `FEAT_BUCKETIZE` again (the manifest golden changed) | U41 |
| (none) | `descriptor.Operator.IgnoresFrame` (`ignores_frame`, `omitempty`): a window that takes a frame but never reads it (`WIN_EWMA`, cumulative) | added | a manifest reader treats the frame as inert for a flagged operator; a materialized column's description omits it (the manifest golden changed) | U41 |
| (none) | `descriptor.Operator.ReadsExpression` (`reads_expression`, `omitempty`): an operator whose defining input is the slot's `Expression`, not a `Params` entry (`ATTR_FORMULA`, `FILTER_EXPRESSION`) | added | a manifest reader knows the `expression` slot is required and read for a flagged operator and ignored by every other one; a materialized formula column's description quotes the expression (`ATTR_FORMULA = a * 0.9 — derived from …`) instead of naming its ignored `field`, and a regression attribute's names its `penalty` / `alpha` / `l1_ratio` (the manifest golden changed) | U41 |
| a cohort-bound MCP input schema carried each enum-bound string on a `features[]` / `attributes[]` / `windows[]` item as `{"type": "string", "enum": [...]}` | when `capability:for_each` is enabled, those properties are `{"type": "string", "anyOf": [{"enum": [...]}, {"pattern": <axis placeholder>}]}`, and the item gains `if {required: [for_each]}` / `else` (no placeholder); an item without `for_each` still rejects a value outside the enum | behaviour change (schema shape) | a client that reads a property's `enum` directly finds it at `enum` or, on these items, as the first `anyOf` branch | U41 |
| a stored request template substituted every `{{name}}` / `{"$var": "name"}` it found | the renderer leaves a `for_each` item's own axis placeholders for the expansion; a declared variable named like an axis is refused at load with `errors.PULSE_TEMPLATE_AXIS_COLLISION` | added / behaviour change | rename a template variable that collides with an axis name | U41 |

## Changes from U42 (fit scoring)

In progress for U42. Contract: `.claude/reference/execution-modes.md` (Scoring a bound fit); skills `op-attr-reg-fitted`, `op-attr-reg-residual`, `op-attr-reg-leverage`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"` (a null figure was already `null` on the wire).

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ATTR_REG_FITTED`, `ATTR_REG_RESIDUAL` and `ATTR_REG_LEVERAGE` wrote `0` for a row with a null predictor (and `ATTR_REG_RESIDUAL` for a null target), so a downstream `AGG_AVERAGE` / `AGG_SUM` / error figure averaged those rows in as zeros | the row's output is null (as `ATTR_REG_PREDICT`): a downstream aggregation counts it in `n_null` and skips it, and a materialized or streamed row carries `null`. A null target alone still gets a fitted value and a leverage; a row the weighted refit excludes keeps leverage `0` | behaviour change | none if every input row is complete. Otherwise averages over the column change (they now cover only the defined rows) and `n` / `n_null` move | U42 |

## Changes from U27 (vector metrics and aggregates)

Complete for U27 (no release tag cut by the unit). Contract: `.claude/reference/matrix-and-vectors.md` (Metric registry, `MAT_DISTANCE`); skills `op-mat-distance`, `op-group-kmeans`, `expression-language`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`. Every metric now resolves through the public `linalg/metric` registry (manifest `matrix.metrics`); no consumer keeps a local list or kernel.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MAT_DISTANCE` `params.metric` `"sqeuclidean"` | `"sq_euclidean"` (the registry spelling); `"sqeuclidean"` is refused with `SERVICE_VALIDATION` `reason` `bad_params`, `details.valid` the registry's `MAT_DISTANCE` list. No alias | behaviour change (rename) | respell the metric; the components operator key `metric` and the manifest enum carry the new name | U27 |
| `MAT_DISTANCE` accepted `euclidean`, `sqeuclidean`, `manhattan`, `chebyshev`, `cosine`, `mahalanobis` | adds `hellinger` and `aitchison` (compositions; refused beside `standardize`); the enum is `metric.Accepted(metric.ConsumerMatDistance)` | added | none | U27 |
| a `MAT_DISTANCE` pair whose centroids had ANY undefined (NaN) or infinite coordinate was null — in practice every standardized distance touching a zero-spread member | pairwise-complete (the registry's one policy): an undefined coordinate is dropped and the element sums (`euclidean`, `sq_euclidean`, `manhattan`) are rescaled by p / p_observed (R `stats::dist()`); a zero-spread member under `standardize` has no z-score and is skipped; `mahalanobis` still needs every coordinate. ±Inf is an ordinary value (an infinite centroid gives an infinite distance, not null) | behaviour change (values) | expect defined distances where nulls were; set `params.min_valid` to the member count to restore "null unless every member is defined" | U27 |
| (none) | `MAT_DISTANCE` `params.min_valid` (int, default 1, 1 … p): the fewest complete member pairs a distance needs, null below it | added | none | U27 |
| (none) | `errors.PULSE_MATRIX_METRIC_DOMAIN` (per-matrix warning: a negative centroid coordinate under `hellinger` / `aitchison`, or an aitchison zero part; the cells touching it are null) | added | `pulse errors lookup CODE`; the manifest error list changed | U27 |
| the manifest `matrix.metrics` listed `expr` as a consumer of every row-local metric (E1-S1 interim) | `expr` lists exactly the metrics an expression function exposes — `euclidean` (`dist`), `cosine` (similarity only), `jaccard`, `dice`, `hamming`, `overlap` | behaviour (manifest) | none; the manifest golden changed | U27 |
| the expression `dist(a, b)` summed the complete pairs without rescaling (unreleased U26 behaviour) | `dist` is the registry's `euclidean`: a partial pair is rescaled by p / p_observed, as `MAT_DISTANCE` | behaviour change (values, partial vectors only) | none for complete vectors (bits unchanged) | U27 |
| (none) | New reserved names: the expression built-in `ref`, and the operators `ATTR_PROFILE_SIMILARITY` and `ATTR_VEC_DISTANCE` (the built-ins `ATTR_SCALE_SCORE`, `AGG_VEC_MEAN`, `AGG_VEC_SUM` are ordinary built-in collisions) | added (reserved names) | an embedder `ExprFunction` named `ref`, or an extension operator of those names, is refused `PULSE_EXTENSION_NAME_RESERVED`; rename it | U27 |
| (none) | `Extensions.ReferenceVectors` (`types.ReferenceVectorTable{Description, Labels, Profiles}`), validated at `pulse.New`; `extensions.reference_vectors` in the manifest lists labels and keys, never values; `errors.PULSE_VECTOR_REFERENCE_UNKNOWN` / `_MISMATCH` | added | register a profile and read it with `ref("table", "key")`; `pulse errors lookup CODE` | U27 |
| `MAT_RELIABILITY` used only its own `scale_min` / `scale_max` | a vector `kind: scale` with `scale_min` / `scale_max` feeds `MAT_RELIABILITY` when the spec has none, and out-of-range values are range-checked against them | behaviour change | declare bounds on the spec, or on the vector, to match the data | U27 |
| multi-shard `AGG_VEC_*` and `MAT_*` figures agreed with the single-file twin only to rounding | a shard archive matches its single file bit for bit (merge blocks are keyed by global ordinal), across `ShardWorkers` / `DecodeWorkers` | behaviour change (last bit) | none; a stored multi-shard figure may differ in its last bit. An extension's own `Merge` stays outside the blocked merge, so it is ULP-equal across workers | U27 |
| extension aggregators refused vector input | an extension aggregator may declare `VectorInput` and `ArrayValued`; the built-in array-valued refusals (crosstab, sort, windows, post-tests, overlays) apply to it | added | opt in on the registration | U27 |
| aggregation, grouper and attribute items required `field` in the payload schema / MCP | `field` is no longer required (a vector aggregator takes `fields`); the payload-schema golden changed | behaviour (schema) | regenerate any stored payload-schema snapshot | U27 |
| attributes had no components entry | `Response.Components.Attributes` (one `AttributeComponents` per attribute slot declaring a `ComponentSchema`, keyed by `label` + `type`) | added | none; additive `omitempty` | U27 |
| (none) | `errors.PULSE_VECTOR_METRIC_DOMAIN` and `errors.PULSE_VECTOR_REFERENCE_MISSING` (runtime warnings: rows a reference comparison nulls because of a metric-domain violation, or a missing / null `key_field` key; the keys named are the 10 smallest unmatched) | added | `pulse errors lookup CODE`; the manifest error list changed. Both are absent on a clean run | U27 |
| `key_field` on a reference lookup (new) | a categorical or unsigned-integer (`u4`–`u64`) column; a float, date or set `key_field` is refused (`key_field_type`). A `u64` key above 2^53 is matched via its float64 value | added | none | U27 |

## Changes from U54 (multi-grouper)

Complete for U54 (no release tag cut by the unit). Contract: `.claude/reference/execution-modes.md` (Two or more groupers), `.claude/reference/predict-inspect.md`; skill `grouper-design`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; a single-grouper request is byte-identical.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `groups` with two or more entries silently ran only `Groups[0]` | every entry executes: one row per OBSERVED key combination, ordered by each grouper's native key order (outer to inner), every grouper's key column on each row; `Components.Groupers` holds one entry per slot and `groups[].group_key` / grouped matrix `group_key` carry the full tuple | behaviour change | read every key column; stop re-grouping by the first entry or issuing one slot per outer bucket | U54 |
| two groupers writing the same key column were accepted (the second was ignored) | refused `SERVICE_VALIDATION` (output-namespace collision) | behaviour change (refusal) | give each entry a distinct `field` or `label` | U54 |
| an extension grouper key containing `"\x00"` was harmless in a single grouper | refused `PROCESSING_GROUP` once a request has two or more groupers | behaviour change (refusal) | do not emit NUL in extension grouper keys | U54 |
| `OVERLAY_YOY` on a request with two or more `groups` | refused `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE` (`details.groupers`) on predict and runtime | behaviour change (refusal) | use one date grouper, or a Compose slot per outer bucket. **Lifted by U46 (#429, see "Changes from U46"):** YoY now runs once per combination of the non-date keys | U54 |
| `OVERLAY_DELTA_VS_PRIOR` / `_INDEX_VS_PRIOR` / `_INDEX_VS_ROLLING_MEAN` / `_ZSCORE_VS_ROLLING` / `_DELTA_VS_BASELINE` / `_INDEX_VS_BASELINE` / `_DELTA_VS_SIBLING` / `_INDEX_VS_SIBLING` on a request with two or more `groups` folded down the composite rows: a lag or window crossed outer buckets (the first row of a bucket got a delta against the previous bucket's last row), and a baseline or sibling resolved to one row of another bucket | refused `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE` (`details.groupers`) on predict and runtime; one grouper is byte-identical | behaviour change (refusal) | use one grouper, or a FILTER_INCLUDE / Compose slot per outer bucket. **Lifted by U46 (#320, see "Changes from U46"):** these kinds now run once per partition of the leading keys | U54 |
| predict `max_groups` / group estimate for several groupers was the first grouper's | the PRODUCT of the per-grouper estimates, an upper bound (runtime enforces on observed combinations); the matrix `bucket_basis` is `"product"` | behaviour change (estimate) | treat the estimate as a ceiling, not a row count | U54 |

## Changes from U46 (grouped analysis)

Complete for U46 (no release tag cut by the unit). Contract: `.claude/reference/execution-modes.md` (Series measure, Series partitions, Compose series value + key, Chain-stage series value + key, Grouped tier-1 tests, Multiplicity, Crosstab — Data-dependent axis groupers); skills `op-overlay-*`, `statistical-testing`, `multiplicity-correction`, `op-group-quantile`, `op-group-kmeans`, `crosstab-guide`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; an overlay-free Compose is byte-identical.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a Compose `OVERLAY_INDEX_VS_REF` / `_DELTA_VS_REF` / `_T_VS_REF` / `_Z_VS_REF` / `_PANEL_INDEX_VS_REF` over SERIES slots with two or more aggregations scored the alphabetically FIRST aggregation label and folded the other aggregations' values into the row key, so it failed `PULSE_OVERLAY_KEY_SET_DIVERGENT` or scored the wrong measure | each row keys on the slot's group columns alone and scores the aggregation `params.field` names (output label), else the slot's FIRST-LISTED aggregation; single-aggregation slots are byte-identical | behaviour change | drop slot-per-measure and aggregation-reordering workarounds; name the measure with `params.field` | U46 |
| process-host series overlays scored `aggregations[0]` (an undocumented default) | all twelve process-host series overlay kinds accept `params.field` (an aggregation output label: explicit `label`, else `TYPE_field`); absent = `aggregations[0]`, unchanged; an unknown label is `PULSE_OVERLAY_FIELD_UNKNOWN` at predict and runtime | added | set `field` instead of reordering `aggregations` or splitting a slot per measure | U46 |
| (none) | `params.field` on those five kinds; a label a slot the overlay reads lacks is `PULSE_OVERLAY_FIELD_UNKNOWN` with `details.slot`, on predict-compose and runtime | added | set `field` to a label every read slot carries | U46 |
| a whole-chain `OVERLAY_INDEX_VS_STAGE` / `_DELTA_VS_STAGE` over SERIES stages with two or more aggregations scored the alphabetically FIRST aggregation label and folded the other aggregations' values into the row key, so rows missed the reference (`PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_ZERO`) or the wrong measure was scored | each row keys on the stage's group columns alone and scores the aggregation `params.field` names, else the stage's FIRST-LISTED aggregation; a label the target or reference stage lacks is `PULSE_OVERLAY_FIELD_UNKNOWN` with `details.{which, stage_index}` on predict-chain and runtime; single-aggregation stages are byte-identical | behaviour change | name the measure with `params.field`; drop aggregation-reordering workarounds | U46 |
| predict-compose admitted two SERIES slots whose group columns differ by name (same grouper types); the runtime refused them `PULSE_OVERLAY_KEY_SET_DIVERGENT` | predict-compose refuses them too, `PULSE_OVERLAY_KEY_SET_DIVERGENT` with `details.{reference_fields, target_fields}` | behaviour change (predict refusal) | group every compared slot on the same key columns | U46 |
| `OVERLAY_DELTA_VS_PRIOR` / `_INDEX_VS_PRIOR` / `_INDEX_VS_ROLLING_MEAN` / `_ZSCORE_VS_ROLLING` / `_DELTA_VS_BASELINE` / `_INDEX_VS_BASELINE` / `_DELTA_VS_SIBLING` / `_INDEX_VS_SIBLING` on a request with two or more `groups` were refused `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE` (`details.groupers`; U54 interim) | accepted: each runs once per PARTITION — the rows sharing every key but the last, so the series runs down the last grouper — and a lag, window, baseline or sibling never crosses a segment; partitions match by key equality, so a `sort` that interleaves them gives the same figures; the layer summary merges the partitions (min / max widen, count sums) and each warning code fires once per layer with `details.partitions`; one grouper is byte-identical | behaviour change (refusal lifted) | drop FILTER_INCLUDE / Compose-slot-per-segment workarounds; put the segment keys first in `groups` and the series key last | U46 |
| (none) | `params.partition_by` on those eight kinds (and the three `_TOTAL` kinds, below): a LEADING PREFIX of the group key columns (`[region]` over `[region, store, day]` runs the series down `(store, day)` within each region); `[]` = one walk over every composite row (the unpartitioned order); a non-string-list value, an unknown column or a column out of prefix order is the new `PULSE_OVERLAY_PARTITION_INVALID`, Details `{index, kind, partition_by, groups, reason, field}`, identical on predict and runtime | added | omit for the default; name a shorter prefix to run the series across inner keys | U46 |
| `OVERLAY_INDEX_VS_TOTAL` / `_SHARE_OF_TOTAL` / `_ZSCORE_VS_TOTAL` (SERIES host) on a request with two or more `groups` scored every composite row against the GRAND total (grand mean and SD for the z-score) | each row is scored against its PARTITION's total — the rows sharing every key but the last by default, so `[region, product]` gives each product's share within its region (shares sum to 1 per region); `params.partition_by` takes a leading prefix as on the eight ordered kinds, `[]` restores the grand total byte for byte; a zero total in a partition raises one `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_ZERO` per layer with `details.partitions`; one grouper and the MATRIX host are unchanged | behaviour change | pass `partition_by: []` to keep grand-total figures; drop FILTER_INCLUDE / Compose-slot-per-segment workarounds for within-segment shares | U46 |
| (none) | predict advisory `PULSE_ADVISORY_SERIES_AXIS_NOT_DATE` on an ordered series overlay (prior, rolling, baseline, sibling) over two or more groupers with no `partition_by` whose last grouper is not date-family; `details {slot, operator, series_axis, grouper, groups, date_axis}`; never escalated by Strict, silenced by an explicit `partition_by` or `Options.SuppressAdvisories` | added | put the date grouper last, or state the order with `partition_by` | U46 |
| `OVERLAY_YOY` on a request with two or more `groups` was refused `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE` (`details.groupers`; U54 interim) | accepted when exactly one grouper is `GROUP_DATE`, wherever it sits: YoY runs once per combination of every OTHER key, so the prior-year lookup stays inside its segment; each entry keeps its full composite key; `params.partition_by`, if given, must name exactly the non-date key columns (any order), else `PULSE_OVERLAY_PARTITION_INVALID` with `reason: "not_non_date_keys"`; zero or two or more `GROUP_DATE` groupers are refused `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE` with `details.{groupers, date_groupers}`; the `frequency` fallback reads the `GROUP_DATE` grouper's `params`, not `groups[0]`'s; one grouper is byte-identical | behaviour change (refusal lifted) | drop FILTER_INCLUDE / Compose-slot-per-segment workarounds; move `frequency` onto the date grouper or the overlay | U46 |
| a vector-input grouper with no `field` (e.g. `GROUP_KMEANS` over `fields`) gave the process-host series overlays an empty key element on every row | the host keys each row on the grouper's Data column `<TYPE>_<first field>` (`vectors.GroupKeyField`), so sibling lookups and partitions see the real bucket | fix | none | U46 |
| `tests[]` on a request with `groups` ignored the groups: each test ran ONCE over the whole filtered record set, and `Response.Tests` held one result per test | each test runs once per COMPOSITE group key (the grouped `data` rows): `Response.Tests` holds tests x groups results, TEST-MAJOR, groups in the final `data` row order, each carrying the new additive `group_key` (`TestResult.GroupKey`, the `MatrixResult.GroupKey` shape); every group's result equals the same test filtered to that group; a group whose data the test refuses (`PULSE_TEST_SPLIT_GROUPS_LT_2`, `_INSUFFICIENT_N`, `_VARIANCE_ZERO`, `_CONTINGENCY_DEGENERATE`, `_CORRELATION_UNDEFINED`) gets `null` `statistic` / `p_value` plus a warning under that code with `details.group_key`, and the other groups proceed; a `request` multiplicity family pools every (test x group) p; ungrouped requests are byte-identical | behaviour change | to keep one whole-set test, run it in its own ungrouped request (or Compose slot); read `group_key` to pair a result with its group; map flat entry k to `Request.Tests[k / groups]` | U46 |
| a grouped request whose test splits on a grouper field (`split_by`, or a `TEST_CHISQ` / `TEST_FISHER_EXACT` `rows` / `cols`) ran the whole-set test | refused at predict and runtime with identical Details `{index, type, slot, field, groups, reason: "split_is_grouper"}`: `PULSE_TEST_SPLIT_GROUPS_LT_2` for `split_by`, `PULSE_TEST_CONTINGENCY_DEGENERATE` for `rows` / `cols` (each group holds one level) | behaviour change (refusal) | drop the field from `groups`, or run the across-groups test (e.g. ANOVA by region before a Tukey HSD post-test) as its own ungrouped request | U46 |
| predict counted one p-value per tier-1 test and graded `MaxGroups` on the bucket bound alone | a grouped request's tests count tests x `EstimateGroups` p-values (dictionary basis; a data-dependent grouper counts one per test, lower bound), so `PULSE_ADVISORY_MANY_TESTS` fires on the grouped total; `MaxGroups` grades tests x groups on predict and the runtime refuses it `PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`; the streamable reason reads "grouped tier-1 tests run once per group via the buffered path"; `group_key` joins the `standard` / `minimal` `return` presets for `tests[*]` / `post_tests[*]` | behaviour change | raise `Limits.MaxGroups` or cut tests / groups when the product trips it | U46 |
| `post_tests[]` on a request with two or more `groups` ran ONCE over every composite row, so a trend or a correlation mixed every segment | each post-test runs once per PARTITION of the result rows — the rows sharing every key but the last (a `TEST_TREND` over `[warehouse, week]` runs down the weeks of each warehouse); `Response.PostTests` is flat post-tests x partitions, test-major, partitions in first-appearance `data` order, each result's `group_key` the partition key; a too-sparse partition gets `null` statistic / p plus a warning under its code naming `group_key`; one grouper (and a crosstab host) is byte-identical | behaviour change | read `post_tests[k]` with its `group_key`; pass `params.partition_by: []` on every post-test to keep the one run over every row (an ANOVA ACROSS the leading key needs it) | U46 |
| (none) | `params.partition_by` on every post-test: a leading prefix of the group key columns, `[]` = no partition; stripped before the test's own params decode, listed on every tier-2 manifest entry. An off-prefix value or post-tests at different depths are the new `PULSE_TEST_PARTITION_INVALID` (reasons `not_string_list` / `unknown_field` / `not_prefix` / `mixed_depth`), and a `split_by` / `rows` / `cols` naming a partition key is `PULSE_TEST_SPLIT_GROUPS_LT_2` / `_CONTINGENCY_DEGENERATE` with `reason: split_is_partition_key` — identical Details on predict and runtime | added | give every post-test the same `partition_by`; split post-tests that partition differently into separate requests (a Compose slot each) | U46 |
| predict counted one p-value per post-test | a partitioned post-test counts `EstimateGroups` over its partition keys (dictionary basis, else one as a lower bound), beside tests x groups, so `PULSE_ADVISORY_MANY_TESTS` reads the grouped total | behaviour change (predict) | none | U46 |
| `multiplicity {method, family, alpha, m}` gave no count of the members an undefined p left out of `m` | the echo gains additive `excluded` (integer, `omitempty`): the family's NaN-p members (a sparse group or partition), on test and overlay echoes alike, summed over `row` / `column` families like `m`; absent when 0, so a fully defined family is byte-identical. One `request` family pools every (test x group) and (post-test x partition) p-value | added | read `m + excluded` as the family's member count | U46 |
| a crosstab with a `GROUP_QUANTILE` axis at a nested position, or on the column axis, refitted the quantile cut points per outer bucket (per row bucket for a column-axis quantile), so `[region, quartile]` gave per-region quartiles there but whole-set quartiles in plain `groups`; a `GROUP_KMEANS` axis always fitted once | `params.fit` on `GROUP_QUANTILE` / `GROUP_KMEANS`: `"global"` (default) fits once over the filtered set whatever the position, so crosstab bands equal plain `groups` and Compose bands; `"within"` refits inside each bucket of the groupers before it (crosstab: rows, then columns) on plain `groups`, crosstab and Compose alike, and is a no-op on the outermost position (`groups[0]`, crosstab `rows[0]`); under `"within"` each per-bucket refit's warnings (k-means non-convergence) reach `Response.Warnings` once per outer bucket with `details.group_key` naming it, beside the whole-set fit's unstamped warning, on every path; any other value is refused `PROCESSING_CONFIG` (`details {type, param: "fit", value, valid, reason: "bad_fit"}`) identically on predict and runtime; plain `groups` without `fit` and the fused-crosstab dispatch are unchanged | behaviour change | set `"fit": "within"` on a nested or column-axis quantile crosstab to restore per-bucket cut points; use it on a nested `groups` quantile / k-means to get per-segment bands in one request | U46 |
| a Compose overlay pairing a grouped (SERIES) slot with an ungrouped one, or a crosstab slot with a grouped one, was refused `PULSE_OVERLAY_KEY_SET_DIVERGENT` at runtime (the ungrouped slot's one aggregate row read as a key set; matrix keys were diffed against series keys) but `PULSE_OVERLAY_SLOT_SHAPE_DIVERGENT` by predict-compose; a whole-chain `OVERLAY_INDEX_VS_STAGE` / `_DELTA_VS_STAGE` pairing a grouped stage with an ungrouped one was `PULSE_OVERLAY_CHAIN_STAGE_SHAPE_DIVERGENT` on predict-chain but folded at runtime, every row warning `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_ZERO` (index) or `PULSE_OVERLAY_REFERENCE_UNKNOWN` (delta) | the runtime judges a slot's / stage's host shape off its authored request, as predict does (crosstab = matrix, groups + aggregations = series, else scalar): Compose refuses the pair `PULSE_OVERLAY_SLOT_SHAPE_DIVERGENT` with predict's Details `{index, kind, reference, target_label, reference_shape, target_shape}` (`kind` is new on the runtime Details of every shape refusal), and a MATRIX-requiring kind refuses the series or scalar reference `PULSE_OVERLAY_SLOT_NOT_CROSSTAB` first, `observed_shape` now `scalar` for an ungrouped slot; the chain layer is the empty payload plus ONE `PULSE_OVERLAY_CHAIN_STAGE_SHAPE_DIVERGENT` warning naming both shapes; two ungrouped slots or stages are unchanged | behaviour change (runtime code) | branch on `PULSE_OVERLAY_SLOT_SHAPE_DIVERGENT` / `_CHAIN_STAGE_SHAPE_DIVERGENT` for a grouped-vs-ungrouped pair; group both slots or stages, or neither | U46 |
| a chain whose stage 0 joins resolved its smart defaults and ran its chain gate against the bare LEFT cohort, so an untyped aggregation over a right-side field stayed untyped and was refused `PULSE_CHAIN_NOT_MERGEABLE` ("aggregator  is not mergeable") on predict-chain and runtime; predict-compose and predict-chain skipped the `params.field` check on any slot or stage with an untyped, unlabelled aggregation | a joined stage 0 defaults and gates against the JOINED schema (right side read header only), so it runs and its `TYPE_field` label reaches the next stage and the whole-chain overlays exactly as the typed twin; predict-compose and predict-chain resolve each slot's / stage's defaults against its (joined) schema and judge the label, refusing an unknown one `PULSE_OVERLAY_FIELD_UNKNOWN` with the runtime's Details; unjoined stages and typed or labelled aggregations are unchanged | fix | drop explicit `type`s added only to get a joined stage 0 past the gate | U46 |
| an overlay on a Compose slot, a whole-chain stage or a grouped Process request that sat beside ANY array-valued (vector) aggregation was refused `PROCESSING_CONFIG` (`reason: "array_valued"`) even when it scored a scalar aggregation (a grouped Process request: whenever `aggregations[0]` was array-valued, even when every series overlay named a scalar via `params.field`); and a grouped Process overlay whose `params.field` named a non-first array-valued aggregation was NOT refused | each overlay is judged on the aggregation it scores only — a series kind's `params.field`, else `aggregations[0]` (every aggregation on a crosstab slot / stage; every non-series Process kind) — on predict and runtime alike (one shared walk); a Compose slot / chain stage is narrowed to its group columns plus that value column, so `[AGG_SUM x, AGG_VEC_MEAN ...]` scoring `x` runs and equals the single-aggregation slot byte for byte; scoring the vector aggregation is refused with the same code, message and Details as before (grouped Process: `details.slot` `aggregations[<i>]`); an unknown `field` beside a vector aggregation is `PULSE_OVERLAY_FIELD_UNKNOWN`, not `array_valued` | behaviour change (refusal moved to the scored aggregation) | drop slot-per-measure splits made only to keep a vector aggregation out of an overlaid slot; a request that scored a non-first vector aggregation via `params.field` must name a scalar instead | U46 |
| predict-chain refused every whole-chain overlay stage shape mismatch (matrix vs series, series vs scalar, ...) with a `PULSE_OVERLAY_CHAIN_STAGE_SHAPE_DIVERGENT` envelope ERROR (`Valid: false`), while the runtime ran the chain and answered an empty layer plus one warning whose Details were `{kind, target_index, ref_index, target_shape, ref_shape, overlay_index}` | predict-chain reports it as an envelope WARNING and stays `Valid` for an otherwise-valid chain, matching the runtime; both carry the same Details — the runtime warning gains `index` (the overlay's position), `stage_index` and `stage_name` (the target stage) and predict gains `overlay_index`, so every key deep-equals; `OverlaysSchemaDivergence` still lists the pair; `Strict` escalates it on neither side | behaviour change (refusal -> warning) | stop treating the code as a predict refusal: read it from `warnings` (predict) or `OverlayLayer.Warnings` (runtime), read `stage_name` to locate the stage, and keep compared stages shape-identical | U46 |
| an overlay on any host (grouped Process, Compose, whole-chain) whose scored aggregation was non-scalar — `AGG_WELFORD` (moment struct), `AGG_SET_UNION` / `AGG_SET_INTERSECTION` (label list), `AGG_SET_FREQUENCY` (count map) — returned an EMPTY layer (`entries: []`, `count: 0`) with no error and no warning, on predict and runtime; and a Compose or whole-chain series overlay whose scored aggregation (the default `aggregations[0]`) was simply not numeric silently scored the alphabetically first numeric column of the slot or stage instead | the scored aggregation is the one named (`params.field`, else the first-listed) and a NON-SCALAR one is refused `PROCESSING_CONFIG` with the array-valued refusal's Details shape and `reason: "non_scalar"`, on predict and runtime alike; an unscored one beside a scalar still runs; `OVERLAY_T_VS_REF` / `OVERLAY_Z_VS_REF` still read the Welford triple (next row); on a whole chain a non-mergeable one (`AGG_WELFORD`) stays `PULSE_CHAIN_NOT_MERGEABLE` | behaviour change (silent empty layer / wrong column -> refusal) | score a numeric aggregation (`params.field`, or list it first) | U46 |
| a Compose `OVERLAY_T_VS_REF` / `OVERLAY_Z_VS_REF` over `AGG_WELFORD` slots returned an empty layer — the series arm reads a `{mean, variance, n}` map and never the in-process `WelfordTriple` struct; and over a grouped slot built with components disabled (`Options.DisableComponents`, or the slot's `disable_components`) it returned an EMPTY layer with no error | each narrowed slot reads every row's leg from that row's bucket components (`mean` / `variance` / `m2` / `n`, plus `sum_weights` / `n_eff` when weighted — the matrix arm's weighted-host rule); with components disabled it is refused `PULSE_OVERLAY_COMPONENTS_REQUIRED`, Details `{overlay_index, kind, slot, host, aggregation, label, reason: "components_disabled"}`, on predict and runtime alike; a `return` excluding `components` still runs (the Compose veto keeps them); components on is byte-identical | behaviour change (bug fix; silent empty layer -> refusal) | enable components on that slot (an explicit `disable_components: false` re-opens an engine-off gate), or score a scalar mean with the `params` triple | U46 |
| a Compose `OVERLAY_T_VS_REF` / `_Z_VS_REF` over a scalar scored aggregation, or `OVERLAY_T_CELL` / `_Z_CELL` over a scalar crosstab cell (any cell without mean + variance components, or any cell of a slot with components disabled), computed every p-value from variance 1 and n 2 per side when `params` carried no `variance_*` / `sample_size_*` (a non-positive or non-numeric one fell back the same way); a vs-ref kind over crosstab slots answered an EMPTY layer; predict-compose accepted all three and `OVERLAY_Z_CELL` over grouped slots | a scalar side REQUIRES its variance and sample size — `variance_target` / `variance_ref` and `sample_size_target` / `sample_size_ref`, or the symmetric `variance` / `sample_size`, each a finite number > 0 (a per-side key that is present but unusable does not fall through to the symmetric one) — else `PULSE_OVERLAY_PARAM_MISSING` with Details `{overlay_index, kind, slot, host, aggregation, label, side, params, reason: "params_missing"}`; a vs-ref kind over a crosstab slot is `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE` `{overlay_index, kind, slot, shape: "matrix", shapes: ["series"]}`; `OVERLAY_Z_CELL` over grouped slots is `PULSE_OVERLAY_SLOT_NOT_CROSSTAB` on predict too — one rule (`descriptor.ComposeOverlayTwoSampleInputsRefusal`), identical code, message and Details on predict-compose and runtime. Explicit params compute exactly as before; components routes (`AGG_WELFORD` rows, `AGG_WELFORD` / `AGG_VARIANCE` / `AGG_STDDEV` cells with components on) read no params and are byte-identical | behaviour change (refusal) | pass the variance and sample size you meant (the old figures were `{"variance": 1, "sample_size": 2}`), or score `AGG_WELFORD` with components on so each group / cell carries its own; compare crosstab slots with the `_CELL` twin | U46 |
| a ProcessChain stage carrying a set fold (`AGG_SET_UNION` / `AGG_SET_INTERSECTION` / `AGG_SET_FREQUENCY`) that fed a later stage handed it a column the next stage read as null — a sum over it came back 0 | refused `PROCESSING_CONFIG` `reason: "non_scalar"` (Details `{stage_index, stage_name, aggregation, label, reason}`, the array-valued rule's), predict and runtime alike | behaviour change (silent wrong figure → refusal) | make that stage the last, or fold a scalar (`AGG_SET_CARDINALITY_SUM`, `AGG_SET_DISTINCT_VALUES`) | U46 |
| `PULSE_OVERLAY_KEY_SET_DIVERGENT` `missing` / `extra` keys could carry a rich-payload column as `<label>=\u0001unknown` | keys carry group-key-shaped columns only | behaviour change (Details) | match keys on the group columns alone | U46 |
| an overlay kind authored on a slot that does not run it: a Compose kind (`OVERLAY_T_CELL` / `_Z_CELL` / `_T_VS_REF` / `_Z_VS_REF`) on a crosstab or grouped Process request passed predict (its `params` were even checked) and failed at runtime `PULSE_OVERLAY_KIND_UNKNOWN`; the other Compose, whole-chain and FACET kinds on `Request.Overlays` were silent on predict and the same runtime failure; a Process, whole-chain or FACET kind on `ComposedRequest.Overlays` answered an EMPTY stub layer; a non-chain catalog kind on `ChainRequest.Overlays` was `PULSE_OVERLAY_KIND_UNKNOWN`; a non-FACET kind on `FacetRequest.Overlays` was this code on predict but a population or `PULSE_OVERLAY_KIND_UNKNOWN` error at runtime | refused on every slot, predict and runtime alike, with `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE`, Details `{index, kind, host, hosts}` (`host` the slot sent on — `request` / `compose` / `chain` / `facet` — `hosts` every slot that runs the kind) and a message naming both slots; the earlier FACET-on-`Request.Overlays` refusal gains `hosts`; a kind outside the catalog keeps `PULSE_OVERLAY_KIND_UNKNOWN` | behaviour change (refusal) | move the spec to the slot `details.hosts` names (the t / z cell and vs-ref tests compare two Compose slots: put them on `ComposedRequest.Overlays`); branch on `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE` + `details.hosts`, not `PULSE_OVERLAY_KIND_UNKNOWN` | U46 |
| a Process overlay kind over the other Process host shape — a MATRIX-only kind (`OVERLAY_SHARE_OF_COL`, the χ² / Fisher / pairwise / margin kinds, `OVERLAY_FORMULA`) on a grouped request, or a SERIES-only kind (`OVERLAY_YOY`, the prior / rolling / baseline / sibling / `_TOTAL` kinds) on a crosstab — was refused by each kind's own predict check with assorted Details (`OVERLAY_FORMULA` on a grouped request passed predict) and failed at runtime `PULSE_OVERLAY_KIND_UNKNOWN` | refused on predict and runtime alike, before any per-kind rule, with `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE`, Details `{index, kind, host: "request", shape, shapes}` (`shape` the host the request makes, `shapes` every Process shape the kind runs on); an ungrouped request is not judged | behaviour change (refusal) | branch on `details.shapes`: pick a kind for the host shape, or reshape the request; a SERIES or SCALAR `OVERLAY_FORMULA` goes on `ComposedRequest.Overlays` | U46 |
| a Compose `OVERLAY_FORMULA` (`ComposedRequest.Overlays`) passed predict-compose without an identifier check and answered an EMPTY layer | it computes over the first target slot in the reference slot's shape — MATRIX `cell` / margins / SDs + `ref_cell`, SERIES `value` / `total` / `prior` + `ref_value`, SCALAR `value` + `ref`, and `slot.<label>.cell|value` for the reference and each target, aligned by key (no figure ⇒ NaN ⇒ `null`); an identifier, function or slot path outside that namespace, or no `params.formula`, is refused identically on predict and runtime (`PULSE_OVERLAY_FORMULA_INVALID_IDENT` / `_PARSE_ERROR`, Details carrying `index`); `OVERLAY_FORMULA` now depends on `capability:crosstab` OR `capability:compose` in a feature profile | behaviour change (bug fix) | read the layer's payload; fix a formula naming an identifier outside the host's namespace | U46 |
| an overlay on any host (grouped Process, Compose, whole-chain) whose scored aggregation was `AGG_MODE` over a `categorical_*` field (its value the modal DICTIONARY INDEX) or a `packed_bool` field (its 0/1 truth value) scored the code as a magnitude — an index / delta / share of codes, plus a spurious `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_ZERO` wherever the modal code was 0, on predict and runtime | refused `PROCESSING_CONFIG` with the scored-value Details shape, `reason: "categorical_code"`, plus the input `field` and its `field_type`, on predict (request + schema, no records) and runtime alike; judged on the schema the slot / stage executes over — on a chain stage >= 1 the synthesised one, so `AGG_MODE` over a forwarded group key (`categorical_u32`) is refused, and over a forwarded aggregation column (`f64`) it scores unless a code-emitting aggregation wrote that column (code-ness propagates through every stage, for the whole-chain overlays and a stage's own; `field_type` names the origin type); a crosstab (`Request.Overlays`, MATRIX) whose CELL is such a code is refused the same way (`slot: "crosstab.cell"`) under every kind that reads cell values — the share / index / delta / z-score vs-margin kinds, χ², Fisher, `OVERLAY_PAIRWISE_PROP_Z` / `_PROBIT_T`, `OVERLAY_FORMULA` — while the moment pairwise kinds (`_WELCH_T`, `_TWO_MEANS_Z`, `_WEIGHTED_TWO_MEANS_Z`) are not judged; `AGG_MODE` over a numeric field, the smart default `AGG_MODE_COUNT`, and an unscored code beside a scalar are unchanged | behaviour change (meaningless figure → refusal) | score a count or share (`AGG_FREQUENCY` of one label, `AGG_AVERAGE` of a `packed_bool`) or name a numeric aggregation with `params.field` | U46 |
| extension aggregators had no way to declare a non-scalar `Rich` value, or that their value is picked from the input column (a code over a categorical) | `AggregatorRegistration.NonScalar` (manifest `non_scalar`) and `AggregatorRegistration.EmitsInputValue` (manifest `emits_input_value`) — declared, the slot is refused as an overlay's scored aggregation like the built-ins (`AGG_WELFORD` / the set folds; `AGG_MODE`); undeclared, `NonScalar` folds as no value and an overlay scores the code as a magnitude | added | declare `NonScalar: true` on an aggregator whose `Rich` returns a label list, map or struct, and `EmitsInputValue: true` on a mode / first / last-style aggregator | U46 |
| the bundled example `25_tukey_hsd_after_anova` carried a tier-1 `TEST_ANOVA_F` beside `groups` | the example drops its tier-1 ANOVA (a grouped request now runs the test per group, and a test splitting on a grouper is refused); the ANOVA runs as its own ungrouped request, then the Tukey HSD post-test | behaviour change (example) | read the example again through `pulse_examples_get`; a stored copy of the old request must split the ANOVA out | U46 |

## Changes from U28 (matrix overlays)

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TEST_KENDALL_TAU` (row and `tau_b_post`) p used the normal approximation with a continuity correction, z = (S − sign(S))/√Var(S); `TEST_SPEARMAN_R` (row and `rank_pearson_post`) p always used the t approximation (p = 0 at \|ρ\| = 1) | both take R `cor.test`'s default p (`exact = NULL`, `continuity = FALSE`), the one helper `MAT_CORRELATION`'s rank cells call: Kendall exact when untied and n < 50, else normal with no continuity correction (`details.z` = S/√Var(S)); Spearman AS 89 when untied and n ≤ 1290, else t on n − 2. Statistic, `df`, `details.t` / `s` / `var_s` unchanged bit for bit; frequency weights as before (a weight above 1 is a tie) | behaviour change (p-values) | re-baseline stored Kendall / small untied Spearman p-values and anything adjusted from them (`p_adjusted`, `reject_null`); the test now equals a matrix rank cell on the same pair | U28 |
| `return` preset `standard` (the MCP default) dropped every `matrices[*].auxiliary` and the matrix `multiplicity` echo | `standard` keeps, per spec, the inference figures the spec asked for: `auxiliary.p` / `ci_lower` / `ci_upper` (`params.inference`), `p_adjusted` / `significant_adjusted` + `matrices[*].multiplicity` (a multiplicity family), and the pairwise `auxiliary.n` beside a kept `p` (a preset companion); a spec without inference is unchanged byte for byte, but the `standard` `returned.digest` is a new value on every response | behaviour change (shaped output) | nothing to do to receive the figures; to keep the old `standard` output exclude `matrices[*].auxiliary` and `matrices[*].multiplicity`; re-pin any stored `standard` digest | U28 |
| `descriptor.ReturnPlan` / `descriptor.ReturnPresetMeta` had no conditional selections | additive `Companions []string` (`companions`, `omitempty`), each `<path> (with <key>)` — `standard`: `matrices[*].auxiliary.n (with p)` | additive field | read it where you mirror a plan or a preset's paths | U28 |
| `linalg` had no general linear solver | `linalg.Solve(a, b)` (one right-hand side), `linalg.FactorLU(a)` returning `*linalg.LUFactor` (methods `N`, `ConditionNumber`, `Solve`, `SolveMatrix`, `Inverse`) for a reused factorization; a singular or rank-deficient system returns a typed error that surfaces as `PULSE_MATRIX_SINGULAR` | added | factor once and call `Solve` / `SolveMatrix` for several right-hand sides; branch on `PULSE_MATRIX_SINGULAR`, not on a message | U28 |
| `(*linalg.CoMoment)` exposed the pairwise count only (`PairN`) | `PairW2(i, j)` (pair sum of squared weights), `PairNEff(i, j)` (Kish effective n) and `PairNMass(i, j)` (the pair's integer positive-weight row count); each panics out of range like `PairN` | added | read these where you build a weighted pairwise inference of your own; `PairNEff` is the n a probability-weighted p must use | U28 |
| `types.OverlayPayload` carried one payload (scalar / series / matrix) | additive `Parts map[string]*OverlayPayload` (`parts`, `omitempty`): a multi-output kind's NAMED secondary payloads, never nested; a `Parts`-free layer is byte-identical. `descriptor.OverlayPart` and `OverlayCapability.Parts` declare each kind's parts in the manifest. Export: the Excel adapter writes the primary payload and drops the parts with the warning `PULSE_OVERLAY_EXPORT_PARTS_DROPPED` | additive field | read `payload.parts[name]` for the kinds below; discover part names from `manifest.overlays[*].parts` rather than hardcoding them | U28 |
| `types.OverlayRef` named a crosstab, series, scalar or other host; no matrix-result host existed | `OverlayRef.Matrix *types.OverlayMatrixRef{Name}` selects a `Request.Matrices` spec by its effective name (exactly one `Ref` arm is populated, as before); an unknown name is refused `PULSE_OVERLAY_TARGET_UNKNOWN` (`which: "matrix"`) on predict and runtime alike | added | author `ref: {matrix: {name}}` on a MATRIX_RESULT overlay | U28 |
| the overlay catalog stopped at the U46 kinds | new kinds, each a `types.OverlayKind` const: `OverlayKindStdResidual` (`OVERLAY_STD_RESIDUAL`, adjusted standardized residuals, `expected` part), `OverlayKindCorrespondence` (`OVERLAY_CORRESPONDENCE`, row / column coordinates), `OverlayKindMarkov` (`OVERLAY_MARKOV`, `step_<n>`, steady state, absorption), `OverlayKindRake` (`OVERLAY_RAKE`, iterative proportional fitting) and `OverlayKindMatrixGroupSummary` (`OVERLAY_MATRIX_GROUP_SUMMARY`, mean and range per cell across the buckets of a grouped matrix result); the first four run on a crosstab, the last on the MATRIX_RESULT host | added | an exhaustive switch over `types.AllOverlayKinds()` gains five arms | U28 |
| `types.MatrixSpec` had no correction block; the `MultiplicityFamily` enum stopped at `compose` | `MatrixSpec.Multiplicity *Multiplicity` (family default `matrix`, inherits `Request.Multiplicity` / `Options.DefaultMultiplicity`), `MatrixResult.Multiplicity *AppliedMultiplicity` (the echo), `types.MultiplicityFamilyMatrix` (the payload-schema `MultiplicityFamily` enum gains `matrix`); the adjusted figures ride `auxiliary.p_adjusted` / `auxiliary.significant_adjusted` beside the raw `auxiliary.p`, which never moves | added | opt in per spec; omitting the block is byte-identical | U28 |
| `MAT_CORRELATION` / `MAT_PARTIAL_CORRELATION` produced the statistic only | opt-in `params.inference` adds the auxiliary `p`, `ci_lower`, `ci_upper` and `n` matrices (Pearson via Fisher z, Spearman / Kendall via the `cor.test` helper, partial correlation with df = n − 2 − g); off is byte-identical | added | set `params.inference` before asking for `multiplicity`; a family needs a raw p to correct | U28 |
| the error code list stopped at the U46 overlay codes | new codes: `PULSE_OVERLAY_NONNEGATIVE_REQUIRED`, `PULSE_OVERLAY_MATRIX_NOT_SQUARE`, `PULSE_OVERLAY_MARKOV_NOT_ERGODIC` (warning), `PULSE_OVERLAY_RAKE_TARGET_MISMATCH`, `PULSE_OVERLAY_RAKE_NOT_CONVERGED` (warning), `PULSE_OVERLAY_GROUPS_REQUIRED`, `PULSE_OVERLAY_EXPORT_PARTS_DROPPED` (warning); `PULSE_OVERLAY_TARGET_UNKNOWN` gains `which: "matrix"` | added | an exhaustive switch over `errors.AllCodes()` gains the arms; look each up with `pulse errors lookup` | U28 |
| a crosstab or series overlay on a request that also carried `Request.Matrices` with a scalar shape was accepted | refused `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_INCOMPATIBLE_WITH_SHAPE`, Details `shapes: ["matrix_result"]` | behaviour change (refusal) | move the overlay to a request without a scalar-shaped `Request.Matrices`, or reference the matrix spec through `Ref.Matrix` | U28 |
| the bundled `survey-crosstab` feature profile did not enable the new residual, perceptual-map and raking overlays; the `empty` / `minimal` / `survey-crosstab` profiles listed the `flows` intent | `survey-crosstab` gains `OVERLAY_STD_RESIDUAL`, `OVERLAY_CORRESPONDENCE` and `OVERLAY_RAKE` (edited in place: `written_with` 1.0.0 is unreleased); the three profiles drop the `flows` intent because `OVERLAY_MARKOV` is not enabled in them | behaviour change (profile contents) | re-run `pulse features check` on a stored copy; add `OVERLAY_MARKOV` and the `flows` intent where you need them | U28 |
| a matrix cell's inference row floor (`INSUFFICIENT_N`) counted weight-0 rows | the floor counts positive-weight rows only; `details.n` is that count | behaviour change (bug fix) | expect `INSUFFICIENT_N` on a weighted matrix whose zero-weight rows used to pad the count | U28 |

## Changes from U29 (vector field types)

Complete for U29 (no release tag cut by the unit). The only file-format change in v1. Contract: `.claude/reference/byte-layout.md` (Vector fields), `.claude/reference/matrix-and-vectors.md` (Native vector fields); skill `vector-fields`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"` (no payload-reachable shape moved).

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Schema.Fields` types stopped at `set_u256` (byte 19); a field's width was `FieldType.ByteSize()` | `encoding.FieldTypeVecF32` (byte 20) and `FieldTypeVecF64` (21): a fixed-dim vector (dim 2..1024) per record. The dim is NOT in the type byte, so a vector's width is `encoding.Schema.FieldWidth(i)` (`ByteSize()` is 0 for both). New schema nouns: `Schema.Vectors []VectorField{FieldIndex, Dim, Kind, ScaleMin, ScaleMax, HasScale, Labels}`, `Schema.HasVectors` / `Vector` / `VectorDim` / `ValidateVectors`, `VectorKind` (`measure`, `scale`, `composition`, `binary`), `MinVectorDim` / `MaxVectorDim`, and the value accessors `encoding.ReadVector` / `WriteVector` (streams) and `PutVector` / `VectorFromBytes` (byte slices), which take the dim explicitly. The format byte becomes `0x02` for any cohort with a vector (a vector-free cohort is byte-identical, `0x01` stays readable); a binary predating vectors refuses a vector cohort loudly (unknown type byte, or unknown REQUIRED section 2) | added (file format) | size every stride with `Schema.FieldWidth`, never a type-only `ByteSize()` (`TestNoTypeOnlyWidthInStridePaths` guards the library's own paths); keep `Schema.Vectors` through any schema copy you make (`Logical()` keeps it) or every vector field sizes at 0 bytes; the `uint64` `ReadFieldValue` / `WriteFieldValue` refuse a vector with `ENCODING_TYPE_MISMATCH`, so branch on `FieldType.IsVector()` and read it with `ReadVector` | U29 |
| `Cohort.Reader` cells and `Pulse.NewCohortBuilder` values covered scalars, strings, masks and decimals | a vector cell is `[]float32` (`vec_f32`) or `[]float64` (`vec_f64`), nil when the vector is null; the builder takes the same slice, of exactly dim elements. Sample and lookup rows carry the vector as a `float64` element array (JSON array, `null` for a non-finite element) | added | type-switch on the two slice types in a reader loop; a cell that was an unknown type before is now one of them | U29 |
| `extend.Record` had no vector accessor | `extend.Record` gains `VectorValue(field string) ([]float64, bool)` (a fresh `float64` copy, NaN elements verbatim; false for a null vector or a non-vector field). `NumericValue` stays false for a vector field but resolves a native element member `"emb[k]"` (1-based; a NaN element or a null vector is false), the name a vector-input slot hands an extension factory for a native vector | interface break (implementers) + added | an embedder who IMPLEMENTS `extend.Record` (a test double, an adapter) must add `VectorValue`; an extension that only reads a `Record` is unaffected. A vector-input extension should read `NumericValue` per member name and need not special-case a native vector | U29 |
| a request named a vector only through `Request.Vectors` (a spec over scalar columns) | a `vec_f32` / `vec_f64` field is a vector with no spec: its name works in every `vector` slot (matrix, vector tests, vector attributes, `GROUP_KMEANS`, `AGG_VEC_*`, expressions). A `fields` literal, glob or `pattern` naming it expands to element members `emb[k]` (1-based); default element labels are the schema's, else `emb_<k>`. A spec named like a native field is `PULSE_VECTOR_NAME_COLLISION` (`details.native: true`). Member identity differs from a scalar twin: `row_keys`, `details` `members` and warning text say `emb[k]` where the twin says its column name; values, counts and labels are equal (a NaN element is a null member, like a null column) | added | map `emb[k]` back to your element names when you compare a native run with a scalar-twin run | U29 |
| `AGG_COUNT`, `AGG_NULL_COUNT`, `FILTER_NULL`, the `{n, n_null}` floor and a crosstab count cell on a vector field | counted every native vector as NULL (the presence check read no vector storage); they now read vector presence: a null vector is null, any other (NaN elements included) is present | behaviour change (bug fix) | expect non-zero counts on a vector field where an unreleased build reported all-null | U29 |
| a vector field in a scalar slot | refused `PULSE_VECTOR_FIELD_NOT_SCALAR` on predict and runtime alike (`details {field, field_type, dim, slot, suggest?}`): a scalar aggregation, grouper, attribute, feature, filter (other than `FILTER_NULL` / `FILTER_EXPRESSION`), test or regression input, window or sort key, weight, date / zone slot and point-lookup key; a join key is `PULSE_JOIN_TYPE_MISMATCH` (`details.reason: vector_key`) | behaviour change (refusal) | name the field under `vector`; `details.suggest` names the vector operator for a bare aggregation or grouper slot | U29 |
