| a `.sav` value-label binding (`--labels`, `ExportJob.Labels`, `io.SPSSWriterOptions.ValueLabels`) on a `u4` / `u8` / `u16` / `u32` field was refused `PULSE_LABEL_FIELD_NOT_CATEGORICAL` (#470) | accepted: the field stays numeric, its stored integers are the codes and the table's keys (their plain decimal spellings) its value labels. A key that is not a value the type stores is the new `PULSE_LABEL_KEY_INVALID` (`details.key`, `details.max`); a binding with no listable entries (a `Lookup`-only table without `Order`, or `SPSSValueLabels.Labels` nil) is `PULSE_LABEL_TABLE_NOT_ENUMERABLE`, now owned `shared` in the manifest. `u64`, floats and dates keep `PULSE_LABEL_FIELD_NOT_CATEGORICAL`; categorical bindings and every non-`.sav` label surface are unchanged | API addition + behaviour change (refusal lifted, new code) | drop any upstream "derive a categorical first" step for a `.sav` export; branch on `PULSE_LABEL_KEY_INVALID` | U50 (E3-S2) |
| a ROW export (`csv`, `tsv`, `ndjson`, `jsonarray`, `excel`, `arrow`, `parquet`; `--labels`, `ExportJob.Labels`) checked no label binding's field type: a binding on a float, `u64`, `decimal128`, `date`, `datetime`, `packed_bool`, `set_*` or `vec_*` field was applied to the cell's rendered TEXT (a hit only when a key happened to spell it, every other row a silent miss), and a `u4`–`u32` binding to a table keyed by text missed every row with only a `PULSE_LABEL_LOOKUP_MISS` warning | the `.sav` rule on every row format: a `categorical_*` field binds as before (output byte-identical), a `u4` / `u8` / `u16` / `u32` field binds with its table keyed by the stored integers' plain decimal spellings (`replace` writes the label text — `arrow` / `parquet` / `excel` type that column as a string — `augment` fills `<field>_label`; an unmatched code keeps its number and leaves the sibling empty, as an unmatched categorical value does), a key that is no such spelling is `PULSE_LABEL_KEY_INVALID`, and any other type is `PULSE_LABEL_FIELD_NOT_CATEGORICAL` — raised before anything is written, and by `Pulse.PredictExport` / `pulse export predict --labels` with any non-`.sav` target or none. A `Lookup`-only table still binds a `u4`–`u32` field on a row export (looked up per cell, keys unchecked). `ConvertJob.Labels` is unchanged | behaviour change (new refusals) | bind labels only to `categorical_*` or `u4`–`u32` fields (derive a categorical at import for floats, `u64` or dates); key an integer field's table `"1"`, `"2"`, …; branch on the two codes | U50 (E5-S3) |
| a ROW export (`--labels`, `ExportJob.Labels`) ignored a binding on a field the cohort lacks (it labelled nothing) and let the last of two bindings on one field win; `ConvertJob.Labels` (`Pulse.Convert`) checked no binding at all — a binding on a float, `u64`, date or any other non-categorical field rewrote the cell text, an unknown field labelled nothing, a duplicate let the last win | a row export refuses an unknown field `PULSE_LABEL_FIELD_UNKNOWN` and a second binding on one field `PULSE_LABEL_DUPLICATE_BINDING` — the codes the `.sav` writer raises — from `Export` and `PredictExport` alike, with nothing written. A convert checks its bindings by the same rules and codes (those two, `PULSE_LABEL_FIELD_NOT_CATEGORICAL`, `PULSE_LABEL_KEY_INVALID`) against the schema it resolves, on every target, from `Convert` and `ConvertJob.Predict` alike, before writing; an INFERRED integer field's keys are bounded by `u32`, since the row pass may promote it past its sampled width. A job's `Labels` are checked even without a `LabelResolver` (the key check needs one). Valid categorical / `u4`–`u32` bindings are byte-identical | behaviour change (new refusals) | bind each field once, to a field the cohort (or the convert's source schema) has; on a convert, bind only `categorical_*` or `u4`–`u32` fields; branch on the codes | U50 (E6-S2) |
| `GROUP_DATE` `day_of_week` keys emitted alphabetically (`Friday`, `Monday`, `Saturday`, …) and `GROUP_DATE_RANGES` labels alphabetically in grouped rows and on crosstab axes (#349, #350); both ignored `Group.Include` | `day_of_week` emits Monday → Sunday and `GROUP_DATE_RANGES` its supplied range order, unmatched last, in grouped `Data` rows, the grouper's `buckets` component, both crosstab arms and a `groups` composite — so a series overlay over a `day_of_week` host now runs Monday → Sunday. Key strings are unchanged. Both groupers now honour `Group.Include` (restrict + order, like `GROUP_CATEGORY`; a value no key carries is dropped, never refused). New `GROUP_DATE` component `hour_of_day` keys the local wall-clock hour `00`..`23` in numeric order (datetime only). Every other component's order is unchanged | behaviour change (row / axis order) + API addition | a caller that re-sorted `day_of_week` rows itself can drop that step; one that relied on alphabetical order sets an explicit `sort` | U50 (E2-S1) |
| `FEAT_DATE_FEATURES` `<prefix>_dow` numbered Sunday = 0 .. Saturday = 6 (#349) | ISO 8601: Monday = 1 .. Sunday = 7, on `date` and `datetime` alike (a `datetime` reads the local day of its resolved or per-row zone). Saturday stays `6`; only Sunday (`0` → `7`) and the range move, so a filter or group on `dow` = `0` now matches nothing. New `ATTR_DATE_PART` part `iso_weekday` emits the same numbering. Every weekday number Pulse emits is now ISO (the formula `weekday()` already was) | behaviour change (values) + API addition | rewrite `dow` `0` as `7`; a mean of `dow` shifts accordingly; an `(dow + 6) % 7` remap upstream can be dropped | U50 (E2-S2) |
| `OVERLAY_ZSCORE_VS_ROLLING` answered from the third present point — a sample SD over just two priors, however wide the window (a `window: 28` z-score of −36 on day 3) — while `OVERLAY_INDEX_VS_ROLLING_MEAN` waited for a full window (#351) | new shared `params.min_periods`: the present prior points a row needs before it gets a figure (absent points never count). Default = `window` on both kinds, so the z-score is now `null` until a full window of present points, matching the index (whose default output is unchanged). An explicit value must be an integer from 2 (z-score) or 1 (index) up to `window`; otherwise `PULSE_OVERLAY_LEVEL_OUT_OF_RANGE` (`details.param` `min_periods`, `min`, `max`) from predict and runtime alike | behaviour change (values: early rows now `null`) + API addition | set `"min_periods": 2` to keep the old z-score warm-up; set it on the index to answer before the window fills (the mean is then over the present priors held) | U50 (E2-S3) |
| `TEST_TREND` re-sorted rows by `order_by` byte-wise, so an `order_by` naming a grouper key column trended alphabetically: `day_of_week` as Friday, Monday, Saturday, …; `GROUP_DATE_RANGES` labels alphabetically; numeric bucket keys (`GROUP_RANGE`, `GROUP_ROUNDED`, `GROUP_QUANTILE`, `GROUP_NUMERIC_RANGES`) as strings (`10` before `9.5`, `D10` before `D2`) | an `order_by` naming a grouper key column (`groups`, or a crosstab row / column axis under a post-test) sorts by that grouper's key order — `day_of_week` Monday → Sunday, date-range and numeric-range labels in supplied order, numeric buckets by value — reversed under `desc`. ISO date keys and numeric columns order exactly as before; any other string column stays byte-wise | behaviour change (values: `s`, `tau`, Z, p) | a caller that ordered by a numeric weekday column to dodge the alphabetical sort can order by the key directly; one that relied on the alphabetical order adds an explicit numeric order column | U50 (E5-S1) |
| `ATTR_DATE_PART` over a null `date` / `datetime` cell answered `0` for every part — a real-looking number that dragged a `MIN` to `0`, counted in a `COUNT` of the part and keyed a `"0"` group — while a null `tz_field` cell already answered `null` | every part is `null` on a null source cell: absent from aggregations (counted in their `n_null`), in no group bucket (the grouper's `n_null`), `null` in `Data`, in every execution mode. Non-null rows are unchanged. `FEAT_DATE_FEATURES` already answered `null` and is unchanged; the score attributes follow in E6-S1 (next rows) | behaviour change (values: `0` → `null`) | a caller that filtered `part != 0` to drop missing dates can drop that step; one that counted the `0` rows reads the aggregation's `n_null` (or `AGG_NULL_COUNT` on the source field) | U50 (E5-S2) |
| `Request.Sort` and every window `order_by` (`WIN_LAG`, `WIN_RUNNING_SUM`, `WIN_RANK`, `WIN_DENSE_RANK`, …) compared a grouper key column byte-wise, so sorting or windowing grouped rows on it ran alphabetically: `day_of_week` as Friday, Monday, Saturday, …; `GROUP_DATE_RANGES` labels alphabetically; numeric bucket keys (`GROUP_RANGE`, `GROUP_ROUNDED`, `GROUP_QUANTILE`, `GROUP_NUMERIC_RANGES`) as strings (`10` before `9.5`, `D10` before `D2`) | a `sort` key or window `order_by` key naming a grouper key column of a grouped request follows that grouper's key order, exactly as `TEST_TREND` (E5-S1): `day_of_week` Monday → Sunday, date-range and numeric-range labels in supplied order, numeric buckets by value — reversed under `desc`, nulls still last. `WIN_RANK` / `WIN_DENSE_RANK` ties are ties of that same comparator. Buffered, streaming, `ShardWorkers` and `DecodeWorkers` arms agree, and grouped `matrices` follow the sorted rows. ISO date keys, numeric columns, record rows (no groups, including `Materialize`) and any other string column order exactly as before | behaviour change (row order; window values: lag / lead / running / rank figures) | drop a numeric order column added to dodge the alphabetical order; a caller that relied on the alphabetical order sorts on an explicit order column | U50 (E5-S5) |
| `ATTR_ZSCORE` / `ATTR_TSCORE` / `ATTR_NORMALIZED` / `ATTR_PERCENTILE` over a null source cell (any field type) answered `0` (`50` for the T-score) — a real-looking score: a `0` z-score reads as exactly average, a `0` percentile sat below every ranked row, and each was counted in a `COUNT`, a group bucket and every mean of the score | the score is `null` on a null source cell: absent from aggregations (counted in their `n_null`), in no group bucket (the grouper's `n_null`), `null` in `Data`, in every execution mode. The pre-pass already skipped nulls (the mean, sd, min, max and rank base are unchanged), so every non-null row's score is unchanged. A constant field scores `null` too since E7-S2 (the E7-S2 row below; `ATTR_PERCENTILE`: distinct ranks) | behaviour change (values: `0` / `50` → `null`) | a caller that filtered `score != 0` (or `!= 50`) to drop missing inputs can drop that step — it also dropped exactly-average rows; one that counted the missing rows reads the aggregation's `n_null` (or `AGG_NULL_COUNT` on the source field) | U50 (E6-S1) |
| `ATTR_SET_POPCOUNT` over a null set answered `0`, the same number as an empty selection, so a mean of the count disagreed with `AGG_SET_CARDINALITY_AVG` (which skips nulls) | a null set is `null` (counted in `n_null`); an empty selection stays `0`. The membership flags `ATTR_CODE_IN` and `ATTR_SET_HAS` deliberately keep reading a null as `0` ("not in the set"): their mean is the share of the WHOLE base, and `FILTER_NULL` drops the nulls first when the share should be among those who answered | behaviour change (values: `0` → `null`) | a mean of the count now matches `AGG_SET_CARDINALITY_AVG`; to keep reading a null set as `0`, use an `ATTR_FORMULA` `tags == nil ? 0 : popcount(tags)` | U50 (E6-S1) |
| a crosstab beside `ATTR_PERCENTILE` (or an extension attribute registered with the `buffered` mode and a `FieldInputs` hook) predicted `crosstab_fusable: true` and then failed `PROCESSING_INTERNAL` ("does not implement RowLocalAttribute") on the fused arm | both predict and the runtime decline fusion with the reason `buffered attribute (<TYPE>)` and the request runs the buffered crosstab | fix (an error becomes a result) | none | U50 (E6-S1) |
| an extension attribute (`extend.AttributeComputer`, `RowLocalAttribute`, `TwoPassAttribute`) owed every row a `float64` and had no null output: a `NaN` was folded as a value, so it could not follow the null-in, null-out rule the built-in scores follow (U50 E6-S1) | new optional sibling `extend.NullableAttribute` (`EmitsNull() bool`, per instance): when it reports true, every `NaN` the attribute returns from `Compute` or `Row` is written as a null in every execution mode — `null` in `Data`, skipped and counted in `n_null` by a downstream aggregation, in no group bucket. An attribute without it (or reporting false) is byte-identical | API addition | implement `EmitsNull() bool { return true }` and return `math.NaN()` for a row with no value (e.g. a null source cell) instead of `0` | U50 (E7-S1) |
| `ATTR_ZSCORE` / `ATTR_TSCORE` over a zero population sd and `ATTR_NORMALIZED` over a zero range (`max = min`) scored every row `0` / `50` / `0`: a constant field, a base with ONE non-null row (the population sd, ddof 0, of one value is 0), or — weighted z / T — a base whose valid, non-zero weights sit on one value or no row at all. The figure is 0/0, undefined, yet read as exactly average and was counted in every `COUNT`, bucket and mean of the score | every row's score is `null` (counted in the aggregation's `n_null`, in no group bucket), in every execution mode, unweighted and weighted alike; a field with any spread scores exactly as before. `AGG_ZSCORE` (null since U30 E4-S6), the overlays (`PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_ZERO`) and the fitted multivariate attributes (`PULSE_MATRIX_SINGULAR`) already answered null | behaviour change (values: `0` / `50` → `null`) | a caller that read a constant score as "everyone is average" reads `null` instead; check the source field's spread (`AGG_STDDEV` / `AGG_RANGE` = 0) or the score's `n_null` | U50 (E7-S2) |

## Changes from U55 (previous state)

Ships in the next alpha. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; every wire change is additive `omitempty`.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a long panel (one row per subject per time point) had no `from` column: a from-state x to-state crosstab or a `flows` overlay needed a pre-pivot to wide `state_t1` / `state_t2` fields | new `types.ATTR_PREVIOUS_STATE` with two new `types.Attribute` fields, `SubjectField` (`subject_field`) and `OrderField` (`order_field`, a `date` / `datetime`); `Field` is the state field. It scores each record with its subject's previous state (null on a first record) as a categorical column inheriting the state field's dictionary, usable on a `GROUP_CATEGORY` axis, in an aggregator, an expression (BY STATE NAME: `prev == "paid"`) and a materialized cohort (an ordinary `categorical_*` column; the SPSS sidecar carries the state variable's labels, `Observed` flags recomputed). Buffered only, not weightable, not zone-capable | additive | none for existing requests (byte-identical without the attribute); an exhaustive `switch` over `AttributeType` gains a case | U55 |
| every attribute ran after the filters | `ATTR_PREVIOUS_STATE` alone runs over EVERY decoded record before the filter pass (a date window keeps a subject's first in-window record with its TRUE previous state); new `descriptor.PredictResult.ComputedBeforeFilters` (`computed_before_filters`, `omitempty`) lists the labels. Its Components floor `{n, n_null}` counts every scored record (`n + n_null` = `Run.total_records`) | additive | read `computed_before_filters` if you render predict; other attributes are unchanged | U55 |
| two records of one subject sharing an order value had no rule | new error code `errors.PULSE_PREVIOUS_STATE_AMBIGUOUS_TIE` (a warning; `Strict` fails the slot with it). Tied records (same subject and order value) each take the state at the last earlier distinct time point; the record after a tie is null only when the tied states differ | additive | match the new code in an exhaustive error switch | U55 |
| the bound MCP request schema typed `attributes[].field` numeric, so a schema-validating MCP client could not name a categorical field for `ATTR_CODE_IN`, `ATTR_DATE_PART` or `ATTR_SET_HAS` | the bound per-type schema accepts the field types each operator takes (categorical, date, set); `ATTR_PREVIOUS_STATE` is bound the same way | behaviour (schema widening) | a client that validated against the old schema now accepts more; nothing narrows | U55 |
| `OVERLAY_INDEX_VS_BASELINE` out-of-range baseline `Position` read differently on predict ("...exceeds the predicted host series length") and at runtime ("...is out of range for the host series") | one shared message (`overlayhost.BaselineIndexOutOfRange`), same code | behaviour (message) | do not match the message text; match the code | U55 |

## Changes from U34 (extension validation)

Ships in the next alpha. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; the one wire change is the removed manifest key `extensions.synth_distributions` (always `[]` — it never listed anything usable; the row below). Every new check is a hard refusal at `pulse.New` (and `CheckFeatureProfile`), never a warning.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| an operator value with its own `Components()` method and no `ComponentsFunc` emitted at runtime unchecked, and could not declare `ComponentSchema.Keys` (refused `PULSE_EXTENSION_PARAM_INVALID`, reason `schema_without_emitter`) | the self-emitter may declare `ComponentSchema.Keys` (+ `Mergeability`) and `pulse.New` probes it like a `ComponentsFunc`: its `Components()` is called once on the probe instance and an emitted key outside the declared keys plus the universal floor is refused with `PULSE_EXTENSION_COMPONENT_SCHEMA_MISMATCH` (`details.emitter` `"Components()"`; a `ComponentsFunc` mismatch now carries `"ComponentsFunc"`); a panic or error from it is `PULSE_EXTENSION_FACTORY_PANIC`. Aggregators, groupers, filterers and attributes alike | behaviour change (new refusal) | declare every key the method emits; make `Components()` safe on an instance that has seen no row (an empty map passes) | U34 |
| a self-emitting aggregator, grouper or filterer with NO `ComponentSchema.Keys` was accepted and emitted undeclared keys (absent from the manifest and predict) | refused at `pulse.New` with `PULSE_EXTENSION_MISSING_COMPONENT_SCHEMA`, reason `self_emitter_without_schema_keys` — the same rule a `ComponentsFunc` already obeyed | behaviour change (new refusal) | declare `ComponentSchema.Keys` + `Mergeability` for what the method emits, or remove the method to stay floor-only | U34 |
| an extension attribute's own `Components()` method was ignored (only a `ComponentsFunc` emitted) | adopted as the emitter, as for the other three categories: a self-emitting attribute that declares Keys gets its `Response.Components.Attributes` entry and stays off the chain merge arm (`PULSE_CHAIN_NOT_MERGEABLE`, predict and runtime alike); one that declares none is refused as above | behaviour change | declare Keys (the attribute then emits), or drop the method | U34 |
| `ComponentSchema.Keys` with no `ComponentsFunc` was refused in `validateExtensions` before any factory ran | refused by the factory probe instead (same code `PULSE_EXTENSION_PARAM_INVALID`, same reason `schema_without_emitter`) when the probe value has no `Components()` method; a registration that also omits `Mergeability` now reports reason `missing_mergeability` first (same code) | behaviour (ordering) | none; match the code, not the reason order | U34 |
| a `FeatureRegistration` whose `Streamable` disagreed with its value passed `pulse.New`; predict read the declaration while the runtime streamed on the value, so the two disagreed silently | `pulse.New` probes each feature factory once and refuses the mismatch IN BOTH DIRECTIONS with `PULSE_EXTENSION_STREAMABLE_MISMATCH` (`details.category` `"feature"`, `details.streamable` = the declaration): `Streamable: true` with a value lacking `extend.StreamingFeatureComputer`, or `Streamable: false` with one implementing it. A feature factory that panics, errors or returns nil on the probe spec (only `Name` set, empty schema) is `PULSE_EXTENSION_FACTORY_PANIC` | behaviour change (new refusal) | set `Streamable` to whether the returned value implements `extend.StreamingFeatureComputer`; make the factory tolerate an empty schema and a spec carrying only the `Name` | U34 |
| an extension aggregator's column always rounded under `return.precision`, even when its value was a count | `AggregatorRegistration.CountSemantic bool` (manifest `count_semantic`): declared, the slot's `data` column, a count crosstab cell's matrix figures and a count auxiliary margin aggregation's figures are written exact, as for the built-in counts | addition | declare `CountSemantic: true` on a count aggregator; nothing to do otherwise | U34 |
| — | `CountSemantic` on a `VectorInput` or `ArrayValued` aggregator is refused at `pulse.New` with `PULSE_EXTENSION_PARAM_INVALID`, reason `count_semantic_vector_input` | new refusal (of a new field only) | declare `CountSemantic` on single-field aggregators only | U34 |
| every extension aggregator was priced by `MaxEstimatedMemory` as a fixed-size accumulator | `AggregatorRegistration.ValueState bool` (manifest `value_state`): declared, the estimate charges the same per-distinct-value term as `AGG_MODE` / `AGG_DISTINCT_COUNT`, on predict and the run's pre-flight alike — a request near the limit may now be refused | addition | declare `ValueState: true` on an operator whose state keeps a map entry per value; recheck a tight `MaxEstimatedMemory` | U34 |
| an aggregator, attribute, filterer, grouper or feature registration with a nil `Factory` passed validation and failed only in the probe as a recovered `PULSE_EXTENSION_FACTORY_PANIC`; a window registration with a nil `Factory` was accepted outright | refused in `validateExtensions` before any probe with `PULSE_EXTENSION_PARAM_INVALID`, details `{category, name, index, reason: "missing_factory"}` — the shape matrix operators already used | behaviour change (code change; new refusal for windows) | supply a `Factory`; match `PULSE_EXTENSION_PARAM_INVALID` / `missing_factory` instead of `FACTORY_PANIC` for a nil one | U34 |
| the explicit-schema late-key refusal was armed through `iocore.LateKeyRefuser`, which had no public name or documentation, so a keyed reader outside the library had no documented way to opt in and dropped late keys silently | public alias `io.LateKeyRefuser` (`RefuseLateKeys(on bool)`, kept across `Reset`). `ImportJob` / `ConvertJob` (`Run` and `Predict`) call `RefuseLateKeys(!IgnoreExtra)` when an explicit `Schema` is matched by name; an inferred import never calls it. Armed, `ReadRows` should fail with `PULSE_IMPORT_SCHEMA_KEY_LATE` (details `column`, `row` 1-based, `columns`) at the first record carrying a key outside the header | addition | a keyed custom reader (column set = first record's keys) implements `io.LateKeyRefuser` and refuses a late key with that code when armed | U34 |
| an extension operator could read a `u64` field only through `extend.Record.NumericValue`'s float64 echo, so values above 2^53 collided (2^53 and 2^53+1 read alike) | `extend.Record.Uint64Value(field) (uint64, bool)`: the exact stored word on every decode path (streaming, buffered, parallel, shard archive, point lookup, filter-to-file), up to `math.MaxUint64`; `(0, false)` exactly when `NumericValue` is false; a non-`u64` field converts its echo (saturating at 2^64). `NumericValue` is unchanged (still the float echo) | addition (a new method on the consumer-only `extend.Record` interface — a compile break only for code that IMPLEMENTS `extend.Record` itself, e.g. a test fake) | read `Uint64Value` to key or match on a `u64`; a hand-written `extend.Record` fake adds the method | U34 |
| `FilterToFileWithRequest` translated its structured `Filterers` into one expression and refused every other type — an extension filterer, `FILTER_TRUE` / `FILTER_FALSE`, `FILTER_SET_*`, `FILTER_DATE_RANGES` — with `PROCESSING_CONFIG` `filter_to_file: unsupported filter type "<T>"`; any filterer `tz` / `tz_field` was refused with its own `filter_to_file: operator <T> does not accept ...` message | every registered filterer is built through the engine filterer registry exactly as `Process` builds it and keeps the same rows (exact `u64` via `extend.Record.Uint64Value`; its `FieldInputs` hook drives the projection). `FILTER_DATE_RANGES` runs with zones resolved exactly as `Process` resolves them (slot `tz` → slot `tz_field` → request `TimeZone` → `Options.DefaultTimeZone` → UTC; an inherited zone is not applied to a `date` field), and every zone it applies (a non-UTC zone or a `tz_field` on a `datetime` field) joins the dedup predicate hash, so a request differing only in resolved zone gets its own output. Zone refusals are `Process`'s own message and details (`filterers[i]: operator <T> does not accept ...`, details `{slot, operator, tz}`; an extension filterer stays non-zone-capable). A hidden or never-registered type keeps the unsupported-type refusal. Requests without a zone-capable filterer or a `tz` / `tz_field` hash exactly as before | behaviour change (refusal → supported; zone-refusal message and details change) | pass an extension or set filterer directly instead of rewriting it as an expression; drop any `FILTER_EXPRESSION`-over-`to_day()` workaround if you prefer the operator; match zone refusals on code + `details.slot` / `operator`, not the message | U34 |
| `Extensions.SynthDistributions []DistributionRegistration` reserved a `SYNTH_<NS>_<NAME>` name and listed it in the manifest's `extensions.synth_distributions`, but the registration's `Factory` was never read — nothing could sample from it | removed: the `pulse.DistributionRegistration` type, the `Extensions.SynthDistributions` field, `descriptor.ExtensionsManifest.SynthDistributions` and the manifest key `extensions.synth_distributions` are gone, and `SYNTH` is no longer an extension category prefix (a `SYNTH_*` name fails the naming policy, `PULSE_EXTENSION_NAME_INVALID`; `CheckFeatureProfile` reports it unknown). Synthetic-data distributions are not an extension category in v1; the built-in kinds stay in the top-level `synth_distributions` section | break (compile + manifest key removal) | delete the registration (it had no effect); read distributions from the top-level `synth_distributions` | U34 |
| the `FilterToFileWithRequest` dedup hash carried a registered table's NAME only, and any predicate reused an existing output: two instances registering the same-named `RangeTables` / `LookupTables` / `ReferenceVectors` entry with different content, or the same-named extension filterer / `ExprFunction` / function-driven lookup table with different code, silently shared one output — a wrong result | each table the predicate references (`FILTER_DATE_RANGES` `params.table`, `lookup("name", ...)`, `ref("name", ...)`; a non-literal name references every table of its kind) joins the predicate hash as a content digest, so a different table lands on a new output; a predicate running embedder code (an extension filterer, a registered `ExprFunction` call, a `lookup()` reaching a `Lookup`-func table) is never reused — it re-runs every call and atomically replaces the output (`Reused` always `false`). A predicate referencing no table hashes exactly as before | behaviour change (bug fix) | none for table-free predicates; an output keyed by a table-referencing predicate is re-materialized once under its new name (delete the old file at will); register a `Rows` table instead of a `Lookup` func where reuse matters | U34 |

Not shipped, by decision (v1; each may arrive additively in v1.x): named tables (`LookupTables`, `ReferenceVectors`, `LabelTables`, `RangeTables`) are not features — no feature profile hides one and they sit outside `feature_set_digest`; an extension test's `Interpretation` is checked for structure only (an extension declares no output keys to resolve paths against); extension operators are not zone-capable (an explicit `tz` on one is `PROCESSING_CONFIG`); synthetic-data distributions are not an extension category. Verified no-ops, not gaps: chain stage rows (stage schema is `categorical_u32` / `f64` only) and the synth fidelity iterator need no exact-`u64` word. `EstimateFacetMemory` prices facet fields only.

Release-notes grouping (rows above; there is no separate behaviour-breaks file — this section is the alpha's release-note source). **Breaks** — `Extensions.SynthDistributions`, `pulse.DistributionRegistration`, `descriptor.ExtensionsManifest.SynthDistributions` and the manifest key `extensions.synth_distributions` are removed (compile + manifest key removal; `SYNTH_*` names fail naming); a new `extend.Record` method `Uint64Value` breaks only hand-written `extend.Record` fakes. **New `pulse.New` refusals** (hard, never warnings) — a self-emitter's `Components()` keys probed against `ComponentSchema` (`PULSE_EXTENSION_COMPONENT_SCHEMA_MISMATCH`, `_MISSING_COMPONENT_SCHEMA` `self_emitter_without_schema_keys`, `_FACTORY_PANIC`); feature `Streamable` checked both ways (`_STREAMABLE_MISMATCH`); a nil `Factory` of any operator category is `PULSE_EXTENSION_PARAM_INVALID` / `missing_factory` (a nil one was `FACTORY_PANIC`; windows used to be accepted); `count_semantic_vector_input`. **Behaviour changes** — attributes adopt their own `Components()`; `schema_without_emitter` now comes from the probe; `FilterToFileWithRequest` runs extension, set, constant and `FILTER_DATE_RANGES` filterers (refusal → supported) and its dedup hash keys on table content and never reuses embedder-code predicates (a wrong-result fix); a tighter `MaxEstimatedMemory` may refuse once `ValueState` is declared. **Additive** — `AggregatorRegistration.CountSemantic` / `ValueState`, `io.LateKeyRefuser`, `extend.Record.Uint64Value`. **Fixes** — `PredictChain` agrees with `ProcessChain` for every extension operator (parity-pinned); the internal test-only `ValidateChain*` wrappers are deleted (no public effect).

## Changes from U56 (operation panic recovery)

Ships in the next alpha with U34. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; the one wire addition is the error code `PROCESSING_PANIC` in the manifest's error-code list. Guide: [Observability](../../src/library/observability.md#panics-inside-an-operation).

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a panic raised inside an operation (a faulty `extend` operator, an engine bug) unwound through the facade and crashed the host process | the facade, the engine's worker pools (`parallel_decode`, `shard_parallel`, `compose_parallel`, `compose_fits`) and the stream producers (`synth_stream`, `process_stream`) recover it and return `PROCESSING_PANIC` (`details.op`, `details.panic_type`, `details.stack` of at most 32 frames; never the panic value, no `Cause`); a stream gets it as its error terminator; the instance stays usable; with Logger/Hooks/Metrics the observed operation ends with the code | behaviour change (a crash becomes an error) | handle `PROCESSING_PANIC` like any coded error; a host that relied on the crash (a supervisor restart, a debugger) sets `Options.DisablePanicRecovery` | U56 |
| (none) | `Options.DisablePanicRecovery bool` (default false, per instance): every site re-panics with the ORIGINAL value, after the observed operation is ended with `PROCESSING_PANIC` | added (public-API golden changed) | set it to keep the old crash; the CLI and `pulse mcp` always recover (exit 1 with the coded error envelope) | U56 |
| `ComposeParallel` re-raised a slot's panic | with `FailFast=false` a panicking slot lands inside the existing `SERVICE_INTERNAL` aggregate (no `Cause`, so `errors.As` does not find `PROCESSING_PANIC`); `FailFast=true` keeps the chain | behaviour change | match `SERVICE_INTERNAL` for the aggregate | U56 |

Not recovered, by design: the `watch.go` stat poller, the `internal/obsprom` HTTP server and the `context.AfterFunc` in the stream observer; a panicking expression function was already recovered by the expression engine (`PROCESSING_RUNTIME`, unchanged); extension probe-time panics stay `PULSE_EXTENSION_FACTORY_PANIC` at `New`.

## Changes from U36 (reference oracles)

Ships in the next alpha. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`: figures that disagreed with R, data-dependent refusals on the existing per-slot failure shape, and refusals lifted where a frequency weight's expansion runs. Additive only, no old reader breaks: `details.ci_low` / `ci_high` on `TEST_FISHER_EXACT`, the `RegressionResult.advisories` slot (`omitempty`) with the new advisory code `PULSE_ADVISORY_PENALIZED_INFERENCE_UNDEFINED`, and `df` on a gamma `REG_GLM` result; `descriptor.Advisory` became an alias of the new `types.Advisory` (source-compatible). Every other row is a behaviour change, marked as such. The rows run in the order the effort landed them: tests (`TEST_*`, E1), regressions (E2), overlays (E3). Every figure in the "New" column equals R's, pinned at relative 1e-10 in a `TestReferenceOracle_*`; the before → after values with the R expression are in the U36 PR body.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TEST_TREND` `details.tau` was S / (n(n−1)/2) (tau-a), so tied values pulled it toward 0 and it disagreed with R whenever the series tied (0.5238 where R's `Kendall::MannKendall` and `cor(seq_along(y), y, method = "kendall")` give 0.5450 on a 15-point series with ties); a fully tied series reported 0 | tau-b, S / √(n₀(n₀ − n₂)) with n₂ the tied value pairs, matching R to 1e-10; `null` (undefined) when every value ties. S, Var(S), Z and p are unchanged; untied series are unchanged | behaviour change (figure) | none; a consumer that recomputed S / (n(n−1)/2) or read a flat-series `tau` as 0 reads null instead | U36 |
| `TEST_SHAPIRO_WILK` (row and post) ran at n = 3 or 4 per group and returned W', z and p from Royston's transform outside its calibrated range with an advisory `warning` | any group with n < 5 fails the slot alone with `PULSE_TEST_INSUFFICIENT_N` (`details.min_required` = 5): the result keeps `label` / `type` / `error`, figures absent, one `PULSE_SLOT_FAILED` warning; Strict fails the run with that code. R's `nortest::sf.test` refuses below 5 too | behaviour change (refusal) | read the slot's `error`; gather at least 5 rows per group, or use a QQ plot for smaller samples | U36 |
| `TEST_SHAPIRO_WILK` W' and p drifted from R at ~1e-9 relative (Blom scores from a Beasley-Springer-Moro quantile; p 0.27599133439 where `nortest::sf.test` gives 0.27599133486 on a 20-row sample) | W' = cor(x, m)² on qnorm-exact Blom scores, matching `nortest::sf.test` to 1e-10 | behaviour change (figure) | none; figures move in the 9th–10th significant digit | U36 |
| `TEST_BROWN_FORSYTHE` (row, post and frequency-weighted) reported F = 0, p = 0 and `reject_null` true when every group's absolute deviations from its median were constant (`ss_within` = 0) | F = +Inf (`null` on the wire), p = 0 when the groups' deviations differ; F and p NaN (`null`) with `reject_null` false when every deviation is equal — the exact limit of R's `anova(lm(abs(x − median) ~ g))` | behaviour change (figure) | treat a `null` statistic as "infinite or undefined", not 0; a consumer that read p = 0 as a rejection on an all-equal input no longer gets one | U36 |
| `TEST_FISHER_EXACT` `statistic` / `details.odds_ratio` was the sample cross-product ratio ad/bc (3.5 on [[6, 4], [3, 7]]), +Inf whenever b or c was 0 | the conditional maximum-likelihood odds ratio R's `fisher.test` reports (3.2742486015053243 there, R's `uniroot` tolerance refined away), closer to 1 in small tables; 0 when a or d is 0, else +Inf (`null` on the wire) when b or c is 0. p is unchanged here (its tie rule moves in a row below); the interval is additive (row below) | behaviour change (figure) | none; a consumer that recomputed ad/bc from `details.contingency` or compared against it reads a different (R-matching) number | U36 |
| A post-test (every family but `TEST_TUKEY_HSD`, which already skipped such a group row) failed the whole request with `PULSE_TEST_FIELD_NOT_NUMERIC` when a column it read held a null — a `WIN_LAG` column's leading row, a nullable field passed through — so lag-1 autocorrelation needed `WIN_LAG` `default: 0`, which biased r; a null `split_by` label formed its own `"<nil>"` group and a null `TEST_TREND` `order_by` key sorted last and stayed in | a row with a null in any column the post-test reads drops listwise (R's default `na.action`) and `details.n` (`per_group[].n`, the group `n`s) reports the rows used; R-pinned per family at 1e-10. A non-numeric non-null value still refuses `PULSE_TEST_FIELD_NOT_NUMERIC`; an undefined (NaN) cell still nulls the result | behaviour change (refusal lifted, figure) | drop the `default: 0` workaround on `WIN_LAG` columns that feed a post-test; read `details.n` for the rows used | U36 |
| `TEST_KS` (row, post and frequency-weighted) p was Stephens' corrected asymptotic, Q_KS((√en + 0.12 + 0.11/√en)·D), at every size and with ties — it disagreed with R everywhere (0.4536 vs `ks.test`'s 0.4156 on n = 5 / 6 with ties) | `ks.test(x, y)$p.value` at its defaults: exact when n₁·n₂ < 10000 (Σw₁·Σw₂ under frequency weights), conditional on the tie pattern when values tie (R ≥ 4.3), else the limiting Kolmogorov distribution at √(n₁n₂/(n₁+n₂))·D with R's own series tolerance. R-pinned at 1e-10 in every regime. D is unchanged; `OVERLAY_KS_VS_POP` follows in its own row below | behaviour change (figure) | none; small and tied samples move most (often to a smaller p), large untied samples move little | U36 |
| A frequency-weighted test, regression or correlation-inference cell refused (or nulled) below its minimum-n floor counted on raw rows, so a one-row group of weight 3 failed `TEST_BROWN_FORSYTHE` / `TEST_T` / the rank tests with `PULSE_TEST_INSUFFICIENT_N` although its `rep()` expansion runs, and a heavy single row refused with `INSUFFICIENT_N` where the expansion says `PULSE_TEST_VARIANCE_ZERO` | under `frequency` every minimum-n guard reads Σw (the expanded rows' count), so the slot equals its expansion at the floor too: it runs, or refuses / answers with the expansion's own code (zero variance, the singular-covariance result, the regression rank check). Probability weights and unweighted runs keep the raw-row floor | behaviour change (refusal lifted) | none; a frequency-weighted slot that used to fail its floor now returns figures — read `sum_weights` as its size | U36 |
| `TEST_SHAPIRO_WILK` (row and post) on a constant group reported W' = 1, p = 1 with a "sample variance is zero" warning | the slot fails alone with `PULSE_TEST_VARIANCE_ZERO` (`details` `{group, n, value}`): `label` / `type` / `error`, one `PULSE_SLOT_FAILED` warning; Strict fails the run. R's `nortest::sf.test` answers NA | behaviour change (refusal) | read the slot's `error`; a constant column is trivially non-normal-testable | U36 |
| `TEST_TUKEY_HSD` (post) skipped a result row whose mean or `n_column` cell was non-numeric or missing, then ran on the rest | refuses like every other post family: `PULSE_TEST_FIELD_NOT_NUMERIC` (non-numeric) or `SERVICE_VALIDATION` (missing column, including an absent `n_column`); null rows still drop listwise | behaviour change (refusal) | make sure the request aggregates the `n_column` (default `n`) and the mean column Tukey reads | U36 |
| `TEST_FISHER_EXACT` emitted no confidence interval for its odds ratio | `details.ci_low` / `details.ci_high`: `fisher.test(x, conf.level = 1 − alpha)$conf.int`, the exact conditional interval (R's `uniroot` tolerance refined away; 1.7045668553034932 / 8.825532453728055 on the frequency-weighted fixture). `ci_low` is exactly 0 when a or d is 0; `ci_high` is +Inf (`null`, key kept) when b or c is 0. The coverage follows the existing `alpha` (default 0.05 → 95%; outside (0, 1) still `PULSE_TEST_INVALID_ALPHA`) | additive (new keys) | none; read the bounds, and a `null` `ci_high` as "no upper limit" | U36 |
| `TEST_FISHER_EXACT` and `OVERLAY_FISHER_EXACT_CELL` two-sided p counted a table as "no more likely than observed" within a 1e-12 log-probability slack, so a table 8.4e-8 relative more likely was left out (p 0.3136180754837235 on [[20, 38], [40, 106]]) | `fisher.test`'s rule: probability ≤ observed × (1 + 1e-7), over max-shifted densities (0.3944449761955223 there, R-pinned at 1e-10). Tables without such a near tie move only in the last ulps | behaviour change (figure) | none; only tables with a probability within 1e-7 relative of the observed one move | U36 |
| `REG_OLS` / `REG_GLM` with `penalty` `l1` / `elasticnet` (no `resample`) reported plug-in standard errors over the kept predictors — OLS from the Gram diagonal alone, GLM from the active-set penalised sandwich — with p-values and intervals from them; zeroed predictors and (OLS) the intercept had no entry; predict warned `PROCESSING_REGRESSION_APPROXIMATE_SE` | `std_errors`, `p_values`, `ci_lower`, `ci_upper` are `null` on EVERY key (intercept, kept and zeroed predictors; key kept): the data choose the active set, so no analytical sampling distribution exists. The result gains `advisories: [{code: PULSE_ADVISORY_PENALIZED_INFERENCE_UNDEFINED, message, details: {slot, operator, penalty, suggested: {resample: bootstrap}}}]`, and predict raises the identical advisory in `advisories` (never a warning, never Strict-escalated; `Options.SuppressAdvisories` drops it from both). `PROCESSING_REGRESSION_APPROXIMATE_SE` is gone. Coefficients unchanged (glmnet-pinned); `vcov` still refused `PROCESSING_REGRESSION_VCOV_UNSUPPORTED`; multiplicity excludes the null p-values from its family | behaviour change (figures → null) + added | for uncertainty set `resample: bootstrap` / `jackknife` (replicate SEs, no advisory), or fit `l2` / no penalty; stop reading p-values off a lasso fit; filter on the advisory code instead of the warning | U36 (E2-S2) |
| `REG_OLS` ridge (`penalty: l2`) standard errors had no external pin | pinned to the closed-form ridge covariance σ̂²·A⁻¹·G·A⁻¹ (A = G + N*·α·I, σ̂² = RSS/(N* − p − 1)), Var(β₀) = σ̂²/N* + μᵀVμ, Student-t p on N* − p − 1 df, computed by R matrix algebra (`ols_penalty.json`, unweighted and frequency-weighted, 1e-10); no figure moved | oracle (no change) | none | U36 (E2-S2) |
| `REG_GLM` `family: gamma` fixed the dispersion at 1: `std_errors` from (XᵀWX)⁻¹, Wald-z `p_values` and normal intervals, no `df`, `vcov` (XᵀWX)⁻¹ — R's `summary(glm, dispersion = 1)`, not its default (SE 0.3194 / 0.3371, p 0.0253 / 0.1386 on `gamma_two_level`, `glm_irls.json`) | the dispersion is estimated as R's `summary.glm` does, φ̂ = Pearson χ² / `df`, `df` = N* − p − 1 (n; Σw under frequency weights; n_eff under probability): `std_errors` and `vcov` scaled by φ̂, `p_values` and `ci_lower` / `ci_upper` on a Student t with `df`, which the result now carries (`null` with every SE / p / bound when ≤ 0); there SE 0.14648777892360754 / 0.15456424576949906, p 0.0018016839051805155 / 0.014453115352759584 = R. φ̂ itself is not emitted. A robust `covariance` (dispersion-free) and `resample` keep Wald z with no `df`; binomial / poisson are unchanged; a probability-weighted gamma fit now warns `PULSE_WEIGHT_LOW_NEFF` at n_eff ≤ p + 1 (was < p + 1) | behaviour change (figure) + added (`df` on the result) | re-read gamma significance — SEs move by √φ̂, so a gamma fit with φ̂ < 1 tightens and one with φ̂ > 1 widens; read `df` for the t reference | U36 (E2-S3) |
| `PredictResult.PValues` (and the `PULSE_ADVISORY_MANY_TESTS` / `PULSE_ADVISORY_COMPOSE_FAMILIES_SPLIT` triggers reading it) counted one p-value per predictor of an `l1` / `elasticnet` `REG_OLS` / `REG_GLM` fit without `resample`, whose p-values have been null since E2-S2 | counts none of them, corrected or not — the runtime fold already left a null p out of every family's `m`; predict and runtime now agree (`TestRegPenalty_PValueCountPredictRuntimeParity`) | behaviour change (predict) | none; an advisory that fired only on lasso predictors no longer fires | U36 (E2-S3) |
| `REG_GLM` `family: gamma` with `selection` (`forward` / `backward` / `stepwise`) scored each candidate by deviance + 2k (`aic`) or deviance + log(n)·k (`bic`) — the fixed-dispersion score, right for binomial / poisson only — so a low-dispersion gamma search ignored real deviance drops (on `glm_gamma_selection.json`, shape 40, every search ended at the intercept-only model) | each candidate is scored by R's `AIC(glm)` / `BIC(glm)`: the gamma log-likelihood at dispersion deviance / n (`Gamma()$aic`), the dispersion counted as a parameter (k + 1); there x1 + x2 scores AIC −7.715055266471644 (was 6.791497380112904) and every `aic` search ends at x1 + x2, every `bic` one at x1, as R's `step()` does. Binomial / poisson / OLS scores are unchanged. Weighted (helper level only — `selection` still refuses any weight): frequency = the `rep()` expansion (BIC on log Σw), probability = the formula on w* (BIC on log n_eff) | behaviour change (selected model) | re-run gamma selections: low-dispersion data now keep predictors the old score dropped; `SelectedFeatures` may grow | U36 (E2-S5) |
| `OVERLAY_CHISQ_VS_REF` left a cell the reference never shows out of χ² and df but kept its target counts in the total that scales the expected counts, with no warning (χ² 13.6987, p 0.0568 with 6 stranded counts in `overlay_chisq_vs_ref.json`) | the target renormalises over the cells with a positive reference count — R's `chisq.test(target[C], p = ref[C], rescale.p = TRUE)`, 14.0712 / p 0.0499 there, pinned at 1e-10 — and each such cell holding target counts warns `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_ZERO` (`details` `{kind, reference, row_key, col_key, target_value}`), as `OVERLAY_CHISQ_VS_POP` does for a value the population lacks. Tables without stranded counts are unchanged | behaviour change (figure, warning added) | read the new warnings as departures in their own right (the target holds cells the reference cannot produce) | U36 (E3-S1) |
| `OVERLAY_KS_VS_POP` p was Stephens' corrected asymptotic, Q_KS((en + 0.12 + 0.11/en)·D), on both reconstruction paths — unlike `TEST_KS`, which reports `ks.test`'s p (0.0919 where `ks.test` gives 0.0326 exact on a 50 × 69 histogram pair, `shifted_small` in `overlay_ks_vs_pop.json`) | `ks.test`'s two-sided p through `TEST_KS`'s port. HISTOGRAM path: `ks.test` on the bucketised samples (each record at its bucket index, sizes = the bin totals), exact conditional on the bucket ties when m·n < 10000, else the limiting form — `TEST_KS` on the expanded bucket indices reports the same p. PERCENTILE path (no tie pattern): the uncorrected limiting form at √(n_subset·n_pop/(n_subset + n_pop))·D, `ks.test(exact = FALSE)`. R-pinned at 1e-10 (`shifted_small` 0.0326, `moderate` 0.1203 exact; `percentile_shifted` 0.4154). D is unchanged | behaviour change (figure) | none; small histogram pairs move most (often to a smaller p), large ones and the percentile path move little | U36 (E3-S4) |
| `OVERLAY_CHISQ_VS_POP`, `OVERLAY_PAIRWISE_PROBIT_T`, `OVERLAY_FISHER_EXACT_CELL`, the mean / proportion overlay kernels, and `OVERLAY_T_VS_REF` / `_Z_VS_REF` over an engine `AGG_WELFORD` series (unweighted, frequency, probability) were tested against expectations computed with the helper each overlay calls (or hand literals) | every expectation comes from R (`overlay_*.json`, `ov_compose_series_welford`) at relative 1e-10; no figure moved | oracle (no change) | none | U36 (E3-S1) |

## Changes from U53 (custom regressions)

Ships in the next alpha. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Options.Extensions` had eight categories; a `REG_*` model beyond `REG_OLS` / `REG_GLM` / `REG_BAYES_LINEAR` could not be registered, and `RegressionSpec` had no place for a model's own configuration | `Extensions.Regressions []pulse.RegressionRegistration` is the ninth category: `REG_<NS>_<NAME>` against `extend.Regression` — `StreamingRegression` (`UpdateRow` / `Merge` / `Finalize`, `Streamable: true`) or `BufferedRegression` (`Fit`), returning a narrow `extend.RegressionOutput` (coefficients with optional `StdErr` / `Statistic` / `PValue`, whole-fit statistics, `Vcov`, `Outputs`). `RegressionSpec.Params` (raw JSON, request key `params`) carries the model's own configuration, strictly checked against the registration's `Params`; `RegressionResult` gains `statistics` (per-coefficient test statistic) and `outputs` (declared extras), both `omitempty`, so a built-in fit is byte-identical. `outputs` survives `disable_components` and every `return` preset (it is in `minimal`); `statistics` is in `standard` and `full`. A built-in `REG_*` declares no params, so any `params` key on one is `PULSE_REQUEST_UNKNOWN_FIELD` (`regressions[i].params.<key>`), predict ≡ runtime. `RegressionRegistration.PValues` declares that the model reports p-values: declared, its p-valued coefficients join a `multiplicity` family and predict counts one per non-intercept predictor (`lower_bound`); undeclared, `p_values` is null on every coefficient (under `resample` too), predict counts none, a non-`none` `multiplicity` method is `PULSE_MULTIPLICITY_INVALID` (predict ≡ runtime) and a finite reported p-value is `PULSE_EXTENSION_REGRESSION_OUTPUT_MISMATCH` `undeclared_p_value`. New codes `PULSE_EXTENSION_REGRESSION_OUTPUT_MISMATCH` (probe and every run) and, for the form, the existing `PULSE_EXTENSION_STREAMABLE_MISMATCH`. The manifest lists models under `extensions.regressions` (with `output_schema`), `pulse schema` and the MCP `regressions[].type` enum carry them, and a feature profile that omits one makes it act never-registered | additive (category, fields, types, codes) | none for a caller; an embedder registers a model per `docs/src/internals/adding-regression.md` (set `PValues: true` on a model that reports p-values). Code that switched over the extension categories gains one; a client that validated `regressions[].type` against a fixed enum should read the instance schema | U53 (E1-S1 / E1-S2 / E1-S5) |
| `resample`, `selection`, `penalty`, `conf_level`, `covariance`, `vcov`, `save` and the other shared `RegressionSpec` slots had no meaning on a registered model | each shared slot is declared by the registration or refused: an undeclared one is `PROCESSING_CONFIG` `{regression, slot}` (`key` names a companion), raised by one rule at predict and runtime alike, and a test reflects over `RegressionSpec` so a new slot cannot reach an extension model unclassified. `family`, `link`, `max_iters`, `tol` and `prior` are always refused (carry the model's settings in `params`). `conf_level` is honoured on every model: the engine writes `ci_lower` / `ci_upper` = estimate ∓ q·SE from the reported (or, under `resample`, the replicate) standard error — q the Student-t on the model's reported `Fit.DF` (REG_OLS's rule), else the normal; normal under `resample` — `null` where the SE is | additive (new category) | declare `Vcov` / `Penalty` / `SupportsSelection` / `CovarianceTypes` on the registration for each slot the model honours; report `StdErr` and `Fit.DF` for t intervals | U53 (E1-S4) |
| media adstock was hand-built from `WIN_EWMA` `decay` (seeded at the first value, geometric only) | new window `WIN_ADSTOCK`: `params.kernel` `geometric` (`decay` in [0, 1)) / `weibull_cdf` / `weibull_pdf` (`shape`, `scale` > 0), `max_lag` (1..10000, required), `normalize` (default true); y_i = Σ w_l·x_{i−l} for l = 0..max_lag, starting from zero; a null row reads null and adds nothing later; `frame` refused. Bad or kernel-mismatched params are `PULSE_WINDOW_INVALID` at predict and run. R-pinned (`adstock.json`, 1e-10) | additive (operator) | use `WIN_ADSTOCK` for adstock (`WIN_EWMA` is unchanged; normalised geometric adstock matches it with `alpha = 1 − decay` once the start-up has faded) | U53 (E3-S3) |
| an embedder `ExprFunctions` entry could be named `hill`, and an expression could not compute a Hill saturation curve except by hand | `hill(x, half_saturation, shape)` = x^s / (x^s + k^s) is a reserved built-in in every expression site but `OVERLAY_FORMULA`: a null argument answers null, `x < 0`, `k <= 0` or `s <= 0` answers NaN (`null` on the wire); a wrong argument count is `PROCESSING_CONFIG` at predict and run. An embedder function named `hill` is refused `PULSE_EXTENSION_NAME_RESERVED` at `pulse.New` | behaviour change (registration refused) + additive (function) | rename an embedder `hill` function (e.g. `acme_hill`). A cohort field named `hill` keeps binding and shadows the built-in in that request: bare `hill` reads the field, and `hill(...)` fails compile (`PULSE_FORMULA_COMPILE_ERROR`), so rename the field to call the curve | U53 (E3-S3) |
| an extension operator's declared `ParamMeta.JSONType` and `Required` were checked only at registration (`pulse.New`); a request value of the wrong JSON type, or a request omitting a `Required` param, reached the factory, which decoded (or mis-decoded) it on its own | in all nine extension categories (aggregator, attribute, filterer, grouper, window, feature, test, matrix, regression), a registration that declares params has every declared value checked before any operator code runs: a value whose JSON type is not its `JSONType` (`null` included; `number` covers integers) or a missing `Required` param (an absent `params` object included) is refused `PULSE_REQUEST_PARAM_INVALID` (`details.path` `<slot>.params.<key>`, `reason` `wrong_type` with `expected` / `got`, or `missing_required`), identically at predict and runtime. Unknown keys stay `PULSE_REQUEST_UNKNOWN_FIELD`; `Default` is unchanged (still the factory's; `ExactlyOneOf` is enforced too, see the decision #12 row); a registration declaring no params still passes its params through unchecked | behaviour change (requests refused) + additive (error code) | callers: send each declared param as its declared JSON type and supply every `Required` param. Embedders: make each `ParamMeta.JSONType` match what the factory accepts (widen it, e.g. to `"string"` only if a string is meant), and mark a param `Required` only if no request may omit it | U53 (E1-S3) |
| a registered `REG_*` model could not be scored or saved: `ATTR_REG_PREDICT` on its fit was `PULSE_MODEL_SCORE_INVALID` reason `type_unsupported` (a fit without an `(intercept)` coefficient failed to bind at all, `PROCESSING_INTERNAL`) and `save` was refused on every extension model | `RegressionRegistration.Scorer` (`extend.RegressionScorerFactory`, building an `extend.RowScorer` from `extend.FittedRegression`) scores the model's fits — whole model only: `params.terms` / a `lower`/`upper` `params.bound` are `PULSE_MODEL_SCORE_INVALID` `terms_unsupported` / `bound_unsupported` — and lets `save` write a `model_format_version` 3 file carrying the fit's `outputs`. Without a `Scorer` the reason is now `no_scorer` and `save` stays refused. Loading a version-3 file on an instance that does not register (or hides) its type is the new `PULSE_MODEL_REGRESSION_UNREGISTERED`, at predict and runtime alike; a `Scorer` is probed at `pulse.New` (`PULSE_EXTENSION_FACTORY_PANIC` / `_REGRESSION_OUTPUT_MISMATCH`, `details.stage` `scorer`) | additive (field, types, error code, file version) + behaviour change (refusal reason) | match `reason` `no_scorer` where code matched `type_unsupported` on an extension fit; supply a `Scorer` to score or save a custom fit, echoing into `Outputs` any figure it needs beyond the coefficients. Built-in model files are unchanged (versions 1 / 2); a build before this one refuses a version-3 file with `PULSE_MODEL_VERSION_UNSUPPORTED` | U53 (E2-S2) |
| `resample` / `selection` on a registered `REG_*` model were refused `PROCESSING_CONFIG` `{regression, slot}` on every model (the drivers were not wired), and `SupportsSelection` had no effect | `resample` (with `jackknife_groups`, `jackknife_by`, `bootstrap_iters`, `rng_seed`) runs on every extension model — the built-in jackknife / bootstrap drivers refit a fresh model per replicate, drawing exactly REG_OLS's rows and seed, replacing `std_errors`, `statistics` (Wald z) and `p_values`; `selection` runs on a `SupportsSelection` model, scoring candidates by AIC / BIC on the fit's `Fit.Deviance`. A `SupportsSelection` model whose fit reports no deviance is refused at `pulse.New` (`PULSE_EXTENSION_REGRESSION_OUTPUT_MISMATCH` `missing_deviance`); a replicate with other coefficients than the full fit is `_OUTPUT_MISMATCH` `replicate_coefficients`. The manifest row always lists `resample` | additive (behaviour) + behaviour change (registration refused) | report `RegressionFit.Deviance` (−2·log L up to a constant) on a `SupportsSelection` model, or drop the flag; report the same coefficient names on every fit | U53 (E2-S4) |
| a `resample` / `selection` modifier on a built-in regression ran every refit to completion after `Options.Limits.RequestTimeout` had passed (the deadline was read only in the record loops), so a long bootstrap returned late and successfully | the replicate and candidate loops read the request ctx before every refit: a deadline mid-modifier is `PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, a cancel the ctx's error | behaviour change (late runs now refused) | raise `RequestTimeout` (or lower `bootstrap_iters`) for a long modifier run | U53 (E2-S4) |
| a feature profile enabling a registered regression but none of `REG_BAYES_LINEAR` / `REG_GLM` / `REG_OLS` failed `PULSE_FEATURE_PROFILE_DEPENDENCY` on `ATTR_REG_PREDICT`, `capability:model_save` and `capability:compose_fits` | an enabled extension regression meets those features' REG_* group — with a `Scorer` for `ATTR_REG_PREDICT` / `capability:model_save`, any for `capability:compose_fits`; `ATTR_REG_FITTED` / `RESIDUAL` still need `REG_OLS` | additive (profiles accepted) | none; a custom-only profile no longer needs a built-in REG_* listed | U53 (E2-S4) |
| `types.RegressionSpec.PriorPrecision` was a `float64` (`prior_precision`, scalar only: Λ₀ = ε·I) | it is `types.PriorPrecision`, built with `types.PrecisionScalar(ε)` or `types.PrecisionVector(v)` and read with `Scalar()` / `Vector()` / `IsVector()` / `IsZero()`: on the wire a number (byte-identical; 0 or absent = the default 1e-3) or an array of predictors + 1 entries, intercept first like `prior_mu`, for Λ₀ = diag(v) — 0 a flat prior on that coefficient. A wrong length or a negative entry is `PROCESSING_CONFIG` at predict and run; a flat prior the data leave improper is `PROCESSING_REGRESSION_RANK_DEFICIENT`. R-pinned (`bayes_precision.json`, 1e-10) | API break (field type) + additive (wire form) | Go: `PriorPrecision: 0.5` → `PriorPrecision: types.PrecisionScalar(0.5)`; read `spec.PriorPrecision.Scalar()`. JSON requests are unchanged | U53 (E3-S1) |
| predict accepted a `REG_BAYES_LINEAR` spec with `resample` / `selection`, a negative or mis-sized prior, a bad `prior` or another engine's knob (`penalty`, `alpha`, `l1_ratio`, `family`, `link`) — only the run refused it | predict refuses each with the runtime's `PROCESSING_CONFIG` code, message and details (one rule, `internal/regbayes`) | behaviour change (predict refuses) | fix the spec predict names; nothing that ran before runs differently | U53 (E3-S1) |
| ridge (`REG_OLS` `penalty: l2`) shrank every predictor | `unpenalized: [predictor names]` skips those on the ridge diagonal (glmnet `penalty.factor` 0; the ridge sandwich SEs read the same system), including in `resample` / `selection` refits. It is refused `PROCESSING_CONFIG` with `l1` / `elasticnet` / no penalty, on `REG_GLM` / `REG_BAYES_LINEAR`, for an unknown or repeated name, and on a registered `REG_*` model (`{regression, slot: unpenalized}`). R-pinned (`ols_penalty.json` `ridge_unpenalized_*`, 1e-10) | additive (field) | none; list controls or dummies that should stay unshrunk. Lasso / elastic-net penalty factors are not offered | U53 (E3-S1) |
| `REG_OLS` had no coefficient constraint; non-negative slopes (media-mix betas) needed an outside solver | `bounds: {nonnegative: [predictor names]}` constrains those slopes ≥ 0 (Lawson–Hanson on the centred Gram, weighted fits too; `resample` refits the bounded model per replicate). No binding bound = plain OLS byte for byte; a binding one keeps the coefficients (held ones exactly 0), R² and residual SE but reports `std_errors` / `p_values` / intervals / `vcov` entries `null`, with the new result-only advisory `PULSE_ADVISORY_CONSTRAINT_ACTIVE` (`details {slot, operator, active, suggested: {"resample": "bootstrap"}}`). Refused `PROCESSING_CONFIG` with any `penalty` or `selection`, on `REG_GLM` / `REG_BAYES_LINEAR`, for an unknown or repeated name, and on a registered `REG_*` model (`{regression, slot: bounds}`); a robust `covariance` is `PROCESSING_REGRESSION_VCOV_UNSUPPORTED` (new `details.reason` `bounds`). `types.RegressionResult` gains the never-serialised `ActiveBounds`. R-pinned (`ols_nnls.json`, `nnls` 1.6, 1e-10) | additive (field, type, advisory code, refusal reason) | none; set `resample: bootstrap` for intervals of a bounded fit. Code matching `PROCESSING_REGRESSION_VCOV_UNSUPPORTED` reasons gains `bounds`; an `Options.SuppressAdvisories` list may name the new code | U53 (E3-S2) |
| the `pulse.New` registration probe built every factory with no `params`, so a factory that refused an absent `Required` param (which a request can no longer omit, see the E1-S3 row) failed registration with `PULSE_EXTENSION_FACTORY_PANIC` | the probe hands each constructed factory (aggregator, attribute, grouper, feature, `WeightAware` test, matrix spec and `MatrixInput.Params`, regression) a params object satisfying its declaration: every `Required` param and the first member of every `ExactlyOneOf` group get a `JSONType` placeholder (number `1`, boolean `false`, object `{}`, string / array the probe's named fields, else `""` / `[]`); other params stay absent, and a registration needing none is probed exactly as before | behaviour change (registrations accepted) | a factory may now refuse an absent `Required` param itself. One that refuses the placeholder fails `pulse.New` with `PULSE_EXTENSION_FACTORY_PANIC` and `details.probe_params` — accept the placeholder at build time or check the value at fit / finalize | U53 |
| an `ATTR_REG_FITTED` / `ATTR_REG_RESIDUAL` naming `params.fit` (scoring a bound fit) was classed weight-aware: predict reported a weight in force on it `applied`, the runtime stamped the weight on the slot, and a probability weight was accepted although it changed no score | a `params.fit` slot is row-local for weighting, as `ATTR_REG_PREDICT` is: predict reports it `skipped_not_weight_aware` and the runtime stamps the slot weight `null` (the fit's own weight applied when it ran); its scores are byte-identical to the unweighted run. Without `params.fit` the slot is the weighted OLS refit, unchanged (`applied`) | behaviour change (reported status) | match `skipped_not_weight_aware` where code read `applied` on a `params.fit` slot; weight the fit itself (the `fits` request or the saved model's run) | U53 (decision #5) |
| `coef(fit, term[, stat])` accepted only `"(intercept)"` and the fit's predictors, so a coefficient a registered model names itself (a nonlinear parameter) was `PULSE_MODEL_TERM_UNKNOWN` at predict and run | on a registered `REG_*` fit `coef()` also reads every coefficient the fit reports by its own name — point, `se` and the engine's `conf_level` `lower` / `upper`, NaN where the fit reports none; `details.available` lists those names after the predictors. An unknown name stays `PULSE_MODEL_TERM_UNKNOWN`: against a loaded model file predict refuses it exactly as the run does; against a Compose `fits` entry of a registered model predict cannot know the names (they exist only once the model runs), so it accepts any term and the run refuses an unknown one. Built-in fits are unchanged. `outputs` are not coefficients and stay unreachable from `coef()` | additive (names accepted) + behaviour change (predict accepts an extension `fits` term) | none; on a registered model's `fits` entry, run the request (or load the saved model) to validate a model-named term | U53 (decision #6) |
| a factory accepting only certain values of a `Required` param (an enum-like string, a range) could not pass the `pulse.New` probe, which handed it the generic `JSONType` placeholder | `ParamMeta.Example` (optional, any JSON value; projected as manifest `example` on `OperatorParamMeta` and, for an extension `MAT_*` / `REG_*`, on `descriptor.Param`): the probe hands it to the factory in place of the placeholder for a `Required` param or an `ExactlyOneOf` group's first member; on any other param it is documentation only. `pulse.New` checks it with the request value checker — not a JSON value of its `JSONType`, or not marshalable, is `PULSE_EXTENSION_PARAM_INVALID` (`details.reason` `example_type_mismatch`, `expected` / `got`) | additive (fields, refusal reason) | declare an `Example` the factory accepts where the placeholder fails; a code matching `PULSE_EXTENSION_PARAM_INVALID` reasons gains `example_type_mismatch` | U53 (decision #11) |
| an extension operator's `ExactlyOneOf` group was declarative only: a request naming none, or several, of its members reached the factory, which had to refuse it on its own (or silently picked one) | in all nine extension categories a request carrying none, or more than one, member of a declared `ExactlyOneOf` group is refused `PULSE_REQUEST_PARAM_INVALID` before any operator code runs (`details.reason` `exactly_one_of`, `details.path` `<slot>.params`, `group`, `members`, `present`), identically at predict and runtime. Faults are checked in order: unknown key (`PULSE_REQUEST_UNKNOWN_FIELD`), wrong type, missing `Required`, then the group. A `Default` is still never injected — the factory applies it | behaviour change (requests refused) | callers: send exactly one member of each group (the manifest marks members `exactly_one_of`). Embedders: a factory may now rely on exactly one member being present; a code matching `PULSE_REQUEST_PARAM_INVALID` reasons gains `exactly_one_of` | U53 (decision #12) |
| an extension regression's p-value handling had no declaration: predict counted one p-value per predictor and a `multiplicity` block ran over whatever the fit reported | `RegressionRegistration.PValues bool` (the matrix-operator precedent): false, the model emits no p-values, a non-`none` `multiplicity` block is `PULSE_MULTIPLICITY_INVALID` at predict and runtime and predict counts 0; true, predict counts one per non-intercept predictor (`lower_bound`). A p-value from a model declaring `PValues: false` is `PULSE_EXTENSION_REGRESSION_OUTPUT_MISMATCH` | API addition + behaviour change (refusal) | set `PValues: true` on a model that reports p-values | U53 (decision 1) |

## Changes from U51 (archive scale)

Ships in the next alpha. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a shard archive was read whole onto the heap (`afero.ReadFile`) by `Open` and again by the scan, and by `OpenAnchor`, `CountRecords`, `shard list` / `verify` / `extract`, the anchored-build cohesion precheck, the filter-to-file archive arm and the limit pre-flight's `CohortCounts` | every one reads the archive by random access: the zip central directory at the tail plus the headers it needs (O(shards), not O(bytes)), each shard's records through its own buffered section. A Process reads the archive's bytes once; `CountRecords` still floors a truncated tail silently and `Inspect` still warns. The request byte counter (`observe` bytes read) on the per-shard parallel arm now reports the bytes actually read instead of the archive's length | behaviour change (memory, I/O, observed byte count) | none; `MaxEstimatedMemory`'s archive term follows in the E1-S2 row below | U51 (E1-S1) |
| a DEFLATED shard entry (written by a third-party zip tool; Pulse writes store-only) was decoded as raw compressed bytes by the serial scan and the per-shard reducer | both refuse it with `PULSE_ARCHIVE_CORRUPT` (`details.archive`, `details.entry`), as the index-addressed record reader already did | behaviour change (requests refused) | re-pack the archive store-only (`pulse shard extract` each shard, `pulse shard create`) | U51 (E1-S1) |
| an anchor-opened shard (`archive.pulse#shard.pulse`) was copied into memory at open, so its scan read that snapshot | a stored anchored shard is read from the archive at scan time through a section at its data offset (a deflated one is still inflated at open); the shard's bytes are never resident | behaviour change (no open-time snapshot) | keep archive rewrites single-writer, as for whole archives: replace the archive only between calls | U51 (E1-S1) |
| `MaxEstimatedMemory` charged a shard archive 3 x its whole byte length (the payload term's `max(records x stride, archive bytes)`), and an anchor-opened shard 3 x its ARCHIVE's byte length | an archive is charged 3 x its LARGEST shard's records x stride + 8 x its central-directory bytes + one canonical schema decode; per-row state still counts every record. An anchored shard is charged as the single file it is. Predict, the pre-flight refusal (`details.observed`), Compose / chain / facet findings and Explain move together, so the estimate falls (archive peak / estimate measured 0.42-0.82, was 0.14-0.36). Internal plumbing only: `descriptor.PredictOptions.AnchorArchiveBytes` (internal package) is gone, `LimitInputs` / `CohortCounts` carry `DirectoryBytes` instead of `ArchiveBytes` | behaviour change (estimate lower; a `MaxEstimatedMemory` cap that refused an archive may now admit it) | none; re-check a cap tuned to an archive's old estimate | U51 (E1-S2) |
| `Pulse.Predict` rebuilt the aggregator capability table on every call, so an off-path predict allocated 272 times | the table is built once per weighting variant (#390); the same off-path predict allocates 150 times. Predict output is unchanged | performance only | none | U51 (E1-S2) |
| `shard add` / `remove` / `compact` (and `create`) built the whole new archive in memory (about 3 x the archive resident for add / remove / compact) from fully decoded, CRC-checked shard payloads, then staged it as `<archive>.pulse.tmp-<random>` | the new archive streams into one deterministic stage, `<archive>.pulse.tmp-staged`: stored shards are copied RAW (zip `CreateRaw` from a section of the old file, no decode), the aggregate is summed from shard headers, memory is the arriving shard plus fixed buffers; only an archive-level set widen or a constant-group promotion re-reads stored shards, one at a time. Published by fsync + rename + a directory fsync; the next mutation sweeps every `<archive>.pulse.tmp-*` a crash left. A raw copy no longer re-checks a stored shard's CRC, so a stored shard with a stale CRC is carried through instead of failing the mutation with `ENCODING_IO` | behaviour change (memory, temp-file name, CRC not re-verified on mutation) | run `pulse shard verify` (or `Pulse.VerifyShardArchive`) to detect a corrupt stored shard; treat `*.pulse.tmp-*` beside an archive as Pulse's stage | U51 (E1-S3) |
| `shard create` held every source shard in memory at once (a widen re-laid-out the earlier ones in place), so memory grew with the sum of the sources | create plans first — an ungrouped include's header and schema only (count from the file length), an include with parent groups read whole one at a time — then streams each include once into the stage: verbatim when nothing changes, else loaded, rewritten and stored. Memory is one include plus the plan. The archive bytes, widenings and warnings are unchanged (create still equals create-then-add-each) | behaviour change (memory) | none | U51 (E1-S4) |
| shard cohesion (`create`, `add`, `verify`, the anchored-build precheck) and the shard dictionary rewrite compared each field's STORED byte offset, so a cohort written with all-zero offsets (an explicit `ImportJob` / `ConvertJob` schema before the canonical layout) or a schema-file cohort with a bit-packed first field (pre-canonical offsets) could not join, or be joined by, a canonical-offset cohort: `PULSE_SHARD_SCHEMA_MISMATCH` | the stored byte offset is a cache and is no longer compared — cohesion is names, type bytes, field widths (the type-derived layout), bit positions, vector dims and nullability. Such cohorts now join, add to and precheck against new cohorts and archives with no re-import (#373); `shard verify` no longer reports a stored-offset difference. Bit position is still compared: decoders read it | behaviour change (shards admitted) | none; re-imports done only to fix offsets are unnecessary | U51 (E1-S4) |
| an archive of more than 65535 shards was written with its `_schema.pulse` `shard_count` (a `u16`) silently wrapped | `shard create`, `shard add` and `cohort filter` writing an archive refuse it with the new `PULSE_SHARD_COUNT_LIMIT` (`details.archive`, `shard_count`, `limit`) before writing anything; `remove` / `compact` of an archive already past the limit refuse it too | behaviour change (writes refused) + new error code | concatenate small shards into fewer, larger ones, or split across archives | U51 (E1-S4) |
| the per-shard parallel arm (`ShardWorkers` fan-out over an archive) decoded every field of every row, so its plan stamp reported no projection (`observe.OperationResult.Projected` false, Debug `projected_fields` 0) | it decodes through the request's projection like every other scanning arm — one plan and one projected record binding per run, parent-group indices and exact `u64` words kept — so it reports `Projected` true and `projected_fields` = the retained-field count; results are unchanged (the projection is output-transparent). An anchor-opened stored shard now streams through a bounded window instead of being copied whole on each scan | behaviour change (observed plan fields, memory, speed) | none; dashboards keyed on `projected` for the `shard_parallel` arm see it set | U51 (E2-S2) |
| the CLI had no way to set `Options.ShardWorkers` / `Options.DecodeWorkers` (every leaf ran with `0` = one worker per CPU) | global persistent flags `--shard-workers N` and `--decode-workers N` on every leaf, `pulse mcp` included; `0` (default) one per CPU, `1` serial; a negative value is a usage error (exit 2, `CLI_INPUT` `{flag, value}`); no environment-variable form | new CLI surface | pass the flags where a host must cap concurrency or memory | U51 (E2-S2) |
| predict's memory estimate ignored `Options.DecodeWorkers`: a mergeable request over a large single file was charged as the serial arm even when the run fanned out into per-worker partials | predict and the pre-flight count the parallel-decode reducer's per-worker partials exactly when the run takes that arm (a memory-mappable single file of at least 100,000 records, `DecodeWorkers` resolving to two or more, a mergeable request); the estimate (`details.observed`) rises on that arm | behaviour change (estimate higher on the parallel-decode arm; a tight `MaxEstimatedMemory` may now refuse) | none; set `DecodeWorkers: 1` (or `--decode-workers 1`) to keep the serial arm and its estimate | U51 (E2-S2) |
| float sums were a naive sequential `+=`, so `AGG_SUM`, `AGG_AVERAGE`, `AGG_RATIO`, the weighted sums (weighted `AGG_COUNT` / `AGG_SUM` / `AGG_AVERAGE`, `AGG_WEIGHTED_MEAN`'s Σ keys), the `sum_weights` / `n_eff` floor, `ATTR_FORMULA` `total()`, the weighted counting aggregators (weighted `AGG_FREQUENCY` match_count / share, `AGG_SET_FREQUENCY` per-member Σw, `AGG_SET_CARDINALITY_SUM` / `_AVG`) and `AGG_DISTINCT_SUM` depended on row order, shard split and `ShardWorkers` / `DecodeWorkers` (last-ULP drift; the docs claimed byte-equality) | every one accumulates in a binned reproducible sum (ReproBLAS fold 3) on every path — serial, streaming, buffered, fused crosstab, both parallel reducers — so the figure is a function of the input multiset: bit-identical across worker counts, shard splits, archive vs single file, and amd64 vs arm64. Serial float-sum bits change ONCE (low-order bits; the binned sum is not correctly rounded, error ≲ 7ε·\|sum\|, usually closer to the exact sum than `+=`). Welford figures (variance, stddev, `AGG_WELFORD`, shape moments, `m2_weighted`, `weighted_variance`) stay within ULP across arms. `AGG_DISTINCT_SUM` is exact only when each key carries one value (first value wins per arm). Per-group state grows (56 bytes per sum; a weighted `AGG_SET_FREQUENCY` keeps one per member) | behaviour change (low-order bits of every float sum, mean and ratio) | re-pin any golden or fixture that stored a float sum's exact bits; drop tolerances that only absorbed worker-count drift on these operators | U51 (E2-S3) |
| the per-shard parallel arm kept one partial (the shard's whole group map) per SHARD alive until a single final merge, so its peak memory grew with the archive's shard count | partials merge incrementally, in shard order, as soon as every earlier shard is done, and are then released; no shard is dispatched more than `2 x ShardWorkers` past the first unmerged one, so at most that many partials are alive beside the merged result whatever the shard count. Answers are byte-identical (the merge order is unchanged). On that arm the merge time is now reported in the `scan` phase (`observe.PhaseScan`) and `reduce` covers the finalize | behaviour change (memory, observed phase split) | none; dashboards that read the shard-parallel `reduce` phase as merge time see it inside `scan` | U51 (E2-S4) |
| `MaxEstimatedMemory` charged a group bucket `512 + 256 x aggregations`, about half the measured peak of a high-cardinality grouped run (the finalize output — the `data` row and per-group components — was not charged), counted weighted slots, `AGG_SET_FREQUENCY` per-member counters and `AGG_SET_DISTINCT_VALUES` masks as fixed accumulators, and on the per-shard arm charged every shard's partial | a bucket is charged its state (`256 + 256` per unweighted / `512` per weighted aggregation `+ 64` per `AGG_SET_FREQUENCY` set member) plus, once merged, its output (`1024 + 512` per aggregation `+ 128` per set member); parallel partitions are charged state only, and on the per-shard arm only the merge window's `2 x ShardWorkers` largest shards; `AGG_SET_DISTINCT_VALUES` is charged per distinct value. A slot is weighted when a weight is in force on it, `Options.DefaultWeight` included. Predict, the pre-flight refusal (`details.observed`), Compose / chain findings and Explain move together | behaviour change (estimate higher on grouped runs, lower on many-shard archives; a tight `MaxEstimatedMemory` may now refuse a grouped request it admitted) | re-check a cap tuned to a grouped request's old estimate | U51 (E2-S4) |
| an `ATTR_FORMULA` calling `total(expr)` kept a request off both parallel reducers: `Options.ShardWorkers` over an archive and `Options.DecodeWorkers` over a large single file ran it on the sequential two-pass arm | an UNGROUPED request whose `total()` formulas read only source fields or row-local labels (no `total()` argument or formula reads another total, directly or through row-local slots) runs two-phase on both reducers: a parallel pre-pass (projected to the filter and argument fields) merges binned `{sum, count}` partials exactly and the reduce binds the published totals — the same bits as serial under any worker count or shard split; the run reports the parallel arm (`observe` `shard_parallel` / `parallel_decode`) and predict's fan-out / memory estimate follows. Grouped, multi-layer and `ProcessChain` `total()` are unchanged (sequential; a chain stage keeps `PULSE_CHAIN_NOT_MERGEABLE`), as is `PULSE_ADVISORY_EXTRA_PASS` (one added scan; its message now names the parallel pre-pass) | behaviour change (execution arm, observed arm / workers, predicted memory) | none; answers are bit-identical | U51 (E3-S1) |
