| a request carrying `ATTR_SCALE_SCORE`, an `ATTR_PROFILE_SIMILARITY` over any reference but a centroid, or an `ATTR_VEC_DISTANCE` over a row-local metric (not `std_euclidean` / `mahalanobis`) and any reference but a centroid, ran on the sequential arm under `Options.ShardWorkers` (archive) and `Options.DecodeWorkers` (large single file), and a `ProcessChain` stage carrying one was refused `PULSE_CHAIN_NOT_MERGEABLE` (their per-run `Components.Attributes` counts and warnings had no merge) | both run on the parallel reducers — each partition scores its rows on its own instance and the tallies fold exactly (integer counts; the unmatched-key list keeps the smallest ten of the union), so the `components.attributes` entry and the `PULSE_ATTR_SCALE_OUT_OF_RANGE` / `PULSE_VECTOR_REFERENCE_MISSING` / `PULSE_VECTOR_METRIC_DOMAIN` warnings equal the serial run's, each warning raised once; a chain stage admits them (runtime and `PredictChain` alike), and the manifest's `process_chain.row_local_attributes` lists them (with `ATTR_CODE_IN`, `ATTR_SET_HAS`, `ATTR_SET_POPCOUNT` and `ATTR_REG_PREDICT`, which the gate already admitted; `ATTR_VEC_DISTANCE` with a rejection rule for its fitted metrics and centroid). The centroid forms and a fitted-metric `ATTR_VEC_DISTANCE` stay sequential and chain-refused | behaviour change (execution arm, observed arm / workers, predicted memory; newly admitted chain stages; manifest list) | none; answers are identical | U51 (E3-S2) |

## Changes from U31 (guidance guides)

Ships in the next alpha. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change. Example requests and metadata, two additive example fields, one additive Recommend field and the CLI example search: no engine behaviour moves.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| example `scale_reliability` (`matrices/08_reliability.json`) ran on `experiment.pulse` (`revenue_before`, `revenue`, `session_minutes`): a Heywood fit, omega null with `PULSE_MATRIX_HEYWOOD` (+ `PULSE_MATRIX_NOT_CONVERGED`), alpha if deleted 0.98 | it runs on the new one-trait fixture `battery.pulse` (six 1-5 items, `bat_dread` reverse-keyed via `params.reverse` + `scale_min` 1 / `scale_max` 5), `return.precision` 4: alpha about 0.82, omega about 0.82, no warning, every alpha if deleted below alpha | example output change | a harness quoting the old figures or the Heywood warning re-reads the example; the Heywood refusal itself is unchanged (`skills/op-mat-reliability.md`) | U31 (E1-S4) |
| example `principal_components` (`matrices/09_pca.json`) ran on the same three `experiment.pulse` measures: three eigenvalues, KMO about 0.49 | it runs on `battery.pulse`, `return.precision` 4: six eigenvalues, the first about 3.19 (about 53% of the variance) and the rest below 0.7, one component retained, KMO about 0.87 | example output change | none beyond re-reading the example | U31 (E1-S4) |
| example `post_trend_on_dates` (`tests/09_post_trend_on_dates.json`) ran Mann-Kendall on DAILY `AGG_AVERAGE` revenue: p about 0.10, no rejection, against a README claiming it picked up the planted drift | it runs on the WEEKLY `AGG_RATIO` revenue / revenue_before (label `revenue_lift`, test label `weekly_lift_trend`), `return.precision` 4: 14 weeks, tau about 0.85, p about 3e-05, rejects | example output change | a harness keyed on the old labels (`avg_revenue`, `daily_revenue_trend`) reads the new ones | U31 (E1-S4) |
| examples `shape_moments`, `group_kmeans`, `crosstab-retention`, `attr_mahalanobis` returned the full response (with `components`) at full float precision; `t_test_one_sample` at full precision | as worked stories they set `return.preset` `"standard"` + `return.precision` 4 (`t_test_one_sample`: precision 4 only, so its confidence interval stays): `components` is absent and figures carry four significant digits; labels and figures are otherwise unchanged | example output change | a harness reading an example's `components` (the k-means centroids and between-SS ratio, the retention crosstab's cell components) drops the `return` block or sets `preset` `"full"` | U31 (E1-S5) |
| a Recommend draft of `ATTR_PC_SCORE`, `ATTR_FACTOR_SCORE`, `ATTR_SCALE_SCORE`, `ATTR_REG_PREDICT`, `FEAT_LOG`, `FEAT_SQRT` or `FEAT_FREQUENCY_ENCODE` carried no plan for the model that reads its column next | it carries `steps` (new public type `descriptor.RecommendStep` `{tool, request, placeholders, why}`, `Recommendation.Steps` additive `omitempty`): a `pulse_materialize` draft (the draft labelled, out `"<out>"`), then the `pulse_process` `REG_OLS` request reading the column there; every other draft is byte-identical, and no plan appears when the instance hides either tool or the model; `FEAT_POLY` gets none by design (its column set depends on the degree the caller picks) | additive field + new exported type | a strict decoder that refuses unknown keys adds `steps`; a caller rendering drafts may show the plan (`skills/tool-recommend.md`) | U31 (E2-S2, E2-S4) |
| examples `null_count_contrast` (`aggregations/11_null_count.json`) and `filter_null_present` (`filterers/06_null.json`) read the non-nullable `small_int` of the 5-row `all_types.pulse`: `small_int_present` 5 / `small_int_null` 0, `rows_present` 5 | they read the nullable `income` of the new fixture `intake.pulse` (300 forms, nulls planted per channel): `income_present` 225 / `income_null` 75 (labels renamed from `small_int_*`), `rows_present` 225 | example output change | a harness keyed on `small_int_present` / `small_int_null` reads `income_present` / `income_null` | U31 (E2-S3) |
| example `attr_mahalanobis` was the data_quality guide's worked story (`_meta.question` / `interpretation`, golden `testdata/stories/attr_mahalanobis.json`) | it is an ordinary example again (request and output unchanged); the new story `agg_missing_by_channel` (`aggregations/24_missing_by_channel.json`, null counts per intake channel on `intake.pulse`) is the data_quality story | example metadata change | a caller reading `examples.StoryGolden("attr_mahalanobis")` reads `agg_missing_by_channel` | U31 (E2-S3) |
| `pulse.Example` / `pulse.ExampleSummary` (`examples.Example` / `ExampleSummary`) carried no story metadata | both gain `Question` and `Interpretation` (`json:"question,omitempty"` / `"interpretation,omitempty"`), from `_meta.question` / `_meta.interpretation`; an example declaring both is a story | additive fields | a strict decoder that refuses unknown keys adds `question` / `interpretation`; a positional struct literal of either type adds the two fields | U31 (E1-S1) |
| an embedder example (`Extensions.Examples`) whose `_meta` held `question` / `interpretation` was refused as an unknown `_meta` key (`PULSE_EXTENSION_EXAMPLE_INVALID`, `details.reason` `"meta"`) | both keys are optional and accepted, served on search and get exactly as declared; the shipped library's story rules (both keys, a digit-free interpretation, `return.precision` 4) are NOT enforced on an embedder example — no `"story"` reason exists | relaxed validation (additive) | none; an embedder may declare either key, ideally both with a qualitative interpretation | U31 (E1-S1) |
| example `process-index-vs-total` (`overlays/06_process_index_vs_total.json`) declared `_meta.intents` `["benchmark"]` and described `OVERLAY_INDEX_VS_TOTAL` as an index | it declares `["composition"]`, and its description says what the figure is: each region's share of the grand total, ×100, so the regions add up to 100 (request and output unchanged) | example metadata change | an intent-filtered search (`intent: "benchmark"`) no longer returns it; `"composition"` does | U31 (E2-S3) |
| `pulse examples search` took `--query` / `--tag` / `--category` and searched `internal/examples` directly | it gains `--intent ID` and positional query words (`pulse examples search --intent compare_groups region`; the query passed both ways is `CLI_INPUT`) and routes through `Pulse.ExamplesSearchWith`, the path MCP `pulse_examples_search` already used: it gains the synonym tier (operator aliases, intent phrasings), drops examples a feature profile hides, and an unknown intent is `PULSE_RECOMMEND_INTENT_UNKNOWN` (exit 1) | CLI behaviour change | a script that saw an empty search may now get hits (synonym and fuzzy tiers); one parsing the old error placeholder reads the coded error | U31 (E2-S1) |
| example search (facade `ExamplesSearch` / `ExamplesSearchWith`, MCP `pulse_examples_search`, CLI) ended at the literal and synonym tiers: a misspelt word matched nothing | a final fuzzy tier runs only when no earlier tier hits: every content word must match a field word exactly or within its edit budget (optimal string alignment; one edit for 4-7 characters, two for 8+, words of 3 characters or fewer never fuzz), so `regon` finds the region examples | search behaviour change | a caller relying on an empty result for a misspelt query handles hits; exact and synonym results are unchanged | U31 (E2-S1) |

## Changes from U38 (skill sync)

Ships in the next alpha. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change; the only payload change is the additive regression `holdout` slot pair (E3-S1). Skill bodies, manifest declarations, embedder skill validation and two `GROUP_DATE` behaviour breaks (marked). Skill text is served content, not contract: an agent that quoted a skill body re-reads it; nothing below needs a code change unless a row says so.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GROUP_DATE` accepted every scalar field (only `vec_*` refused) and silently read a non-date value as epoch days / seconds — at predict and at runtime, on `Request.Groups`, crosstab rows / columns and a `MAT_DISTANCE` `params.between` | only a `date` or `datetime` schema field is accepted; any other scalar type is refused `PULSE_FIELD_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED` by predict and the runtime alike (a `vec_*` field keeps `PULSE_VECTOR_FIELD_NOT_SCALAR`; a derived column absent from the schema is not judged, as before). Smart defaults, examples and recommend already used date-family fields only | **behaviour break** (refusal) | group a numeric epoch column with `GROUP_RANGE` / `GROUP_ROUNDED`, or import it as `date` / `datetime` | U38 (E3-S4) |
| `GROUP_DATE` `period` `hour` / `hour_of_day` on a non-date scalar field refused `PROCESSING_CONFIG` (the sub-day rule) | refused `PULSE_FIELD_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED` — the type rule now runs first, in the same order at predict and at runtime; `hour` on a `date` or derived field stays `PROCESSING_CONFIG`. Predict also stops reporting the sub-day rule beside a vector field's `PULSE_VECTOR_FIELD_NOT_SCALAR` (one error, as the runtime) | **behaviour break** (error code) | a caller matching `PROCESSING_CONFIG` for this case matches `PULSE_FIELD_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED` | U38 (E3-S4) |
| atomic skill body budgets: `op-*` 1200, `tool-*` 2000, `type-*` 2000 bytes, HARD at `pulse.New` for an embedder skill (soft, 1000% slack, for the shipped pack) | one frozen tier table (`skills.BodyBudget`), hard for both: `op-test-*` 2200, `op-overlay-*` 2400, `op-reg-*` 3000, every other `op-*` 1800, `tool-*` 2500, `type-*` 2000; `ext-*` stays 6000. An embedder `op-agg-acme-trim` skill of 1,500 bytes, refused before, now loads; one byte over its tier is still `PULSE_EXTENSION_SKILL_INVALID` (`details.reason` `"budget"`, `bytes`, `budget`) | relaxed validation (loosening) | none; an embedder skill that loaded before still loads | U38 (E3-S3) |
| an embedder op skill's `<!-- generated: contract -->` line was an unknown marker, served verbatim and counted toward the budget | it renders `## Contract` from the registration (`Accepts`, `WeightAware`, `PValues`, `Streamable` / `Mergeable`, `ComponentSchema` keys; profile-aware) and is measured out of the budget. Optional, never auto-injected; a skill without it is byte-identical | additive (opt-in marker) | an embedder may add the marker just before `## Gotchas` and drop hand-written type / weight / mergeability / component-key boilerplate | U38 (E2-S1) |
| `## Components` was a required section of every `AGG` / `GROUP` / `FILTER` / `MAT` atomic skill (built-in and embedder; a missing one refused `PULSE_EXTENSION_SKILL_INVALID`, reason `"sections"`) | optional: the generated contract lists the keys; keep the heading only for per-key meaning | relaxed validation (loosening) | none | U38 (E2-S2) |
| manifest `WIN_LAG` / `WIN_LEAD`: `offset` "≥ 1"; `emits_type: "f64"` | `offset` "≥ 0; 0 reads the current row itself" (what predict and the runtime accepted); no `emits_type` — the `emits_type_note` says the output is a verbatim copy of the source cell (`params.default` past the partition edge) | manifest declaration correction | a harness that typed the column `f64` from the manifest reads the source field's type | U38 (E1-S1) |
| manifest `GROUP_RANGE` / `GROUP_ROUNDED` `interval`: `required: true` | `required: false`, `default: 1`, "≤ 0 is treated as 1" (`GROUP_RANGE` also names the smart default 10). Runtime unchanged; Recommend drafts still ask for `<interval>` | manifest declaration correction | a validator that demanded `interval` may drop the demand | U38 (E1-S1) |
| manifest `ATTR_REG_LEVERAGE` output range [0, 1] | [1/n, 1] within the fit; a row outside the fit (null target, predictors present) can exceed 1 | manifest declaration correction | none | U38 (E1-S1) |
| manifest `t_critical` component key (`AGG_CI_*`) described as a scaled critical value | "Normal critical value z = qnorm(1 − α/2) … (not Student t)" — the figure was always the normal z | manifest declaration correction | none | U38 (E1-S1) |
| manifest `accepts_types` of `AGG_VEC_MEAN` / `_SUM` / `_SD`, `GROUP_KMEANS`, `ATTR_MAHALANOBIS`, `ATTR_PC_SCORE`, `ATTR_FACTOR_SCORE`, `ATTR_SCALE_SCORE`, `ATTR_PROFILE_SIMILARITY`, `ATTR_VEC_DISTANCE` listed `date` and omitted `packed_bool` | the vector member rule the runtime applies (`f32`, `f64`, `packed_bool`, `u16`, `u32`, `u4`, `u64`, `u8` — the `MAT_*` set): `date` dropped (it was always refused `PULSE_VECTOR_MEMBER_TYPE`), `packed_bool` added | manifest declaration correction | none | U38 (E2-S3) |
| manifest `ATTR_REG_PREDICT` `accepts_types` omitted `datetime`, `decimal128`, `packed_bool` | the scoring-frame rule the runtime applies (`IsNumericForAnalytics`, else `PULSE_MODEL_PREDICTOR_NOT_NUMERIC`): the three are added | manifest declaration correction | none | U38 (E2-S3) |
| `Pulse.Ontology()`: a topical (`kind: design`) skill's `## See` section emitted no edges | it emits `skill:<design> routes_to skill:*` per backticked stem and `skill:<design> exemplified_by example:*` per `tags=[…]` list (quotes trimmed, `a\|b` alternatives, several lists per line), exactly like an atomic skill; an embedder `ext-*` skill likewise. Edges from a skill never prune a node: no profile visibility moves | additive (ontology edges) | a caller walking the graph sees more edges from design skills | U38 (E1-S3) |
| on a feature-profiled instance a topical skill body (`Skill`, `pulse_skills_get`, `pulse-skill://`) still named hidden skills and example searches in its `## See` | it renders `## See` by edge like an atomic skill: a hidden stem and a `tags=[…]` search no visible example answers are cut, an emptied line goes, and a topical section left blank goes with its heading (an atomic skill keeps its required heading). Profile-free output is byte-identical | behaviour change (profiled skill text) | none | U38 (E1-S4) |
| MCP `pulse_examples_search` / `pulse_examples_get` descriptions said "embedded library"; CLI `pulse skills` / `pulse examples` help did not say what they list | the MCP descriptions name the server's own library (shipped + embedder, minus hidden features); the CLI help says built-in only (no behaviour change) | description text | none | U38 (E1-S2) |

## Changes from U37 (shell completion)

Ships in the next alpha. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change and no payload shape change. Every closed vocabulary a CLI flag takes now has one exported library lister (a fresh slice, deterministic order, default first); the CLI validates and documents each flag from it, and shell completion offers its values. All additive.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the float-width, DST-policy and SPSS user-missing spellings were constants or literals only (`io.DSTPolicy*`, `io.SPSSMissing*`; the float widths unexported) | `io.FloatWidths()` (`f64`, `f32`), `io.DSTPolicies()` (`error`, `earlier`, `later`), `io.ParseDSTPolicy(s)` (empty = `DSTPolicyError`; any other spelling `SERVICE_VALIDATION`, details `{option: "dst_policy", value}` — the import's own refusal), `io.SPSSMissingModes()` (`auto`, `null`) | additive (functions) | an embedder validating or offering these values reads the listers instead of hard-coding them | U37 (E2-S1) |
| `types.LookupMultiplicity*` and `types.LabelMode*` were constants only; the CLI translated `--mode` / `--labels` mode spellings itself | `types.AllLookupMultiplicities()` (`assert_unique`, `first`, `all`), `types.ParseLookupMultiplicity(s)` (also accepts the `assert-unique` alias; empty is not a spelling), `types.AllLabelModes()` (`replace`, `augment`), `types.ParseLabelMode(s)` (empty = `replace`) | additive (functions) | none | U37 (E2-S1) |
| `descriptor.Level*`, `descriptor.ExplainTerse` / `ExplainFull` and `descriptor.ExplainRoot*` were constants only | `descriptor.Levels()` (`basic`, `intermediate`, `advanced`), `descriptor.ExplainDetails()` (`terse`, `full`), `descriptor.ExplainRequestRoots()` (the request-mode roots `request`, `composed`, `chain`, `facet`, `sample` — never a response-mode spelling) | additive (functions) | none | U37 (E2-S1) |
| the rake missing policies, the limit names and the `WidenSetField` target names were reachable only through internal packages or prose | `pulse.RakeMissingRefuse` / `pulse.RakeMissingBase` + `pulse.RakeMissingPolicies()`, `pulse.LimitNames()` (snake_case, `Limits` field order), `pulse.WidenSetFieldTargets()` (`set_u16` … `set_u256`) | additive (constants + functions) | none | U37 (E2-S1) |
| CLI help lines of the enumerated flags were hand-written | built from the listers above (wording changed, every value still named); `pulse api lookup --mode` refusal reads `want assert_unique\|first\|all (or assert-unique)` — still uncoded, every previously accepted spelling still accepted | CLI help / message text | a script matching the old help or `--mode` message text re-reads it | U37 (E2-S1) |

## Changes from U57 (transformed predictors)

Ships in the next alpha. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change and no payload shape change.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a regression slot naming a window label of its own request (`target`, `predictors`, `unpenalized`, `covariance` `cluster` / `order_by`, `jackknife_by`) was refused `SERVICE_VALIDATION` "regression references unknown … field" | admitted when the windows run over the record rows (no groups, aggregations or crosstab): the engine copies the label onto each record before the fit (as the materialize route stores it — a null cell drops listwise; a non-numeric window value is `PROCESSING_CONFIG`). In a grouped / aggregated / crosstab shape the name is the NEW code `PULSE_REGRESSION_WINDOW_UNAVAILABLE` `{regression, field, reason: "grouped_shape"}`; a slot `weight` naming a window label stays `SERVICE_VALIDATION` with a message naming the window output | behaviour change (refusal lifted) + new error code | none; a materialize-then-regress pipeline may collapse into one request. Match `PULSE_REGRESSION_WINDOW_UNAVAILABLE` where a grouped request used to fail generically | U57 (E1-S1) |
| an attribute reading a window label of its own request (`hill(tv_ad, k, s)` over a `WIN_ADSTOCK`) was refused as an unknown field / formula name: attributes ran before windows | a row-local `ATTR_FORMULA` (no `total()`) or `ATTR_REG_PREDICT` reading a window label — directly or through an earlier such attribute's label — runs AFTER the windows over the record rows, in declared order; its label rides the response row and is readable by a regression, a sort key and a post-test, and `ATTR_REG_PREDICT` scores a bound model whose terms are window labels when the slot declares those windows. Any other attribute reading one (a non-row-local type, a `total()` formula, a `params.fit` regression attribute, an extension attribute, or any attribute outside the record-row shape) is the NEW code `PULSE_ATTRIBUTE_WINDOW_ORDER` `{attribute, field}`; a request whose attributes read no window label runs byte-identically | behaviour change (refusal lifted) + new error code | none; drop the materialize step of an adstock → saturation chain. Match `PULSE_ATTRIBUTE_WINDOW_ORDER` where such a request used to fail as an unknown name | U57 (E2-S1) |
| a NaN or ±Inf regression input — a schema cell, a feature / attribute output or a window value (a `WIN_LAG` carrying a NaN forward) in the target, a predictor, a robust `covariance` `cluster` / `order_by` or `jackknife_by` — stayed in the fit (only a null dropped listwise): `n_obs` counted the row and the slot failed (typically `PROCESSING_REGRESSION_RANK_DEFICIENT` / `_NO_CONVERGE` via `PULSE_SLOT_FAILED`), or a GLM / bootstrap carried NaN figures | a non-finite input is missing data: the row drops listwise exactly like a null (not in `n_obs`, `sum_weights` or a resample's rows; a bootstrap / jackknife seed hashes it as a null) on every built-in `REG_*`, `ATTR_REG_*` refit and selection refit, and on the engine's admission of an extension regression's rows. Scoring (`ATTR_REG_PREDICT`, `ATTR_REG_FITTED` / `_RESIDUAL`, compose `fits` scorers, an extension `RowScorer`) reads a row with a non-finite column as a null score, as for a null column. A non-finite WEIGHT is unchanged (already invalid, `n_weight_invalid`) | behaviour change (refusal lifted, figure) | none; a fit that failed on a stray NaN / Inf now returns figures over the finite rows — compare `n_obs` with the record count to see the drop | U57 (E1-S3) |
| `types.RegressionSpec` / `types.RegressionResult` had no out-of-sample slot: a holdout score took a filter-split fit plus `ATTR_REG_PREDICT` and `AGG_RMSE` / `AGG_MAE` | NEW `types.RegressionSpec.Holdout string` (`holdout,omitempty`: an expr-lang boolean predicate over the built record — schema, features, attributes, windows, post-window attributes; `true` rows are held out of the fit and scored after it, `null` rows join neither) and NEW `types.RegressionResult.Holdout *types.RegressionHoldout` (`holdout,omitempty`; NEW struct `types.RegressionHoldout {N, NNull, RMSE, MAE, R2}` → `{n, n_null, rmse, mae, r2}`, response scale, slot-weighted, `r2` `null` when undefined). `types.RegressionSpec.StreamableAs` / `Streamable` now report false for a spec with a `Holdout` (it forces the buffered path). A non-boolean / unknown-name predicate is `PULSE_FORMULA_COMPILE_ERROR`, an unscorable type `PULSE_MODEL_SCORE_INVALID` `no_scorer`, no scorable held-out row a per-slot `PROCESSING_REGRESSION_INSUFFICIENT_DATA` (`reason: "holdout_empty"`). NEW feature `capability:regression_holdout` (hard edge any-of `REG_*`) gates both slots; an explicit feature profile must list it to use `holdout`. The `standard` and `minimal` `return` presets keep `regressions[*].holdout`, so their `returned.digest` (`rp1:`) changes | additive (new fields + struct + capability) + digest change | none for an unset `holdout` (byte-identical). A profile allowlist that wants the slot adds `capability:regression_holdout`; a code switching on `Streamable()` sees false for a holdout spec; a cache keyed on a `standard` / `minimal` `returned.digest` re-keys once | U57 (E3-S1) |
| `Explain`'s `steps` listed every attribute before the windows, and a predict `streamable_reasons` entry for windows read "windows run over the post-aggregate row set" | an attribute that reads a window label (the post-window phase) is described AFTER the windows, its step text naming the window value it waits for, and a regression step names the window labels it reads; the windows reason reads "windows run over the whole row set (every filtered record, or the post-aggregate rows when grouped)" | text / ordering change in `ExplainResult.Steps` and a predict reason string | none; a consumer indexing `steps` by position, or matching the old reason text, should match on `slot` / the new wording | U57 (E3-S3, E4-S1) |

## Changes from U58 (expression maths, lags and params)

Shipped in `v1.0.0-alpha.26`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change; one additive payload slot, `params`, on `Request`, `ComposedRequest` and `ChainRequest`.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| an embedder `ExprFunctions` entry could be named `sin`, `cos`, `tan`, `atan2`, `pi` or `e`, and an expression had no trigonometry | `sin(x)`, `cos(x)`, `tan(x)` (radians), `atan2(y, x)` and the zero-argument constants `pi()` / `e()` (CALLS, not bare identifiers) are reserved built-ins in ATTR_FORMULA, FILTER_EXPRESSION, `total()` arguments, a regression `holdout` and predict's compile check — NOT in `OVERLAY_FORMULA` (`PULSE_OVERLAY_FORMULA_INVALID_IDENT`). A null argument answers null, ±Inf / NaN answer NaN (`null` on the wire); a wrong argument count is `PROCESSING_CONFIG` at predict and run. An embedder function with one of the six names is refused `PULSE_EXTENSION_NAME_RESERVED` at `pulse.New` | behaviour change (registration refused) + additive (functions) | rename the embedder function (e.g. `acme_sin`). A cohort field named like one keeps binding: bare `pi` / `e` / `sin` reads the field, and calling that name (`pi()`, `sin(x)`) fails compile (`PULSE_FORMULA_COMPILE_ERROR`) in that request, so rename the field to reach the built-in. A bare `pi` with no such field is a function, not a number: write `pi()` | U58 (E1-S2) |
| the expression built-ins `exp`, `log`, `log1p`, `pow` (and `hill`'s power), the `**` / `^` operator and the `FEAT_LOG` operator ran on the Go standard library's `math.*`, whose bits depend on the machine: amd64 `math.Exp` picks an FMA path per CPU, `math.Log` is assembly on amd64 and pure Go on arm64, and arm64 contracts the pure-Go kernels' multiply-adds | every one (and the new trig set) runs on Pulse's portable `internal/detmath` kernels, so those calls and `FEAT_LOG` give identical bits on every architecture and CPU, and `FEAT_LOG` equals the formula `log1p(x)` bit for bit. The `**` / `^` operator now also moves onto `detmath`: Pulse rewrites it into the same power built-in, so `x ** y` is bit-equal to `pow(x, y)`; its result type (always float, `2 ** 3` too), compile refusals and null behaviour are unchanged (an unguarded null operand still raises `invalid operation: float(<nil>)`, and a filter still reads that row as UNKNOWN). Ordinary results may move in the last bits on any machine: within 1 ulp for `log` / `log1p`, 2 ulp for `exp`, 8 ulp for `pow` (it compounds exp ∘ log). Three standard-library defects are also fixed, so these inputs change by far more than an ulp: on amd64 `math.Log` of a subnormal (`log(5e-324)` was about -709.09, now the correct -744.44); on amd64 `math.Exp` overflowed to `+Inf` from about x = 709.44 (now finite up to 709.78, the true limit); on arm64 `math.Exp(-745.1)` underflowed to 0 (now the smallest subnormal, 5e-324) | figure change (last bits; three edge corrections) | none for ordinary data. A golden, cache or fixture that pins exact `exp` / `log` / `pow` / `**` / `hill` / `FEAT_LOG` bits regenerates once; compare against a tolerance (or against Pulse itself) rather than against `math.*`. A pipeline that relied on `log` of a subnormal or `exp` near ±709 sees the corrected value | U58 (E1-S2) |
| a synth `constraints[]` entry, rule `when` or `set_expr` could call expr-lang's stock `now()` (the wall clock), and had no `sqrt` / `exp` / `log` / `log1p` / `pow` / `hill`, no trigonometry, and no float `%` (`x % 2` on a synth field did not compile) | `now()` is removed from the synth row environment and refused at spec validation with the new code `PULSE_SYNTH_EXPR_NONDETERMINISTIC` (`details.function`, `details.expr`; rule faults also carry `rule_index` / `slot`). The row environment gains the formula attribute's maths — `sqrt`, `exp`, `log`, `log1p`, `pow(x, y)`, `hill(x, k, s)`, `sin`, `cos`, `tan`, `atan2(y, x)`, `pi()`, `e()` — and its float `%`, all on the portable `detmath` kernels; a wrong argument count or a string argument is refused at spec parse (`PULSE_SYNTH_RULE_EXPR_INVALID`, `SERVICE_VALIDATION` for a constraint). The `**` / `^` operator in a synth expression now runs on `detmath.Pow`, so a spec that used it may move in the last bits (and, through rounding into an integer field or a later `when` comparison, in a stored value). A field named like a built-in (or like `row` / `lag`) keeps reading as a bare name; CALLING that name, or naming a built-in bare with no such field (`pi` for `pi()`), is refused | behaviour change (refusal) + additive (functions) + figure change (`**` last bits) | replace `now()` with a literal or a drawn `date` / `datetime` field; rename a field to call the built-in it shadows; regenerate any pinned output of a spec using `**` / `^` | U58 (E2-S1) |
| `profile create --fit-shape` evaluated its mixture fit with `math.Exp` / `math.Log`, so a `shape` section could differ in the last bits between machines | the fit runs on `detmath`, identical on every architecture and CPU; a profile document's `shape` section may move in its last bits once (unchanged on the in-repo fixture on arm64) | figure change (last bits) | none; re-capture a committed profile document if you diff it bit for bit | U58 (E2-S1) |
| a synth `monotonic_from` field advanced on every generation ATTEMPT, so when a `constraints[]` entry rejected a row the column skipped a value (one gap per rejected attempt), and a synth expression had no row ordinal | `monotonic_from` commits its step only after a row is ACCEPTED, so the column is `start + i*step` with no gaps. Output bytes change for any spec that combines `monotonic_from` with a constraint that rejects at least one attempt — only that column moves; a spec that never rejects writes identical bytes. The row environment gains `row()`, the 0-based ACCEPTED-row ordinal (a rejected attempt redraws at the same ordinal; it consumes no RNG), in `set_expr`, `when` and `constraints[]`; under `AugmentFromProfile` both count generated rows from 0 | behaviour change (figure change in `monotonic_from` under rejection) + additive (`row()`) | regenerate any pinned output of a spec combining `monotonic_from` with a rejecting constraint; drop any gap-tolerant post-processing of its IDs. A field named `row` keeps reading as a bare name; calling `row()` then is refused | U58 (E2-S2) |
| a synth expression could read only the current row's fields | the row environment gains `lag("field", n[, default])`, the post-rule value of the declared field (a string literal; self-lag works) `n` ACCEPTED rows back (`n` an integer literal 1..1024; before the first row `default`, else null; a lagged null stays null), so a recursive adstock is a `set_expr`: `spend + 0.6 * lag("true_adstock", 1, 0)`. NEW codes `PULSE_SYNTH_LAG_FIELD_INVALID` (not a string literal naming a declared field) and `PULSE_SYNTH_LAG_OFFSET_INVALID` (offset not an integer literal in range); a field named `lag` keeps reading as a bare name and calling `lag()` then is refused. Profile capture, the fidelity report and `--suggest-rules` know nothing of `lag` | additive (function + codes) | none; see skill `synth-cross-row` | U58 (E2-S3) |
| synth GENERATION evaluated its transcendentals with the standard library: the lognormal draw `math.Exp`, the pareto draw `math.Pow`, the poisson threshold `math.Exp`, and the copula's Φ / Φ⁻¹ (`math.Erf` / `math.Erfinv`) and lognormal / exponential marginal transforms (`math.Exp` / `math.Log` / `math.Expm1`) — every correlated, modelled (`Spec.Models`) and vector field passes through Φ — so the same spec and seed could write different last bits on different machines | every one runs on the portable `detmath` kernels (new `Expm1`, `Erf`, `Erfinv` ports), identical on every architecture and CPU. Output bytes of a spec drawing a `lognormal`, `pareto` or `poisson` field, or using `correlations`, `models`, residual correlations or vector fields (whatever their marginals), may change ONCE on a given machine (measured on arm64: lognormal, pareto and copula specs moved, poisson did not); a spec of only `uniform` / `normal` / `exponential` / categorical / boolean / set / `monotonic_from` fields is unchanged. The standard normal / exponential variate itself followed in E2-S3 (next row) | figure change (last bits; a rounded or clamped value can move further) | regenerate any pinned bytes of such a spec once | U58 (E2-S1) |
| synth's standard normal and exponential variates came from `math/rand/v2`'s ziggurat (`(*rand.Rand).NormFloat64` / `ExpFloat64`), consumed by every `normal`, `lognormal`, `exponential`, `mixture`, copula, model, residual and vector draw; its rare tail branch calls `math.Log` and arm64 fuses its `rn + x`, so a tail draw's last bits — and, through a constraint or rule deciding on that value, the rows after it — could differ between machines | they run on a portable port of the same ziggurat that pulls the seeded RNG exactly as the stdlib does (same calls, order and count per draw), so seeding and the stream are unchanged and only a rare extreme (tail) draw can move, in its last bits. Synth GENERATION is now byte-identical per `(spec, seed)` on every architecture and CPU. Measured on arm64 at 20k–50k rows: `normal`, `exponential` and copula specs unchanged; one `lognormal` row moved 3 ulps, a vector spec 1–3 rows, a `models` augment one byte; under linux/amd64 (docker) the stdlib tail matched the port on every measured draw. The one remaining machine-sensitive surface is CAPTURE under `profile create --fit-models`: its regression engine is not yet fusion-free, so a captured `models` coefficient can differ in its last bits | figure change (last bits of rare tail draws; through a rounded value, a constraint or a rule, possibly more) | regenerate any pinned bytes of a spec drawing these variates once; synthetic output can now be compared bit for bit across machines (a `--fit-models` profile document still cannot) | U58 (E2-S3) |
| a request had no way to hand an expression a value: a `{{name}}` template variable was spliced into the formula TEXT | NEW `types.Request.Params types.Params` (`params,omitempty`; NEW type `types.Params map[string]any` whose `UnmarshalJSON` keeps every number as a `json.Number`) — named constants every request expression (ATTR_FORMULA, FILTER_EXPRESSION, a `total()` argument, a regression `holdout`, OVERLAY_FORMULA) reads, read-only, as `params.<name>`; folded as typed constants at compile (an integer binds as `int64` / `uint64`, so an id beyond 2^53 compares exactly; a one-kind list serves `x in params.list`). NEW codes `PULSE_PARAMS_INVALID` (null value, non-identifier name, mixed list, other value; `details.reason`), `PULSE_PARAMS_UNDECLARED` (`details.param`, `declared`), `PULSE_PARAMS_FIELD_COLLISION`. NEW feature `capability:params` (hard edge any-of ATTR_FORMULA, FILTER_EXPRESSION, OVERLAY_FORMULA, `capability:regression_holdout`) gates the slot; an explicit feature profile must list it. Compose / chain inheritance: next row | additive (field + type + codes + capability) | none for a request without `params` (byte-identical, `Request.Hash` included). Replace a spliced `"wave == {{w}}"` with `"wave == params.w"` and `"params": {"w": {"$var": "w"}}`. A profile allowlist that wants the slot adds `capability:params` | U58 (E3-S1) |
| an expression reading `params.<name>` failed compile as an unknown name (`PULSE_FORMULA_COMPILE_ERROR` "unknown name params"; OVERLAY_FORMULA `PULSE_OVERLAY_FORMULA_PARSE_ERROR`), and over a cohort with a field named `params` failed as a member access on that field's value | the read is a parameter read: undeclared on the request it is `PULSE_PARAMS_UNDECLARED` (predict and run alike, before any record); over a request where `params` is a column (schema field, feature output, attribute label, vector) it is `PULSE_PARAMS_FIELD_COLLISION`. A bare `params` (and `$env["params"]`) still reads the column unchanged | behaviour change (refusal code) | match the new codes where such a request used to fail as a compile error; a cohort field named `params` stays readable bare — rename it to use request parameters there | U58 (E3-S1) |
| a `u64` FIELD bound into every request expression (ATTR_FORMULA, FILTER_EXPRESSION, a `total()` argument, a regression `holdout`, predict's compile check) as its `float64` echo, so `id == params.k`, `id == 9007199254740993`, `id < other_id` and `id in [...]` were exact only up to 2^53 (2^53 and 2^53+1 compared equal) | the field binds as its stored word and every comparison (`==` `!=` `<` `<=` `>` `>=`, `in` over a list) against an integer param or literal, a float, or another `u64` field is exact over the full 0..2^64−1 range, on every arm (`DecodeWorkers` 1 ≡ N, the grouped filter precompute, the null-row path). Everything else reads the echo exactly as before — arithmetic (`id + 1`, `id * 2.5`, `id % 2` stay float64, bit for bit, at any magnitude), the maths built-ins, result types, compile errors and nil rules. A comparison now differs from before only where its operands differ past 2^53 (e.g. `id == 9007199254740993` is false for id = 2^53, which the rounded echo called true). An expression reading `$env`, and a `u64` column a feature or attribute rewrote, keep the echo. A `params.<name>` read no longer keeps a filter off the grouped filter precompute (speed only) | figure change (comparisons past 2^53) | none for ids below 2^53; a request that relied on 2^53-rounded equality of large ids now gets the exact verdict | U58 (E3-S1) |
| a ComposedRequest / ChainRequest had no params of its own: each slot, fit request or stage repeated the block; a Compose-level OVERLAY_FORMULA reading `params.<name>` passed `PredictCompose` and failed at run as `PULSE_OVERLAY_FORMULA_PARSE_ERROR` | NEW `types.ComposedRequest.Params` and `types.ChainRequest.Params` (`params,omitempty`, `types.Params`, gated by `capability:params`): merged during normalization into every Compose slot (sweep- and banner-expanded ones included), every Compose fit request and every chain stage, a key the sub-request declares itself winning; validated once first (`PULSE_PARAMS_INVALID` at the root, even when every sub-request overrides the name). `--echo-request` / `NormalizedRequest` and `PredictCompose` / `PredictChain` show the merged params on each sub-request; the caller's sub-requests are never written. A Compose-level OVERLAY_FORMULA reads the batch's top-level params (folded as constants); an undeclared read there is `PULSE_PARAMS_UNDECLARED` at predict and at run alike | additive (fields) + behaviour change (Compose OVERLAY_FORMULA refusal code) | none without top-level `params` (byte-identical, `Hash` included). Hoist a block repeated on every slot or stage to the root; match `PULSE_PARAMS_UNDECLARED` where a Compose formula used to fail as a parse error | U58 (E3-S2) |
| a request template could interpolate `{{name}}` into an expression string — a filterer's or attribute's `expression` (a `total()` argument included), a regression `holdout`, an overlay's `params.formula` — splicing the variable's raw text into the expression source (no quoting, so a value could rewrite the expression's logic), although the docs called such a string "inert text" | a `{{token}}` inside any expression-bearing string slot, at any depth of any target (Compose slots and overlays, sweep requests, chain stages, facet filterers, an attribute's params-borne `fit_on` / centroid `filter`), is refused at declaration with the NEW code `PULSE_TEMPLATE_EXPR_SPLICE` (`details.template`, `variable`, `path`, `slot`) — an author error, so the template fails `pulse.New` (or is served last-good and marked `Broken` after a hot edit). Still legal there: a whole-value `{"$var": "f"}` marker supplying the entire expression and the `{{{{` escape (a `for_each` / `sweep` axis token is refused too: next row). `{{token}}` everywhere else (labels, field names, a `params` entry) is unchanged | behaviour change (template refused) + additive (code) | move the value into the request's params: `"params": {"w": {"$var": "w"}}` (the marker keeps the value's JSON type) and read it as `params.w` — `"wave == {{w}}"` becomes `"wave == params.w"`; or supply the whole expression through a `{"$var": …}` marker | U58 (E4-S1) |
| a Compose `sweep` or a window / feature / attribute `for_each` spliced each axis value's text into every `{{axis}}` token, an expression string included (`"expression": "week * {{k}}"`, a `holdout`, an overlay `params.formula`), so an axis value — possibly from a template `list` variable — could rewrite the expression's logic; a template could carry such an axis token through to the expansion; FEAT_TARGET_ENCODE's `params.fit_on` row expression was not treated as an expression slot at all | an `{{axis}}` inside any expression-bearing string slot (the `internal/exprslots` list, now including a feature's `params.fit_on`) is refused before any substitution, identically by predict (`Predict`, `PredictCompose`, `PredictChain`) and the runtime: NEW code `PULSE_SWEEP_EXPR_SPLICE` for a sweep's request or overlays, NEW code `PULSE_FOR_EACH_EXPR_SPLICE` for a for_each item (every request host: process, compose slot, chain stage, materialize); details `field`, `placeholder`, `slot`. Before this a sweep or for_each axis token in an expression was refused only as `PULSE_SWEEP_INVALID` / `PULSE_FOR_EACH_INVALID` reason `placeholder_unknown` ("names no axis"). In a request template the same axis token, and a `{{variable}}` in a feature's `params.fit_on`, is now `PULSE_TEMPLATE_EXPR_SPLICE` at load (`details.variable` = the axis name). Axis tokens in labels, fields and other strings, `{"$var": "axis"}` markers on params, and the `{{{{` escape are unchanged | behaviour change (refusal code; template refused) + additive (codes) | for a sweep, carry the value through the slot request's params — `"params": {"w": {"$var": "w"}}` and `params.w` in the expression — or let the axis values be whole expressions supplied by a `{"$var": "w"}` marker. For a for_each over an attribute formula, run the value as a Compose sweep reading `params.<name>` instead (an item's `expression` is a typed string, so no marker can stand there); a params-borne expression (a `fit_on`) may take a whole-expression `{"$var": …}` axis marker. Match the new codes where a client matched `placeholder_unknown` | U58 (E4-S1) |

## Changes from U59 (SEM extensions)

Shipped in `v1.0.0-alpha.26`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change. M1 (mean structure, multi-group, invariance), M3 (`:=` defined parameters, the bootstrap, the interval level), M2 (`ATTR_SEM_SCORE`, the `sem` fit kind, saved SEM fit files) and M4 (the ordinal DWLS / WLSMV estimator and EBM scores) are below. A `MAT_SEM` slot that uses none of the new features (a single-group covariance-only model) is byte-identical: semopy's conventions, figures and columns are unchanged.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MAT_SEM` modelled the covariance only; a `~ 1` model line was `bad_params` | NEW `params.meanstructure` (bool) and `~ 1` lines (`x ~ 1` free, `x ~ 0*1` fixed, `x ~ a*1` labelled; `f ~ 1` a latent mean). Such a fit adds `"x ~1"` estimate rows, `auxiliary.implied_means` / `observed_means` (one `mean` column per member) and follows lavaan's conventions (next row) | additive (param + rows + auxiliaries) | none; set `meanstructure` to model means | U59 (E1-S1) |
| — | a fit using `meanstructure`, a `~ 1` line, `group`, a `:=` line, `resample` or an ordinal `estimator` follows lavaan 0.7, not semopy: `solver` defaults to `fisher`; variances are unbounded; `chi2`, `dof` (means counted), baseline, `cfi`, `tli`, `rmsea` are lavaan's; `gfi` / `agfi` are lavaan's `gfi_lisrel` / `agfi_lisrel`; `objective` is 2·F, so `chi2` = N × `objective`; `loglik` is the log-likelihood and `aic` / `bic` derive from it; `nfi` is NaN (`null` on the wire) when `dof` exceeds `dof_baseline` and 1 on a saturated model; a `~~` row between two variables has `std_estimate` = their residual correlation (lavaan `std.all`); the first indicator of every factor is the marker (a label on it keeps it fixed at 1); the estimates carry 95% Wald `ci_lower` / `ci_upper` columns (`null` on a fixed row) | convention (new-feature fits only) | compare fits on ONE convention: set `meanstructure` on every slot of a comparison, since the same covariance model reports a different `chi2` on the two paths. Read `ci_lower` / `ci_upper` by column name, never by position | U59 (E1-S1, E1-S3) |
| `NA*x` in a model parsed as a parameter LABEL named `NA` (every `NA*` term shared one value) | `NA*x` frees the parameter, unlabelled (lavaan's `NA`); on a first indicator it removes the marker | behaviour change (parser) | rename a parameter label spelled `NA` (e.g. `na1*x`); write `NA*x` to free a marker loading | U59 (E1-S3) |
| — | NEW `params.group` (a categorical, `packed_bool` or `u4` / `u8` field) fits ONE joint multi-group model (configural; a labelled parameter is one value in every group), always with a mean structure; `params.group_reference` names the level whose implied covariance is `primary` (default the first level) and which identifies the latent means (they stay 0 there). Estimate row keys become `"[level] lval op rval"` with aligned NEW `vectors.group` / `vectors.label` (`records` rows add `group`, `label`); per-level auxiliaries `implied_covariance.<level>`, `residual_covariance.<level>`, `implied_means.<level>`, `observed_means.<level>`; joint scalars plus `chi2_group_<level>` and `n_group_<level>`. Refused at predict and run (`SERVICE_VALIDATION`): request `groups` with `group`, another field type, a one-entry dictionary, a member as the group, a standardized vector, `params.fit`; fewer than two levels with rows, a level no larger than the observed-variable count or an empty reference level fail the slot alone (`PULSE_MATRIX_INSUFFICIENT_N`) | additive (params + shape) | none; key a grouped result's rows by `vectors.group` / `vectors.label` rather than parsing the `[level]` prefix | U59 (E1-S2) |
| — | NEW `params.group_equal` (lavaan `group.equal` classes `loadings`, `intercepts`, `means`, `residuals`, `residual.covariances`, `lv.variances`, `lv.covariances`, `regressions`) and `params.group_partial` (rows spelled `f=~x2`, `x1~1`, `x1~~x2`); each accepts a list OR one comma-separated string (`""` = configural, so a compose sweep axis can carry it). Equal intercepts without `means` free the latent means outside `group_reference`. lavaan per-group modifiers `c(a1,a2)*x` / `c(0.5,NA)*x` bind to the LEVEL INDEX (dictionary order; `packed_bool` 0, 1; a level without rows keeps its position) and are refused on an integer group field; `thresholds`, an unknown class, a `group_partial` row the model lacks or a modifier whose length is not the level count is `bad_params` (predict alike) | additive (params + syntax) | none; write the invariance ladder as one slot per step or a compose sweep over `group_equal` (`internal/examples/compose/09_sem_invariance_sweep.json`) | U59 (E1-S3) |
| — | NEW features `capability:sem_meanstructure` (→ `MAT_SEM`) and `capability:sem_groups` (→ `MAT_SEM` and `capability:sem_meanstructure`), `Since` 1.0.0. Hidden, their params keys are `PULSE_REQUEST_UNKNOWN_FIELD`, `~ 1` lines and `c(...)` modifiers are `bad_params`, and the manifest drops their params and output keys | additive (features) | an explicit feature-profile allowlist that wants the surfaces lists both capabilities | U59 (E1-S4) |
| `descriptor.MatrixPredict` had no group-level fields | NEW `descriptor.MatrixPredict.GroupLevels []string` (`group_levels,omitempty`) and `GroupLevelsBasis string` (`group_levels_basis,omitempty`: `dictionary`, `boolean` or `unknown`) — an UPPER bound (a level without rows after the filters is no group); on a `u4` / `u8` field the basis is `unknown`, the list is absent and the `matrices` size entry is left out. `estimated_bytes` counts one co-moment state per level (the integer range when unknown). The manifest declares per-level output keys as `<level>` patterns. The public API golden changed | additive (fields) | none; size a grouped SEM result from `group_levels` as a ceiling, not an exact count | U59 (E1-S4) |
| — | NEW `:=` model lines (lavaan defined parameters: labels, numeric constants, `+ - * / ^`, parentheses, `sqrt` / `exp` / `log` / `abs`, earlier `:=` names). Each adds an estimates row `"name := expr"` after the parameter table with a delta-method `std_err`, `z_value`, `p_value` and Wald `ci_lower` / `ci_upper`; a `:=` line switches a covariance-only fit to lavaan conventions. Refusals: `defined_invalid` (`details.problem` `unknown_label`, `forward_reference`, `duplicate`, `syntax`) and `constraint_unsupported` (lavaan `==` / `<` / `>` lines). `abs()` is differentiated by its sign, so its SE differs from lavaan's (whose complex step reads 0) | additive (model syntax + rows) | read `:=` rows by key; write equalities as shared labels | U59 (E2-S1) |
| grouped `MAT_SEM` `vectors.group` was `[]string` (every row's level) | `vectors.group` is `[]any`: a level string on every per-group row, `null` on a `:=` row (it belongs to no group); `vectors.label` is the `:=` row's name | behaviour change (Go type of a `vectors` entry) | type-switch the entry (`[]any`), treat `nil` as "no group"; JSON readers see `null` on `:=` rows only | U59 (E2-S1) |
| — | NEW warning code `PULSE_MATRIX_DEFINED_NONFINITE` (a `:=` value not finite at the estimate: the row and its dependents are `null`), with fixups and an owner | additive (error code) | handle it like any matrix warning | U59 (E2-S1) |
| a multiplicity block on `MAT_SEM` was refused (no p-values) | `MAT_SEM` joins a correction through its OWN `multiplicity` block only (an inherited one never reaches it): the family is every estimates row with a p — free parameters and `:=` rows — and the fold adds `auxiliary.p_adjusted` / `significant_adjusted` (one column, a row per estimates row); the raw `p_value` never moves. Predict counts the members' p-values | additive (multiplicity surface) | set `multiplicity` on the `MAT_SEM` spec to correct its rows | U59 (E2-S1) |
| — | NEW `params.resample: "bootstrap"` with `bootstrap_iters` (default 1000, no `Options.Limits` cap, as a regression's), `rng_seed` and `ci_level`: lavaan's `se = "bootstrap"` — within-group replicates, every free and `:=` row's `std_err` the replicates' SD, the normal p over it, percentile `ci_lower` / `ci_upper`; estimates stay the full data's. NEW scalars `bootstrap_requested` / `bootstrap_succeeded`. Fewer than 10 successes fail the slot alone (`PULSE_MATRIX_NOT_CONVERGED`, `reason: bootstrap_too_few`); buffered (predict reports it); any weight is `PULSE_WEIGHT_UNSUPPORTED`; `params.fit` is `fit_unsupported`; `bootstrap_iters` / `rng_seed` without `resample` are `bad_params` | additive (params + scalars) | opt in per slot; budget `bootstrap_iters` refits (`Options.Limits.RequestTimeout` stops a long run) | U59 (E2-S2) |
| — | `rng_seed` absent or 0 DERIVES the bootstrap seed from the request and the slot's rows — the same rule (`regression.DeriveSeed`) a regression's `resample` uses — so the same request over the same data draws the same replicates at any worker or shard count, and a changed request (the slot name included) or data draws new ones | additive (seed default) | pin a non-zero `rng_seed` for draws that do not move with the request or data | U59 (E2-S3) |
| — | `params.ci_level` (default 0.95) is lavaan's `level` on the Wald interval too: it sets `ci_lower` / `ci_upper` of every lavaan-convention fit, bootstrapped or not; 0.95 is byte-identical. On a semopy-convention fit (no interval) it is `bad_params` | additive (param) | set `ci_level` for a 90% or 99% Wald interval | U59 (E2-S3) |
| — | NEW features `capability:sem_defined` (→ `MAT_SEM`; model syntax only — hidden, a `:=` line is `bad_params`) and `capability:sem_bootstrap` (→ `MAT_SEM`; hidden, `resample`, `bootstrap_iters`, `rng_seed` and `ci_level` are `PULSE_REQUEST_UNKNOWN_FIELD` and the bootstrap scalars leave the manifest), `Since` 1.0.0 | additive (features) | an explicit feature-profile allowlist that wants them names them | U59 (E2-S1, E2-S2) |
| — | NEW attribute `ATTR_SEM_SCORE` (`types.ATTR_SEM_SCORE`, in `types.AllAttributeTypes()`): one `f64` per filtered row, the row's score on `params.latent` (any latent, higher-order included) of a structural equation model fitted inline with `MAT_SEM`'s knobs (`model`, `meanstructure`, `group`, `group_reference`, `group_equal`, `group_partial`, `solver`, `information`, `ftol`, `max_iter`, `grad_tol`, `singular`); `method` `regression` (default) or `bartlett` (lavaan `lavPredict`). With a mean structure (or `group`) the scores sit on the latent-mean scale (the reference level at 0), else they are centered at the fit rows; each row is scored with its own group's parameters; a null member or group scores null. The fit is two-pass and cohort-wide over the rows every `fit_on` filterer admits (`"filtered"` default; an admitted row with a null member or an invalid / zero weight adds no mass), bit-equal to `MAT_SEM`'s fit over the same rows at any worker count or shard split; frequency weight only (`PULSE_WEIGHT_UNSUPPORTED` on probability). A fit that cannot score nulls every score with its `PULSE_MATRIX_*` warning. New feature row `ATTR_SEM_SCORE` (`Since` 1.0.0), an any-of host of `capability:sem_meanstructure` / `sem_groups` / `sem_defined`; manifest, payload-schema and public API goldens changed | additive (operator) | none; read a score like any attribute field (group means, regression predictors — `internal/examples/compose/10_sem_fit_scores.json`) | U59 (E3-S1) |
| — | NEW warning code `PULSE_SEM_SCORE_LEVEL_UNSEEN`: rows whose group level has no fit rows (inline) or no parameters in the bound fit score null, tallied into one warning per slot (folded across shards and chain stages), with fixups and an owner | additive (error code) | handle it like any slot warning; fit on every level you score, or filter the unseen level out | U59 (E3-S1, E3-S2) |
| a Compose `fits` entry holding one `MAT_SEM` matrix was a VECTOR fit (`fits.<name>` = `{"vector": …}`, the battery's listwise mean + co-moment, readable by a fitted attribute's or a co-moment `MAT_*` slot's `params.fit`) | it is a SEM fit, NEW kind `types.FitKindSEM` (`"sem"`): `fits.<name>` = `{"sem": SEMFitResult}` (the model, options, labels, the model's variable orders, solver stop and per group `n`, Σw and the full-precision `lambda` / `beta` / `psi` / `theta` / `nu` / `alpha` / `sample_mean`; a failed fit `{"sem": {operator, error}}`). A vector consumer naming it is `PULSE_MODEL_KIND_MISMATCH` (`kind: "sem"`); an undefined input fails the fit (`PULSE_MATRIX_INSUFFICIENT_N` / `_SINGULAR`) rather than nulling; its `save` writes a SEM fit file (`.pulse-sem.json`, U59 E3-S3 — next row). NEW public `types.FitResult.SEM`, `types.SEMFitResult`, `types.SEMFitGroup`, `descriptor.FitSummary.SEM` (predict lists `{name, cohort, sem: {operator, labels, members}}`); public API and payload-schema goldens changed | behaviour change (fit kind) + additive (types) | read a `MAT_SEM` fit's entry under `sem`; take a vector fit for a co-moment consumer from a `MAT_COVARIANCE` battery over the same members | U59 (E3-S2) |
| — | NEW `ATTR_SEM_SCORE` `params.fit`: scores a bound SEM fit row-local (streams beside groupers, fuses into a crosstab, merges across shards and chain stages), bit-equal to the inline fit over the same rows; members and each row's group level matched by LABEL; refused beside a model knob (`fit_and_model`) or `fit_on` (`fit_and_fit_on`); a failed fit nulls every score with `PULSE_FIT_UNAVAILABLE` | additive (param) | none | U59 (E3-S2) |
| a SEM fit's `save` was refused (`PULSE_MODEL_SAVE_INVALID` reason `sem_unsupported`, U59 E3-S2 only) | it writes a SEM fit file: the path must end `.pulse-sem.json` (any other suffix is reason `suffix`; a regression or vector fit's save under `.pulse-sem.json` is reason `suffix` too), written atomically under the fit's name with `PULSE_MODEL_OVERWRITTEN` on replace and `PULSE_MODEL_WRITE_FAILED` on a write fault; a failed fit writes nothing. A `models` entry whose path ends `.pulse-sem.json` loads it as a SEM fit (the suffix picks the kind; `ATTR_SEM_SCORE` `params.fit` scores from it bit-equal to the in-compose fit, chain stages included); a `models` path ending in neither suffix is `PULSE_MODEL_REF_INVALID` reason `suffix` with a message naming both; a file whose model does not re-specify its stored variable orders is `PULSE_MODEL_FILE_INVALID` reason `schema`. The reason `sem_unsupported` is gone. `.pulse-sem.json` joins the table-directory sidecar exclusion. `capability:models` now also counts `ATTR_SEM_SCORE` as a reader | behaviour change (save accepted; a `models` suffix message) | save SEM fits instead of refitting; load them with `models` | U59 (E3-S3) |
| — | NEW `MAT_SEM` `params.estimator` (`ml` default, `dwls`, `wlsmv`) and `params.ordered` (the ordered members by label, a list or one comma-separated string; required by `dwls` / `wlsmv`, refused under `ml`): lavaan's two-step ordinal fit — each ordered member's thresholds, then every pair's polychoric / polyserial / Pearson correlation, fitted weighted by the inverse diagonal of their asymptotic covariance (Gamma, from casewise scores) in the delta parameterization, with lavaan's robust (sandwich) SEs; `:=` rows use the robust covariance. The estimates gain one `"x | tk"` row per threshold after the parameter table; an ordered member's intercept is fixed at 0 and its residual variance is reported as a fixed row. Scalars: `chi2` / `dof` are the unscaled test with `chi2_p_value` `null`, and `loglik` / `aic` / `bic` are `null`; `wlsmv` adds NEW `chi2_scaled`, `chi2_scaled_p_value`, `scaling_factor`, `shift_parameter`, `chi2_baseline_scaled`, `cfi_scaled`, `tli_scaled`, `rmsea_scaled`. Buffered (predict reports it); frequency weights only. Refused `bad_params` (predict alike): `solver: "slsqp"`, `information`, `singular` or `meanstructure: false` beside an ordinal estimator, a standardizing vector, a latent, an observed predictor or a member the model does not read in `ordered`; a probability weight is `PULSE_WEIGHT_UNSUPPORTED`; on the data the slot fails alone — a constant ordered member `PULSE_MATRIX_SINGULAR`, an empty category or no rows `PULSE_MATRIX_INSUFFICIENT_N`. NEW feature `capability:sem_ordinal` (→ any of `MAT_SEM`, `ATTR_SEM_SCORE`), `Since` 1.0.0: hidden, `estimator`, `ordered` and `parameterization` are `PULSE_REQUEST_UNKNOWN_FIELD` and the scaled scalars leave the manifest. Manifest and payload-schema goldens changed | additive (params + rows + scalars + feature) | none; judge a `wlsmv` fit by the scaled figures, and see the `sem-ordinal` skill for when to prefer it over ML | U59 (E4-S1, E4-S2) |
| an ordinal `MAT_SEM` (`estimator` `dwls` / `wlsmv`) with `params.group` or `params.resample` was `SERVICE_VALIDATION` `bad_params` | it runs: lavaan's multi-group DWLS / WLSMV (categories coded over every level's rows; `group_equal` gains `"thresholds"`, ordinal only; `group_partial` takes `"x|tk"`; a grouped table adds an `x ~*~ x` scale row per ordered member per level, free outside the reference level under `thresholds` for a non-binary member) and a seeded non-parametric bootstrap (each replicate refits the two-step statistics at the full fit's coding). A category one level lacks is `PULSE_MATRIX_INSUFFICIENT_N` `empty_category` with `details.level`; a `group_partial` threshold the data lack is reason `partial_threshold_absent` | behaviour change (accepted) + additive (rows) | none | U59 (E4-S3) |
| — | NEW `MAT_SEM` `params.parameterization`: `delta` (default, lavaan's) only; `theta` is `SERVICE_VALIDATION` reason `parameterization_unsupported`, another value or the knob without an ordinal estimator `bad_params`; manifest golden changed | additive (param) | omit it, or pass `delta` | U59 (E4-S3) |
| — | NEW error code `PULSE_MATRIX_TOO_MANY_CATEGORIES`: an ordered member with more than 32 categories fails the slot (`details.variable` / `categories` / `limit`), with fixups and an owner | additive (error code) | bin the member, or drop it from `ordered` | U59 (E4-S3) |
| an ordinal fit's polychoric correlation over a sparse table could stop at the −0.999 bound (the score bent negative just inside it) | the ML estimate is a local ascent from the start value — lavaan's figure (a fit reading such a pair moves) | behaviour change (output) | none | U59 (E4-S3) |
| a Compose `fits` entry over an ordinal `MAT_SEM` (`estimator` `dwls` / `wlsmv`) was `PULSE_FIT_INVALID` reason `sem_ordinal`, and `ATTR_SEM_SCORE` had no `estimator` / `ordered` keys | the entry binds a SEM fit (it may `save`), and `ATTR_SEM_SCORE` scores an ordinal fit — inline (NEW `params.estimator`, `ordered`, `parameterization`, under `capability:sem_ordinal`), bound or loaded — by lavaan's empirical Bayes modal scores (each row's posterior mode, per group); `method: "regression"` means them (lavaan's rule), `"bartlett"` is `SERVICE_VALIDATION` `bad_params` (`param` `method`; predict alike); an observed mediator (an observed variable with an outgoing arrow) is scored as lavaan's dummy latent. A row's ordered value is coded by the fit's categories: a value outside them scores null with `PULSE_SEM_SCORE_LEVEL_UNSEEN` (`details.reason` `category_unseen`). The reason `sem_ordinal` is gone | behaviour change (a refusal lifted) + additive (params) | drop any handling of `sem_ordinal`; omit `method` (or set `regression`) on an ordinal fit | U59 (E4-S4) |
| `types.SEMFitResult` / `SEMFitGroup` carried no ordinal figures; a SEM fit file was always `sem_format_version` 1 | NEW `SEMFitResult.Ordered` (`ordered,omitempty`), `Categories` (`categories,omitempty`) and `SEMFitGroup.Thresholds` (`thresholds,omitempty`), set on an ordinal fit only; an ordinal fit's file is `sem_format_version` 2 (an ML fit's stays version 1, byte-identical). The public API and payload-schema goldens changed | additive (fields) + file version | none for ML fits; an older build reading an ordinal fit file refuses it as `PULSE_MODEL_VERSION_UNSUPPORTED` | U59 (E4-S4) |
| — | an ordinal fit's `ATTR_SEM_SCORE` scores a row with a null ordered (or continuous) member `null`; lavaan's `lavPredict` instead skips the missing indicator and scores the row from the rest. Listwise is Pulse's rule on every SEM surface | divergence from lavaan (documented) | filter or impute rows with a missing rating before scoring if you need lavaan's partial-row scores | U59 (E4-S4) |
| — | parity notes for the ordinal slices, no wire change: the WLSMV bootstrap is held to lavaan's DWLS bootstrap, because lavaan refuses `se = "bootstrap"` under WLSMV and the two estimators share their point estimates; the EBM scores are held to lavaan's EBM objective minimised to `reltol` 1e-15, because `lavPredict(method = "EBM")` at its default (BFGS, `reltol` 1e-8) stops up to about 4e-4 short of the posterior mode, which Pulse returns | parity note | compare Pulse's EBM scores with lavaan's only after tightening lavaan's optimiser; expect differences up to ~4e-4 against a default `lavPredict` call | U59 (E4-S3, E4-S4) |

## Changes from U60 (MMM estimation)

Shipped in `v1.0.0-alpha.27`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| — | NEW public package `github.com/frankbardon/pulse/optimize`: `Minimize(f, grad, x0, MinimizeOptions)` — SLSQP (SciPy 1.18.1's, BSD-3 notice in `optimize/SCIPY_LICENSE.txt`) over `Lower` / `Upper` bounds and `[]Constraint{Fn, Grad, Type: Eq\|Ineq}`, `Acc` (default `1e-6`) and `MaxIter` (default 100), returning `*MinimizeResult` (`X`, `F`, per-constraint `Multipliers`, per-variable `Active` bound, `MaxViolation`, `Status`, `Iterations`, `FuncEvals`, `GradEvals`); FMA-free, so the same callbacks give the same bits on every architecture. Docs: `docs/src/library/optimize.md` | additive (package) | import it to solve a bounded or constrained smooth problem | U60 (E1-S1) |
| — | NEW `optimize.LeastSquares(residual, jacobian, x0, lower, upper, LeastSquaresOptions)` — bounded Levenberg–Marquardt (projected steps reach a bound exactly; λ from `1e-3`, ×⅓ on accept, ×4 on reject; `MaxIter` 2000, `CostTol` 1e-13, `StepTol` 1e-10), returning `*LeastSquaresResult` (`X`, `Cost`, `JTJ *linalg.Sym` at the solution, per-parameter `Active`, `Status` `LSConvergedCost` / `LSConvergedStep` / `LSConvergedDamping` / `LSIterLimit`, `Iterations`, `FuncEvals`, `JacEvals`); an iteration-limit stop returns the iterate with `PULSE_OPTIMIZE_NOT_CONVERGED`. Docs: `docs/src/library/optimize.md` | additive (function) | import it for a bounded nonlinear fit; form `σ²(JᵀJ)⁻¹` from `JTJ` | U60 (E1-S2) |
| — | NEW error codes `PULSE_OPTIMIZE_INVALID` (bad input, nil result), `PULSE_OPTIMIZE_NOT_CONVERGED` and `PULSE_OPTIMIZE_INFEASIBLE` (a short stop; the final iterate returned with the error), with fixups and the `shared` owner | additive (error code) | handle them like any coded error | U60 (E1-S1) |

A sweep's `mode_unknown` `valid` lists all four | behaviour change (enum + `details.valid`) | do not iterate `AllSweepModes()` to build a `for_each` mode list | U60 (E2-S1) |

Serial and `ComposeParallel` give the same bytes | behaviour change (new output) | read `optimizations[i]` | U60 (E3-S2) |
