Refusals keep the model-file codes (`PULSE_MODEL_REF_INVALID`, `_NAME_COLLISION` against a fit, `_FILE_UNREADABLE`, `_FILE_INVALID`, `_VERSION_UNSUPPORTED`, `_TERM_UNKNOWN`) with `details.optimize` / `details.field`, predict and runtime alike; hidden `capability:models` refuses the key `PULSE_REQUEST_UNKNOWN_FIELD` | additive (field) | set `models` on a block to optimise over a saved fit | U60 (E3-S2) |
| — | a block that fails on its numbers (no start converged: `PULSE_OPTIMIZE_NOT_CONVERGED` / `_INFEASIBLE`; or its `coef()` reads a Compose fit that failed on its data: the fit's own code) fails alone: `{name, direction, error}` plus a `PULSE_SLOT_FAILED` warning (`slot_kind` `optimizations`, `index`, `label`); `Strict` fails the batch with the block's own code. `PULSE_SLOT_FAILED` gains the owner `capability:compose_optimize`. `pulse api compose --stream` ends with one `{"optimizations":[...]}` line | additive (output + warning) | handle `optimizations[i].error` | U60 (E3-S2) |
| `internal/detmath` (not importable outside the module; `contrib/mmmpulse` carried a copy of `Exp` / `Log` / `Pow`) | NEW public package `github.com/frankbardon/pulse/linalg/detmath`: the FMA-free deterministic ports `Exp`, `Expm1`, `Log`, `Log1p`, `Pow`, `Sin`, `Cos`, `Tan`, `Atan2`, `Asin`, `Erf`, `Erfinv`, NEW `Lgamma(x) (float64, int)`, and the ziggurat `NormFloat64` / `ExpFloat64(*rand.Rand)`; bits identical on every architecture. `contrib/mmmpulse` imports it (its copy and `GO_LICENSE.txt` are gone). Docs: `docs/src/library/linalg.md` (Deterministic math) | additive (package) | import it in a pack or extension operator whose output must match across architectures | U60 (E4-S4) |
| `WIN_DISTRIBUTED_LAG`'s geometric / Weibull CDF / Weibull PDF weights used `math.Pow` / `Exp` / `Log`; the Student-t p-values, quantiles and the `ci_lower` / `ci_upper` bounds built on them (`StudentTQuantile`, the incomplete beta, `logBeta` with `math.Lgamma`) used `math.*` with contractible products | both run on `linalg/detmath` with every product barriered, so the weights, the lagged column, t p-values and CI bounds are bit-identical on arm64 and amd64. On amd64 a Weibull weight or a t-quantile can move by 1 ulp (none of the committed goldens or R references moved); on arm64 by a few ulps (a t p-value up to ~1e-14 relative) | behaviour change (last-ulp rounding) | none; compare these figures with a tolerance, never bit for bit across releases | U60 (E4-S4) |
| — | NEW nested Go module `github.com/frankbardon/pulse/contrib/mmmpulse` (own `go.mod`, lockstep-tagged with Pulse like `contrib/otelpulse` / `contrib/prompulse`; `make contrib`, CI, `scripts/release-contrib.sh` and dependabot pick it up; the root module never imports it). `mmmpulse.Extensions()` registers the regression `REG_MMM_HILL` and the pack skills `op-reg-mmm-hill` and `ext-mmm-workflow`, served by `Skills` like the built-in pack. Docs: `contrib/mmmpulse/README.md`, `docs/src/library/mmm-pack.md` | additive (module) | add the module and pass `Extensions: mmmpulse.Extensions()` to `pulse.New`; the stock `pulse` CLI and `pulse mcp` do not register packs, so use the library or `mcpserve` with `Options.Extensions`. Unregistered, `REG_MMM_HILL` is `PULSE_REGRESSION_INVALID` | U60 (E4-S1) |
| — | NEW regression `REG_MMM_HILL` (pack): `y = β0 + Σ β_c · hill(a_c, k_c, s_c) + Σ γ_j · z_j`, all parameters fitted jointly by bounded Levenberg–Marquardt on `optimize.LeastSquares`. `params.channels` (required; the Hill predictors, adstocked upstream), `nonnegative` (default `true`: each channel β bounded at 0, so an unsupported channel is exactly 0), `prior` (`{channel, mean, precision}`, a Gaussian prior on one β). Coefficients in order: `(intercept)`, channel betas, controls, `k:<channel>`, `s:<channel>`, so `coef()` reads the curve; `k` is bounded to [0.02, 20] × the channel mean and `s` to [0.5, 5]. Standard errors are `σ²(JᵀJ)⁻¹` over the free parameters (delta method for `k` / `s`), per parameter: `null` for a parameter on a bound and for the `k` / `s` of a zero-effect channel, unlike `REG_OLS` with bounds, where one binding bound nulls every SE. A shared slot the model did not declare is `PROCESSING_CONFIG`; no convergence in 1000 iterations fails the slot alone (`PROCESSING_REGRESSION_NO_CONVERGE`). Holdout, `resample`, `vcov`, `save` and `ATTR_REG_PREDICT` work. Estimates and SEs are bit-identical on arm64 and amd64 | additive (pack regression) | register the pack; fit decays in a `lhs` sweep ranked on `regressions.<name>.holdout.rmse`, then fit and `optimize` in a second request (`regression-model-options`, `ext-mmm-workflow`) | U60 (E4-S1) |
| the test-only `REG_TESTMMM_HILL` (root test registration, its own Levenberg–Marquardt) ended a negative-effect non-negative fit on a NaN Jacobian (β 0, `k` +Inf, `s` 0) | it fits through `optimize.LeastSquares`, a trial whose Jacobian is not finite is rejected, and the same fit ends finite (β ~2.5e-133); on the 211 fits the existing tests run, 185 moved by under 1e-9 relative and the unidentified display channel's β and `k` by up to 0.19% (SSR unchanged to 4e-13) | behaviour change (test registration only; embedders who copied the recipe get the new solver by importing `optimize`) | none for the shipped surface | U60 (E1-S2) |

## Changes from U62

Not yet released (targets `v1.0.0-alpha.28`). `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change. Covers the public `mcmc` package, the three `extend` additions and the `REG_MMM_BAYES` pack regression.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| an extension regression had no warnings channel (only matrix extensions did) | NEW `extend.RegressionOutput.Warnings []*types.ResponseWarning` and NEW `types.RegressionResult.Warnings` (`warnings`, `omitempty`). The adapter copies a model's warnings onto its result and lifts each onto `Response.Warnings` with `details.regression` (slot label) and `details.index` (request slot index) added unless the model set them, so `pulse api process --json` promotes it to the envelope `warnings`; a Compose fit's lift to `ComposedResponse.Warnings` with `details.fit`. Under `Strict` the first warning fails its slot with the warning's own code, and the per-slot Strict rule fails the run with that code plus `{slot_kind, index, label}`. A code must be registered (`errors.ParseCode`) and a message non-empty, else `PULSE_EXTENSION_REGRESSION_OUTPUT_MISMATCH` reason `unregistered_warning_code` / `invalid_warning` (at the `pulse.New` probe and on every run). A model emitting none is byte-identical | additive (fields) | return coded diagnostics in `Warnings`; read `regressions[i].warnings` or the response warnings | U62 (E2-S1) |
| a response's `returned.digest` (`rp1:…`) for a given `return` block | moves for every shaped response: `regressions[*].warnings` joins the nested warning keep-paths the plan hashes (as each new nested slot did before, e.g. U57's holdout); the shaped bytes are otherwise unchanged and a `return` never drops a regression's warnings | behaviour change (digest value) | never pin a digest across releases; recompute it from `PredictResult.Return` | U62 (E2-S1) |
| — | NEW error code `PROCESSING_REGRESSION_DIAGNOSTIC`: the generic warning of a fit whose own diagnostic crossed its threshold (R-hat, ESS, divergences, tree depth), `details.reason` naming it; fixup and the `shared` owner | additive (error code) | emit it from an extension regression's `Warnings`; handle it like any coded warning | U62 (E2-S1) |
| a buffered extension regression's `Fit(rows)` got no ctx: a long fit ran past a caller cancel and `Options.Limits.RequestTimeout` (honoured only between phases and refits) | NEW optional sibling `extend.ContextRegression { FitContext(ctx context.Context, rows extend.Rows) (extend.RegressionOutput, error) }`, implemented beside `Fit`. The engine prefers it on the run, every `resample` / `selection` refit and the `pulse.New` probe (a background ctx there); ctx is the operation's, so a caller cancel surfaces as `context.Canceled` and the deadline as the coded `PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` `limit: "request_timeout"`. A model without it is fitted through `Fit`, unchanged | additive (interface) | for a long fit, add `FitContext`, check `ctx.Err()` between iterations and return it; keep `Fit` (it can delegate with `context.Background()`); refuse the 8-row probe cheaply | U62 (E2-S2) |
| an extension regression could not refuse `resample` declaratively ("always honoured"; only its factory could reject `spec.Resample`) | NEW `pulse.RegressionRegistration.NoResample bool`: set, `resample`, `jackknife_groups`, `jackknife_by` and `bootstrap_iters` are `PROCESSING_CONFIG` `{regression, slot: "resample"}` (`key` names a set knob; `name` the slot label) at predict and runtime alike, the manifest row lists no `resample` slot, and `rng_seed` still reaches the factory. Unset is byte-identical. `descx.RegressionFieldClassOf` now classifies the resample keys `opt_out` (was `accepted`) | additive (field) | set `NoResample: true` on a model a row-subset refit would break or make too slow; drop any factory-side `spec.Resample` check | U62 (E2-S2) |
| — | NEW public package `github.com/frankbardon/pulse/mcmc`: `Model` (`Dim`, `LogDensityGrad` on the unconstrained space), `Sampler`, `NUTS{MaxTreeDepth, TargetAccept, Workers}` (zero value usable; `DefaultChains` 4, `DefaultWarmup` 1000, `DefaultDraws` 1000, `DefaultMaxTreeDepth` 10, `DefaultTargetAccept` 0.8), `Options{Chains, Warmup, Draws, Init, Constrain}`, `Result` / `Diagnostics` / `ChainStats`, the `Positive` / `Unit` / `Bounded` transforms, and `RHat` / `ESSBulk` / `ESSTail`. Seeded: the same seed gives the same bytes across runs, `Workers` and architectures, provided the model is FMA-free and uses `linalg/detmath`. Docs: `docs/src/library/mcmc.md` | additive (package) | import it to sample a custom model with NUTS; wrap products feeding an add as `float64(a*b)` | U62 (E1) |
| — | NEW error codes `PULSE_MCMC_INVALID` (bad options, model shape or `Init`; `reason` `nil_model`, `dim`, `chains`, `warmup`, `draws`, `max_tree_depth`, `workers`, `target_accept`, `init_length`, `init_not_finite`, `init_density_not_finite`) and `PULSE_MCMC_FAILED` (the sampler cannot run the model; `reason` `init_not_found`, `step_size_unbounded`, `step_size_zero`, `metric_not_finite`, plus `chain`), with fixups and the `shared` owner. `NUTS.Sample` returned `PROCESSING_CONFIG` / `PROCESSING_RUNTIME` for these before alpha.28 | additive (error code) | match on the `PULSE_MCMC_*` codes; `details.reason` is unchanged | U62 (E3-S2) |
| — | NEW pack regression `REG_MMM_BAYES` in `contrib/mmmpulse` (Bayesian media mix: raw spend adstocked in the model, Hill saturation, linear controls, NUTS posterior on `mcmc`). Params `channels`, `time` (required, contiguous integers), `max_lag`, `priors`, `chains` / `warmup` / `draws`, `credible_level`, `target_accept`, `holdout_from`; outputs ROI, analytic mROI and contribution intervals, diagnostics and holdout; sampler health arrives as `PROCESSING_REGRESSION_DIAGNOSTIC` warnings. No save, scoring or resample. Skill `op-reg-mmm-bayes` | additive (pack operator) | register `mmmpulse.Extensions()` as for `REG_MMM_HILL`; use it when decay uncertainty matters, the sweep route to allocate budget | U62 (E3) |

## Changes from U63 (mmm-followups)

Not yet released (targets `v1.0.0-alpha.29`; release notes in [U63](../units/U63-mmm-followups.md)). `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change. Covers the MMM regressions becoming built-ins (and the `contrib/mmmpulse` removal), a sweep winner bound as a fit with `sweep_axis()`, aggregation over window columns (windows-first) with predict's `post_window` block, and two CLI fixes (`--limit` on every leaf, the `WIN_ADSTOCK` rename hint).

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the retired window type `WIN_ADSTOCK` was refused as a bare unknown type: `PULSE_WINDOW_INVALID` `window[i]: unknown window type WIN_ADSTOCK`, details `{window_index, type}` | still refused (no alias, the refusal is never removed), now naming the replacement on predict and every runtime surface alike: `window[i]: unknown window type WIN_ADSTOCK (renamed to WIN_DISTRIBUTED_LAG)`, details gain `renamed_to: "WIN_DISTRIBUTED_LAG"`, and the code carries a fixup pointing at it. An instance whose feature profile hides `WIN_DISTRIBUTED_LAG` keeps the plain refusal; a never-registered name never carries `renamed_to` | behaviour change (refusal message + details key) | none; match on the code, not the message — or read `details.renamed_to` to rewrite the type | U63 (E1-S1) |
| `pulse recommend --limit N` (maximum recommendations) | `pulse recommend --top N`: `--limit` is now the root resource-limit flag. CLI flag only — `RecommendRequest.Limit`, the payload `limit` key and the `pulse_recommend` MCP argument are unchanged | behaviour change (CLI flag rename) | replace `--limit N` with `--top N` in recommend scripts | U63 (E1-S2) |
| `--limit name=value` existed only on `pulse mcp`: no other leaf could raise an `Options.Limits` ceiling (a 2,000-slot `pulse api compose` failed with `PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`); a bad `pulse mcp --limit` failed startup with exit status 1 | `--limit` is a repeatable ROOT persistent flag, accepted before or after any leaf name, reaching every instance a leaf builds (`pulse mcp` keeps its per-key layering over the feature profile and its startup-line echo). A bad name or value is now a usage error on every leaf, `pulse mcp` included: exit status 2, `CLI_INPUT` with details `{flag, value, limit?}` unchanged. Limits stay instance-level: no request slot carries one. Shell completion offers the `name=` candidates on every leaf | additive (CLI flag) + behaviour change (exit status) | pass `--limit max_compose_slots=N` (etc.) to any leaf; a script that checked `pulse mcp`'s exit status 1 for a bad `--limit` should expect 2 | U63 (E1-S2) |
| `REG_MMM_HILL` was an extension regression shipped by `contrib/mmmpulse`: an instance ran it only after `pulse.Options.Extensions: mmmpulse.Extensions()` (the stock `pulse` CLI and `pulse mcp` refused it, `PULSE_REGRESSION_INVALID`); the manifest listed it under `extensions.regressions` | `REG_MMM_HILL` is a BUILT-IN regression (`types.REG_MMM_HILL`, new additive constant), same name, params, coefficient table, scorer and figures: every instance runs it with no registration and the manifest lists it under `regressions` (the extension section no longer does), with the resample knobs (`bootstrap_iters`, `jackknife_groups`, `jackknife_by`, `rng_seed`) and `name` declared one by one; its skill `op-reg-mmm-hill` ships in the core pack; a feature profile lists it as `REG_MMM_HILL` (`since` 1.0.0). Predict now knows a fit's `k:<channel>` / `s:<channel>` names from `params.channels`, so a `coef()` naming an uncatalogued curve term over a `fits` entry is refused at predict (`PULSE_MODEL_TERM_UNKNOWN`, the runtime's code and message), not only at run time; likewise the model's spec-only refusals (no `params.channels`, a channel that is not a predictor or is listed twice, an invalid `prior`) are now raised by predict too, with the runtime's `PROCESSING_CONFIG` code, message and details. `mmmpulse.Registration()` is removed and `mmmpulse.Extensions()` now registers only `REG_MMM_HILL`'s sibling `REG_MMM_BAYES` (the module itself is removed by E2-S3, row below); an embedder registering `REG_MMM_HILL` itself is refused at `pulse.New` (`PULSE_EXTENSION_NAME_COLLISION`). Model files saved by the extension (model format version 3) load and score unchanged | additive (built-in operator, constant) + behaviour change (manifest section, predict refusal, contrib API) | drop any own `REG_MMM_HILL` registration and `mmmpulse.Registration()` call; read the model from the manifest's `regressions`; a request that predicted clean with a misspelt curve term or a bad `params` block now reports it at predict | U63 (E2-S1) |
| `REG_MMM_BAYES` was an extension regression shipped by `contrib/mmmpulse`: an instance ran it only after `pulse.Options.Extensions: mmmpulse.Extensions()` (the stock `pulse` CLI and `pulse mcp` refused it, `PULSE_REGRESSION_INVALID`); the manifest listed it under `extensions.regressions`; its spec refusals (bad `params`: no channels, a missing `time`, an out-of-range sampler setting, an invalid prior) surfaced only at run time; a missing `params.time` was `PULSE_REQUEST_PARAM_INVALID`; the skills `op-reg-mmm-bayes` and `ext-mmm-workflow` came with the pack | `REG_MMM_BAYES` is a BUILT-IN regression (`types.REG_MMM_BAYES`, new additive constant), same name, params, coefficient table, outputs, warnings, seed rule and figures: every instance runs it with no registration and the manifest lists it under `regressions` (no resample, holdout, save or multiplicity slot; `name` declared); its spec-only refusals are now raised by predict too, with the runtime's `PROCESSING_CONFIG` code, message and details — a missing `params.time` included, which is now that `PROCESSING_CONFIG` (`slot: params.time`) on both sides instead of `PULSE_REQUEST_PARAM_INVALID`; an unknown `params.time` field is refused by predict and the runtime alike (`regression references unknown field: X`, details `field_inputs: true`); `op-reg-mmm-bayes` ships in the core pack and the media-mix design guide is the core topical skill `mmm-workflow` (renamed from `ext-mmm-workflow`: `ext-` is the embedder namespace); a feature profile lists it as `REG_MMM_BAYES` (`since` 1.0.0). `mmmpulse.BayesRegistration()` and `mmmpulse.BayesName` are removed and `mmmpulse.Extensions()` returns an empty set (the module itself is removed by E2-S3, row below); an embedder registering `REG_MMM_BAYES` itself is refused at `pulse.New` (`PULSE_EXTENSION_NAME_COLLISION`). The 50-replicate coverage study harness (the `mmmcoverage` build tag) and the pass bars are removed (E3-S6: Pulse tests operators one operation at a time) | additive (built-in operator, constant, skills) + behaviour change (manifest section, predict refusals, missing-`time` code, skill stem, contrib API) | drop any own `REG_MMM_BAYES` registration and `mmmpulse.BayesRegistration()` call; read the model from the manifest's `regressions`; fetch `mmm-workflow` instead of `ext-mmm-workflow`; match a missing `params.time` on `PROCESSING_CONFIG` | U63 (E2-S2) |
| the nested module `github.com/frankbardon/pulse/contrib/mmmpulse` (lockstep-tagged with Pulse; `mmmpulse.Extensions()` registered `REG_MMM_HILL` and `REG_MMM_BAYES` through `pulse.Options.Extensions`; book page `library/mmm-pack.md`) | REMOVED, no shim: no `contrib/mmmpulse/v1.0.0-alpha.29` tag is cut, `make contrib`, CI, `scripts/release-contrib.sh` and dependabot cover only `contrib/otelpulse` / `contrib/prompulse`. `REG_MMM_HILL` and `REG_MMM_BAYES` are built-ins on every instance, the stock `pulse` CLI and `pulse mcp` included; their docs are the book page `library/mmm.md` and the core skills `op-reg-mmm-hill`, `op-reg-mmm-bayes`, `mmm-workflow`. Earlier `contrib/mmmpulse/v1.0.0-alpha.*` tags stay resolvable | breaking (module removed, embedder API) | delete the `contrib/mmmpulse` import and its `go.mod` require, and drop `mmmpulse.Extensions()` from `pulse.Options.Extensions` ; nothing replaces it, the models run with no registration | U63 (E2-S3) |
| `types.RankEntry` was `{Label, Value, Rank}` (`ranking[]` entries `{label, value, rank}`); a sweep winner's axis values were recoverable only by parsing its slot label; `RankEntry` was a comparable struct (`==`, `slices.Equal`) | every entry also carries `Slot int` (`slot`, the 0-based index among the sweep slots in expansion order: its response is `Responses[explicit+slot]`) and `Axes map[string]any` (`axes`, each axis name → the typed value the slot ran with: a `json.Number` for a decoded or drawn number, a string or a bool; JSON numbers / strings / booleans on the wire), always set, under grid / zip / random / lhs, in the `--json` envelope, MCP and the `--stream` trailing `{"ranking":[...]}` line alike, serial = parallel. Additive on the wire (`format_version` stays `"1.1"`); ranked sweeps' `ranking` bytes grow by the two keys. `RankEntry` is no longer comparable, so `==` / `slices.Equal` on it no longer compiles | additive (wire, fields) + breaking (Go: `RankEntry` comparability) | read winning values from `Ranking[0].Axes` instead of parsing `Label`; replace `==` / `slices.Equal` on `RankEntry` with `reflect.DeepEqual` (or compare marshalled JSON when one side was decoded from the wire: decoded axes hold `float64`, the library's `json.Number`) | U63 (E3-S1) |
| at `rng_seed` 0 (or absent) a seed was derived from the raw JSON bytes as written: reordering keys inside a sampled sweep's `request` / `overlays` body, or inside a `REG_RESAMPLE` bootstrap's, a `MAT_SEM` bootstrap's or a `REG_MMM_BAYES` slot's `params`, changed the derived seed — so the draws, the `sweep_seed` echo and the sweep winner; whitespace did not. A `PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` message and its raise fixup suggested `pulse mcp --limit <limit>=` | every rng_seed 0 derivation hashes those raw bodies in one canonical form (`internal/regresample.CanonicalJSON`): object keys sorted at every depth, whitespace dropped, array order kept, every number and string kept as written (`0.10` ≠ `0.1`, no float round-trip; `{"$var": …}` and `{{axis}}` pass through). Bodies differing only in key order or spacing derive the same seed and draw identically, serial = parallel, predict's `sweep.rng_seed` included; any value change still moves it. An explicit non-zero `rng_seed` is unchanged. A body whose keys were ALREADY sorted at every depth (and not duplicated or escaped) keeps its old seed; any other unseeded sweep, bootstrap or Bayesian slot gets NEW draws. The `PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` message now reads `… or the CLI's --limit <limit>= on any pulse command …` and the fixup names the root `--limit <limit>=<value>` flag | behaviour change (unseeded draws; error message text) | to keep an old run's draws, pin its seed as a non-zero `rng_seed` (a sweep echoes it as `sweep_seed`, `REG_MMM_BAYES` as `outputs.seed`); match `PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` on its code and `details`, never its message | U63 (E3-S7) |
| an embedder that planned around the post-window phase (which attributes run after the windows, and whether the windows run first) re-implemented the classification from the request JSON — and missed `ATTR_REG_PREDICT`, whose window reads come from the bound model's terms, not an expression | predict reports the plan: `PredictResult.PostWindow` (`post_window`, additive `omitempty`; new `descriptor.PostWindowPlan` `{windows_first, window_labels, attributes}` and `descriptor.PostWindowAttribute` `{index, label, depends_on, window_reads}`) on `Predict` / `PredictBytes` / `PredictEnvelope`, and per slot on `ComposeSlotPredict.PostWindow` from `PredictCompose` (classified over the batch's `fits` too). Only post-window attributes are listed, in declared order, by index into the request as run (item `for_each` expanded; as written when the expansion is refused). The same classification the field-reference pass and the engine order by; reported on every decoded request, refused drafts included — a request stopped before the field references (hidden slot, `for_each` refusal, unreadable cohort header, a Compose slot's join / matrix / `return` / zone refusal) is classified without a schema; absent only without a window | additive (descriptor types + fields) | read `post_window` from predict instead of mirroring the classifier; address attributes by `index` | U63 (E4-S4) |
| a request windows-first ONLY through a bound model's terms (an aggregation over an `ATTR_REG_PREDICT` label whose model reads a window label) moved its attributes after the windows, but a regression, a holdout predicate or a `MAT_*` vector member in it reading that window label was refused on predict and the runtime alike (`PULSE_REGRESSION_WINDOW_UNAVAILABLE`, or the vector's unknown member) | admitted and run: every site reads the one windows-first decision over the catalog it holds (the runtime's bound models, predict's loaded `models` / a Compose slot's `fits`), so the window label is a record column for the regression, the holdout and the vector member exactly as when a slot reads it directly; results equal the same request over a cohort storing the window column. A window label read only through an extension aggregator's `FieldInputs` still does not turn the mode on (refused as an unknown field on both sides) | additive (refusal → result) | none | U63 (E4-S6) |
| a crosstab with `regressions`: the fusion gate admitted it, so a fusable crosstab failed `PROCESSING_INTERNAL` ("fused crosstab path does not implement regressions"), and a buffered one returned no `regressions` at all, silently; predict reported `crosstab_fusable: true` | the fusion gate declines it (`crosstab_fusion_reasons` `regressions force buffered`, predict and the runtime alike; predict's `streamable_reasons` add `crosstab with regressions runs via the buffered path`) and the buffered crosstab fits the regressions over the filtered records, exactly as the same request without its crosstab does: `Response.Regressions` is populated | behaviour change (refusal / silent drop → results; predict verdict) | read the regressions from `regressions`; drop any split into a separate regression request made to work around it | U63 (E4-S6) |
| a crosstab carrying a window no axis grouper, cell or margin aggregation reads ran with the window silently dropped (a crosstab writes no output row for it to run over) | refused before any row, by predict and the runtime alike: `PULSE_WINDOW_INVALID` `details.reason: "crosstab_window_unread"` `{window_index}` — the grouped and aggregated shapes run such a window over their output rows and refuse it when they lack its names; a crosstab's windows run only windows-first | behaviour change (silent drop → refusal) | drop the window, or read its label from an axis, the cell or a margin aggregation (windows-first) | U63 (E4-S6) |
| a `fits` entry always carried its own `request`; a sweep's winning axis values were recoverable only from the slot label or `ranking`, so a search and its follow-up needed two Compose calls | NEW additive `fits[].from_sweep {regression}` (`types.FitFromSweep`; `FitSpec.Request` is now optional) binds the rank-1 slot of the batch's ranked `sweep` as a named regression fit: the batch runs the authored fits, then the sweep slots and the rank (phase 0), then each bound fit (refitted over the winner's rows), then `optimize` and the explicit slots; responses keep index order, serial = parallel. NEW expression function `sweep_axis("<fit>", "<axis>")` reads the winner's axis value (a number as float64) in explicit-slot formulas and filters and in `optimize` expressions. Static refusals (`PULSE_FIT_INVALID` `from_sweep_*` reasons, `PULSE_MODEL_TERM_UNKNOWN` `not_sweep_bound` / `axis_unknown`) are raised alike by predict and the runtime; an empty ranking binds the fit FAILED (`PULSE_SWEEP_RANK_PATH` reason `empty`, `PULSE_SLOT_FAILED` with `details.fit`, readers get `PULSE_FIT_UNAVAILABLE`, `Strict` fails the batch). A batch with no `from_sweep` entry keeps its order and bytes. New feature `capability:compose_sweep_bind` (`since` 1.0.0; a profile that hides it refuses the key) and skill `compose-sweep-bind`. The strict JSON decoder (`pulse api`, MCP) now reports an unknown or hidden key on a `fits` entry with `details.fit` (the library's slot gate already did) | additive (request key, expression function, feature, skill) + behaviour change (strict-decode error details) | bind the winner in-request instead of a second call; read a decode refusal's `details.fit` | U63 (E3-S2, E3-S3, E3-S4) |
| `sweep_axis` was an ordinary name: an embedder `ExprFunction` could take it, and an `optimize` block could name a variable `sweep_axis` | the name is reserved: an embedder `ExprFunction` named `sweep_axis` is refused at `pulse.New` (`PULSE_EXTENSION_NAME_RESERVED`) and it is a reserved `optimize` variable name. `sweep_axis` inside a fit request or `OVERLAY_FORMULA` is not supported (as `coef()` is not) | breaking (reserved name) | rename an embedder function or optimize variable called `sweep_axis` | U63 (E3-S4) |
| a `REG_MMM_BAYES` slot listing the same predictor twice was not refused by the spec check | refused as `PROCESSING_CONFIG` (`slot: predictors`, `predictor "x" is listed twice`) on predict and the runtime alike | behaviour change (new refusal) | list each predictor once | U63 (E2-S2) |
| a recommend draft put an extension regression's own params at the top level of the regression slot (the draft did not validate) | the draft builder (`internal/guide`) puts every non-slot regression param under `params`, built-in and extension regressions alike; `REG_MMM_HILL` is now a `recommend` driver candidate, so `recommend.drivers` goldens moved | behaviour change (draft shape, candidate set) | none; validate a draft as usual | U63 (E2-S1) |
| a request aggregating a window column (an aggregation, grouper or crosstab axis reading a `WIN_*` label or a post-window attribute label) was refused: `PULSE_ATTRIBUTE_WINDOW_ORDER` or an unknown field, because windows ran after aggregation. A window over a categorical field grouped by `GROUP_CATEGORY` could not exist | WINDOWS-FIRST mode, decided in one place (`vectors.WindowsFirst`): such a request runs its windows over the filtered record rows first, copies the window labels onto the records, runs the post-window attributes, then groups / aggregates / crosstabs as if the window outputs were stored columns (data, weights, Components in declared order and Run equal the same request over a cohort storing the column), ungrouped, grouped and crosstab alike. Predict admits and reports the request buffered with a windows-first reason. A window reading an aggregation label there is `PULSE_WINDOW_INVALID` `windows_first_reads_aggregate`; a REQUEST-level `weight` on a windows-first request is refused (`PROCESSING_CONFIG`, `WIN_LAG has no weighted form`: only slot weights on aggregations work); a non-numeric window value an aggregation reads is `PROCESSING_CONFIG` `{field, value_type}`; grouping by a window over a categorical field stays refused (every built-in window refuses a categorical source, `PULSE_FIELD_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED`). A request that is not windows-first keeps its bytes (the example corpus pins it). Skill `windows-first`, examples `windows/15`, `windows/16` | additive (refusal → result) + behaviour change (new refusals inside the mode) | aggregate window labels in-request instead of materialising a column; put a weight on the aggregation slots, not the request | U63 (E4-S1, E4-S2, E4-S3) |
| `crosstabfuse.Decide` admitted a crosstab carrying windows (and one with regressions), so the fused arm failed `PROCESSING_INTERNAL`; predict reported a historical-order crosstab with windows streamable | the fusion gate declines any window (`windows force buffered`) and any regression; predict's streamability gives a historical-order crosstab with windows a buffered reason, parity with the runtime | behaviour change (internal error → result; predict verdict) | read `streamable` / `crosstab_fusable` from predict as before | U63 (E4-S3, E4-S6) |
| `REG_MMM_HILL` / `REG_MMM_BAYES` appeared only after registration, so a feature profile could not name them | both are built-in features (`REG_MMM_HILL`, `REG_MMM_BAYES`, `since` 1.0.0, dependency edges on the regression family): a feature profile can hide them, and a hidden one acts never-registered. The media-mix design guide is the core skill `mmm-workflow` (was the pack skill `ext-mmm-workflow`) | additive (features) + breaking (skill stem renamed) | fetch `mmm-workflow`; add either name to a profile's `features` list | U63 (E2-S1, E2-S2) |

## Changes from expression-wires

Shipped in `v1.0.0-alpha.31`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change. Covers the parse-only expression read set, the manifest's column-naming param marker, the request read map and facet params.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| an embedder learning what an expression reads (an editor wiring a formula to its columns, a linter, an agent planning a draft's data dependencies) re-implemented expr-lang scoping or compiled the expression against a guessed env | NEW `pulse.ExpressionReads(src string) (descriptor.ExprReads, error)` and NEW `descriptor.ExprReads` `{vars, params, funcs, uses_env}`: the free names (columns, derived labels, vectors; first-use order), the `params.<name>` / `params["<name>"]` names and the called functions, from a parse alone with no instance — the engine's own walk (let bindings, closure pointers, member-chain roots, `$env` members, string literals and keywords as the compile binds them; `params` is a var only when used other than as a parameter read). A source that does not parse returns `PULSE_FORMULA_COMPILE_ERROR` (details `compile_error`, `expression`); a templated `{{token}}` must be rendered first. Docs: `docs/src/library/expression-reads.md` | additive (function, type) | call it instead of a hand-rolled identifier walk | expression-wires (E2-S1) |
| a manifest client could not tell that a `type: "string"` param names a column (MAT_SEM / ATTR_SEM_SCORE `group`, a mean test's `covariate`, AGG_DISTINCT_SUM `distinct_by`, AGG_WEIGHTED_MEAN `weight_field`, REG_MMM_BAYES `time`, MAT_PARTIAL_CORRELATION `control`) and guessed from its name or prose | NEW `descriptor.Param.NamesColumn` (manifest `names_column`, `omitempty`): true on every param entry whose value names a record column (cohort field or a column the request computes), one per element on a `list`; every `type: "field"` entry carries it too, and `type` is unchanged everywhere. Result-column names (post tests' `n_col`, overlays' `field` / `partition_by`) and expression slots do not carry it. Docs: `docs/src/cli/manifest.md` | additive (field; manifest key) | read `names_column` to find the params naming columns; ignore-unknown consumers are unaffected | expression-wires (E2-S2) |
| an embedder needing what each slot of a request document reads (an editor wiring every component to its columns and params, a linter, an agent planning a draft's columns) walked the request roots by hand, kept its own list of expression and column slots, and could not see an extension operator's params | NEW `(*pulse.Pulse).RequestReads(doc []byte, root descriptor.ReadsRoot) (descriptor.RequestReads, error)`, NEW `descriptor.RequestReads` `{root, slots}`, `descriptor.SlotReads` `{path, key, request, columns, params, variables, funcs, uses_env, error}` and `descriptor.ReadsRoot` (`request`, `composed`, `chain`, `facet`; `""` detects): no-execute, over the raw document (it need not validate) — expression slots from Pulse's own list, typed column slots, manifest `names_column` params, nested `fit_on` / `between` components, extension `FieldInputs` hooks, Compose sub-requests / fits / sweep / banner / optimize (decision variables in `variables`, never columns), chain stages, facet filterers and fields. A bad slot carries its own `error` (`PULSE_FORMULA_COMPILE_ERROR`, `SERVICE_VALIDATION`); only a non-object document or unknown root fails the call. Templates must be rendered first. Docs: `docs/src/library/expression-reads.md` (Request read maps) | additive (method, types) | call it instead of a hand-kept slot walk | expression-wires (E2-S3) |
| a facet over a filter reading `params.<name>` (`wave == params.wave`) was refused `PULSE_PARAMS_UNDECLARED` — `FacetRequest` had no `params` — so the caller spliced the value into the expression text or faceted without the filter | NEW `types.FacetRequest.Params` (`params`, `types.Params`, `omitempty`; aliased as `pulse.FacetRequest`): the `Request.Params` value rules and refusals (`PULSE_PARAMS_INVALID` / `_UNDECLARED` / `_FIELD_COLLISION`, predict ≡ run), read by the facet's base and additive filterers, on `FacetSchema`, `PredictFacet`, `pulse api facet` / `predict-facet` and `pulse_facet_schema`; a facet template passes `{"$var": ...}` inside it. Gated by `capability:params`, whose host group now also admits `capability:facet` (as `capability:models`). `RequestReads` facet slots already report `request: ""`, which is now the facet's own block. A facet without params is byte-identical. Docs: `docs/src/contract/payload-schema.md` (Request params) | additive (field) | pass the analysis's `params` on the facet instead of splicing values | expression-wires (E2-S4) |

## Changes from cohort-reshape

Shipped in `v1.0.0-alpha.32`. `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change (a reshaped cohort is the existing `0x01` / `0x02` / shard-archive layout), and the SPSS metadata sidecar's schema does not move. **A materialize request without `output` that succeeded before writes a byte-identical cohort, sidecar and result** on every surface; every materialize row below is additive, a fix of a refusal, or applies only with `output`. The SPSS rows change behaviour in three places, each listed with how to adapt: a `.sav` export of a cohort holding a column SPSS cannot type natively gains one record `7/18` (a byte change), re-importing such a Pulse-written `.sav` restores the column's Pulse type instead of `f64` / `categorical_u8`, and under `Strict` an export holding a `u64` past 2^53 now fails instead of rounding silently. A `.sav` without the new attribute (every third-party file, every `.sav` Pulse wrote before) imports exactly as before. Covers the materialize-only `output` slot — pick, rename and describe columns, retype, recode, bucket, the output container (shards, constants elision, parent groups) — its predict, archive-aware and anchored-shard export, and `.sav` type round-trips.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Materialize` wrote every source column then the derived ones, always: no way to drop, rename, reorder or re-describe a column, and a request needed at least one feature, attribute or window (`PULSE_MATERIALIZE_NO_DERIVED`) | NEW `Request.Output` (`output`, `*types.MaterializeOutput`, `omitempty`) on `Pulse.Materialize`: `columns` (list order) XOR `exclude`; per column `from`, `as`, `description`, `nullable` (a null in a `nullable: false` column fails the run, `PULSE_MATERIALIZE_OUTPUT_NULL`); projection runs last, so filters, sort, weight and derived slots read dropped columns; with `output` a request needs no derived column (pure reshape). Gated by the new feature `capability:materialize_output` (`since` 1.0.0; a profile that hides it refuses the key like an unknown one). Every other operation (Process, Compose, ProcessChain, Predict, their stream / api / MCP surfaces) refuses it: `PULSE_MATERIALIZE_OUTPUT_ONLY` | additive (request slot, feature) | add `output` to a materialize request; do not hand it to Process | cohort-reshape (E1-S1) |
| no dry run for materialize: a wrong request surfaced as a run-time refusal, after the source was opened | NEW `(*pulse.Pulse).PredictMaterialize(ctx, *Request) (*descriptor.Envelope, error)` (header + schema only, nothing written); `Data` is a NEW `descriptor.MaterializePredictResult` `{valid, schema_info, columns, derived_columns, risks, container}` with `descriptor.MaterializePredictColumn`, `descriptor.MaterializeRisk` `{column, path, code, check}` (a check only the records settle, failing the run with `code`), `descriptor.MaterializeContainer` and `descriptor.MaterializeGroupPlan`. Every static refusal is the run's own code and details (predict = run, pinned by parity tests). CLI `pulse api materialize --predict` (`--out` then optional; with `--strict` promotes predict warnings); MCP `pulse_materialize` argument `predict` (bool; the answer sits under `predict`, `out` not needed). Reports as a `predict` operation | additive (method, types, CLI flag, MCP argument) | predict before every materialize, store-and-re-run included | cohort-reshape (E1-S2) |
| a dropped or renamed column left the carried SPSS sidecar naming it | the sidecar follows the layout: a dropped multiple-response member removes its whole set, a dropped weight writes an unweighted `.sav`, a rename keeps the old short name and takes the new long name, a retyped / recoded / bucketed column's value labels are rebuilt (`observed: false`) | behaviour change (only with `output`) | none | cohort-reshape (E1-S3) |
| materializing a source holding a `vec_*` field failed (`ENCODING_INVALID`: vector field has no VECTORS entry) — the schema's vector metadata was never copied | each kept vector field's `VECTORS` entry (dim, kind, scale, labels) is carried under its output name and position; the output is format `0x02` when it keeps a vector, `0x01` otherwise | fix (a refusal removed) | none | cohort-reshape (E2-S1) |
| no way to retype a column on write | per column `type` (any field type name), `precision` / `scale` (a `decimal128` target), `on_cast_error` (`fail`, the default, or `null`): numeric to numeric, numeric to / from `packed_bool` (0 / 1 only), numeric to / from `date` (epoch days) / `datetime` (epoch seconds), `date` to / from `datetime` in the request `time_zone`, `set_*` to a wider rung, `categorical_*` to another width or to / from numeric. Exact or failing: `PULSE_MATERIALIZE_CAST_FAILED` (`details` column, row, value, reason; nothing written), `PULSE_MATERIALIZE_CAST_NULLED` warning (once per column, `count`) under `on_cast_error: "null"`, `PULSE_MATERIALIZE_CAST_UNSUPPORTED` for an illegal pair (predict alike). `u64` above 2^53 stays exact. Skill `materialize-casts` | additive (keys, codes) | declare the target type where a downstream consumer needs it | cohort-reshape (E2-S1, E2-S2) |
| no way to relabel a categorical on write | per column `recode {map, order}`: relabel, merge, map to null (needs a nullable output), reorder; a `map` key the source dictionary lacks is `PULSE_MATERIALIZE_RECODE_UNKNOWN_LABEL` at predict and at run (the drift guard for a stored recipe meeting a new cohort); order names every written label once. Per column `bucket {ranges XOR range_table}`: a numeric (half-open `[start, end)`) or date-family (inclusive; a datetime by its local day in `time_zone`) column written as the categorical of its range labels (narrowest width, dictionary in declared order); range refusals reuse the `PULSE_RANGE_*` codes; a value outside every range (or NaN) follows `on_cast_error` (`no_range` / `not_finite`). `on_cast_error` is now accepted with `type`, `recode` or `bucket`. Order per column: recode, bucket, type, nullability. Skill `materialize-recode` | additive (keys, code) | none | cohort-reshape (E3-S1, E3-S2) |
| a materialized cohort was always one file, every constant stored on every row, and a source's parent groups were not carried | `output.shards {max_records}` writes a shard archive (rows in output order, published whole or not at all; a row opening shard 65,536 fails the run with `PULSE_SHARD_COUNT_LIMIT`, now also raised by materialize; predict flags it as a risk); `output.elide_constants` stores single-valued columns once (`0x02` only when something is elided); `output.groups` declares parent groups by output column name (`[]` opts out), else a source group whose members are all written unchanged is inherited, and one that is not is dropped with the warning `PULSE_MATERIALIZE_GROUP_NOT_INHERITED` (fatal under `Strict`); declaration refusals reuse `PULSE_GROUP_*`. `MaterializeResult` gains `Shards`, `ElidedConstants`, `Groups` (`omitempty`); predict reports `container {kind, max_records, max_shards, elide_constants, groups}`. Skill `materialize-container`. Decoding is unchanged: a grouped or sharded output answers every request as its plain twin does | additive (keys, fields, warning) | none | cohort-reshape (E4-S1, E4-S2) |
| exporting (CSV, TSV, NDJSON, JSON array, Parquet, Arrow, Excel, SPSS) from a shard-archive cohort failed | works: the archive is read through its canonical `_schema.pulse` over each shard's records in archive order (a truncated tail dropped per shard), equal to the single-file export; `.sav` applies the carried sidecar | behaviour change (refusal removed) | none | cohort-reshape (E4-S3) |
| exporting (every format), `PredictExport` or a `.sav` from an anchored source `archive.pulse#shard.pulse` failed with a plain file-not-found, though `pulse.Open` reads the same path | works: that one shard is read under the archive's CANONICAL `_schema.pulse` (gated per shard like a whole-archive export, `details.entry`), so the output equals the archive's export filtered to the shard, on every format; a set-widened or grouped archive included. Open / Process of the same anchor keep reading the shard's own header schema: the values are the same, a dictionary or set rung may differ. An unknown, empty or reserved anchor, or an anchor into a single file, is refused with `pulse.Open`'s code and details (`PULSE_SHARD_MISSING`, `PULSE_SHARD_RESERVED_NAME`, `PULSE_ARCHIVE_MAGIC_INVALID`); a deflated shard is `PULSE_ARCHIVE_CORRUPT`, as for a whole-archive export; a missing archive file is the same plain error as a missing single file. A `.sav` reads the ARCHIVE's SPSS sidecar (fresh, stale or absent exactly as for a whole-archive export), never a per-shard one; sidecar messages name the archive path | behaviour change (refusal removed) | none | cohort-reshape (E6-S1) |
| a cohort exported to `.sav` and imported back read every `u4`–`u64`, `f32`, `packed_bool` and `decimal128` column as `f64`, a `categorical_u16` / `_u32` as `categorical_u8`, a multiple-dichotomy `set_*` at the narrowest rung its members need (a 3-member `set_u16` came back `set_u8`) and a `vec_f32`'s element variables as `f64` | **Write:** the `.sav` export records the Pulse type as a hidden record `7/18` variable attribute `@PulseType('<type>')` (`<type>` the field type's name, or `decimal128(<precision>,<scale>)`) only on a variable the plain re-import would type differently: an unlabelled numeric of `u4`–`u64` / `f32` / `packed_bool` / `decimal128`, a `categorical_u16` / `_u32`, a `vec_f32` element, and — on the set's bit-0 member — a `set_*` rung wider than its member count needs (synthesised and sidecar path alike). A variable owed both a column type and a set rung carries ONE attribute with two values, column type first: `@PulseType('u8'\n'set_u16'\n)`. Any `@PulseType` already in a carried source `7/18` is dropped and re-derived from the cohort (a retyped column never keeps a stale hint); a source `7/18` Pulse cannot parse stays verbatim and gains no hint. **Read:** a hint is restored only when every non-missing value fits it exactly — integers whole and in range, `u64` strictly below 2^53, `packed_bool` 0 / 1, `f32` exactly representable, `decimal128(p,s)` within its digits and `abs(v)·10^s < 2^50`; a set rung only when it has a bit per member. A value that does not fit, an unreadable hint (any other multi-value form included), or a hint the variable's SPSS shape cannot carry keeps the plain mapping (the stored doubles, never rounded) and raises one NEW warning `PULSE_SPSS_TYPE_NOT_RESTORED` (`ImportReport.SourceWarnings`; details `variable`, `pulse_type`, and `value` for a misfit; key constant `errors.DetailSPSSPulseType`). Import predict and run read the same schema. A `vec_*` still cannot round-trip (SPSS has no vector) | behaviour change (`.sav` export bytes; re-import types of a Pulse-written `.sav`) + additive (warning code, detail key) | a `.sav` export of a cohort with no hinted column, and every sidecar-path export of an SPSS-origin cohort nothing retyped or widened, is byte-identical; byte digests of other synthesised `.sav` exports move once. A consumer that re-imports a Pulse-written `.sav` and relied on `f64` / `categorical_u8` columns now sees the original types — retype with materialize `output` `type` where it needs the old one. SPSS hides `@`-prefixed attributes from its Variable View | cohort-reshape (E6-S2, E6-S3, E6-S4) |
| a `.sav` export rounded a `u64` value past 2^53 into its variable's double without a word (2^53+1 was stored as 2^53) | the export raises one NEW warning `PULSE_SPSS_INTEGER_INEXACT` per variable on the export's `TargetWarnings` (details `variable`, `cohort_field`, `count`, `first_row` 1-based, `value` the first one's exact decimal; key constants `errors.DetailSPSSCount`, `errors.DetailSPSSFirstRow`); 2^53 itself is silent. Under Strict the first such value fails the encode and nothing is written: NEW `io.ExportJob.Strict` (the facade sets it under `Options.Strict`) rides NEW `io.CohortSource.Strict` to the writer; CLI `pulse export spss --strict` (new flag on the existing leaf; no other export leaf takes it). `ConvertJob.StrictRows` (`pulse convert --strict`) is broadened from dropped rows to this code too, riding NEW `io.ConvertSource.Strict`, which is handed to the target even for a stream that is not schema-faithful (`Schema` and `Sidecar` nil). Export predict lists the same code, without `count` / `first_row`, for every `u64` numeric variable — a risk, never a refusal | additive (warning code, fields, CLI flag) + behaviour change (only under `Strict` / `StrictRows`: an export or convert holding such a value now fails) | read the new warning; under `Strict`, keep `u64` values at or below 2^53 or export to a format that stores them exactly (CSV, NDJSON, Parquet, Arrow) | cohort-reshape (E6-S3, E6-S4) |
| a reshape recipe was re-authored per cohort | a reshape is a request, so it is stored as a `target: "request"` template with the cohort as `{"$var": "cohort"}`: `RenderTemplateRequest`, `PredictMaterialize`, `Materialize`. Docs: `docs/src/library/request-templating.md` (A stored reshape recipe), `docs/src/library/materialize.md`; skill `derived-cohorts` (the router) and example `materialize_reshape_for_handoff` (category `materialize`, the `materialize` example host of `TestLimitsDefaultsNeverTripGoldens`) | additive (docs, example) | none | cohort-reshape (E5-S1) |

New public symbols (all in `internal/apigolden/testdata/public_api.txt`): `pulse.Request.Output`, `(*Pulse).PredictMaterialize`, `MaterializeResult.{Shards, ElidedConstants, Groups}`; `types.MaterializeOutput`, `MaterializeOutputColumn`, `MaterializeRecode`, `MaterializeBucket`, `MaterializeBucketRange`, `MaterializeShards`, `MaterializeGroup`; `descriptor.MaterializePredictResult`, `MaterializePredictColumn`, `MaterializeRisk`, `MaterializeContainer`, `MaterializeGroupPlan`; `io.ExportJob.Strict`, `io.CohortSource.Strict`, `io.ConvertSource.Strict`; `errors.PULSE_SPSS_TYPE_NOT_RESTORED`, `PULSE_SPSS_INTEGER_INEXACT`, `DetailSPSSPulseType`, `DetailSPSSCount`, `DetailSPSSFirstRow`. New codes (12, each with `codeMetadata` fixups and an owner entry): ten `PULSE_MATERIALIZE_*` — `_OUTPUT_ONLY`, `_OUTPUT_INVALID`, `_OUTPUT_UNKNOWN_COLUMN`, `_OUTPUT_DUPLICATE`, `_OUTPUT_NULL`, `_CAST_UNSUPPORTED`, `_CAST_FAILED`, `_CAST_NULLED`, `_RECODE_UNKNOWN_LABEL`, `_GROUP_NOT_INHERITED` — and two `PULSE_SPSS_*` warnings — `PULSE_SPSS_TYPE_NOT_RESTORED`, `PULSE_SPSS_INTEGER_INEXACT`. `PULSE_SHARD_COUNT_LIMIT` is not new; materialize now raises it too. New feature: `capability:materialize_output`. New CLI flag: `pulse export spss --strict`. New skills: `materialize-casts`, `materialize-recode`, `materialize-container`. The earlier known limit — a `.sav` re-import read integers as `f64` — is closed by the `@PulseType` row above for every Pulse-written `.sav`; a third-party `.sav` without the attribute still maps its numerics to `f64`, as SPSS stores them.

## Changes from observability-adapters

Not yet released (rides the next alpha). `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change. Covers the observability adapters moving into the root module and `pulse mcp`'s OTLP export. Guide: [Observability](../../src/library/observability.md).

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| nested module `github.com/frankbardon/pulse/contrib/otelpulse` (own `go.mod`, lockstep tag `contrib/otelpulse/vX`) | package `github.com/frankbardon/pulse/observe/otel` in the root module; package name `otelpulse` and every symbol unchanged (`Hooks`, `Metrics`, `SpanName`, `Attr*`, `DefaultBuckets`); `ScopeName` is now `"github.com/frankbardon/pulse/observe/otel"` | breaking (import path) | change the import to `otelpulse "github.com/frankbardon/pulse/observe/otel"` and drop the `contrib/otelpulse` require from `go.mod` (`go mod tidy`); a dashboard or filter keyed on the instrumentation scope name follows the new `ScopeName` | observability-adapters (E1-S1) |
| nested module `github.com/frankbardon/pulse/contrib/prompulse` | package `github.com/frankbardon/pulse/observe/prom` in the root module; package name `prompulse` and every symbol unchanged (`Metrics`, `WithBuckets`, `DefaultBuckets`) | breaking (import path) | change the import to `prompulse "github.com/frankbardon/pulse/observe/prom"` and drop the `contrib/prompulse` require | observability-adapters (E1-S1) |
| no more `contrib/<module>/vX` tags | the adapters ride the root tag | release change | pin Pulse only | observability-adapters (E1-S3) |
| the Pulse root module required no OpenTelemetry or Prometheus module | the root `go.mod` requires OpenTelemetry (v1.47.0, incl. the OTLP/HTTP exporters) and `client_golang`; only `observe/otel`, `observe/prom` and the CLI import them, so a program that imports neither adapter links none of it | dependency change | none; `go mod tidy` may list the new requires in your `go.sum` | observability-adapters (E1-S1, E1-S2) |
| `pulse mcp` exported metrics only through `--metrics-addr` | `pulse mcp` also exports traces and metrics over OTLP/HTTP when a standard `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_*ENDPOINT` is set (`OTEL_SERVICE_NAME`, `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES` and the SDK's other `OTEL_*` apply); a non-http(s) endpoint or a non-`http/protobuf` protocol fails startup with `CLI_INPUT`; export errors only warn | added (opt-in) | none; unset `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_*ENDPOINT` keeps the old behaviour. A host that already sets a gRPC `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_PROTOCOL` with an endpoint for other processes must unset it for `pulse mcp` or switch to `http/protobuf`. `mcpserve` embedders wire `observe/otel` themselves | observability-adapters (E1-S4) |

## Changes from vibe-2026-10-31

Not yet released (rides the next alpha). `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ATTR_PERCENTILE` ranked tied rows with an unstable sort, so on a cohort of more than a dozen rows a tied row could read a different percentile from run to run (and tied rows at a cut-off fell either side of it at random) | tied rows still take distinct ranks, now in scan order: of two rows with the same value the earlier record ranks lower, so the same cohort always reads the same percentiles; untied rows and null rows are unchanged. Skill `op-attr-percentile`, the operator's `Purpose` and `Interpretation` state the rule | behaviour change | none; a stored output that relied on a particular tied row's percentile may move once. Cut on the value (`AGG_PERCENTILE`, then a filter) for a split that ignores row order | vibe-2026-10-31 (E1-S1) |
| `OVERLAY_CHISQ_VS_POP` compared only the subset's listed values, so a population category with no subset rows never entered the test (χ² and `df` blind to a category that went missing, biased toward not rejecting) | when the subset's facet listing is complete (`truncated_at` absent / 0), every population category the subset lacks enters at observed 0 with its population share: χ², `df`, `summary.count` and the `PULSE_OVERLAY_EXPECTED_LOW` check count it. Under `discrete_top_k` truncation nothing changes (an unlisted category is unknown, not 0). Equals R `chisq.test(x, p, rescale.p = TRUE)` with the zero cells. Skill `op-overlay-chisq-vs-pop`, the kind's capability, `Purpose` and `Interpretation` state the rule | behaviour change | none; a stored χ² / p / `df` on a subset missing a population category moves (χ² and `df` rise) | vibe-2026-10-31 (E1-S2) |
| `TEST_PROP_Z` had no expected-count guard: a run with few expected successes or failures per group reported its normal-approximation z and p with no signal, while `TEST_CHISQ` warned on the same thin table | the result's `warnings` carry `PULSE_TEST_EXPECTED_COUNT_TOO_LOW` when any group's expected successes N*_g·p̂ or failures N*_g·(1 − p̂) is below 5 — p̂ the pooled rate (both two-proportion variants, CUPED included) or `p0` (one-proportion); N*_g is the base the z reads (n unweighted, Σw_g under frequency, Kish n_eff_g under probability). Like `TEST_CHISQ`'s, it is a per-result string warning only: not lifted onto the envelope, not escalated by `Strict`, no predict counterpart (data-dependent); figures unchanged. The code's owners add `TEST_PROP_Z` (feature profiles) and its Message names the two-proportion case. Skill `op-test-prop-z` lists it | additive (new warning) | none; on a warned run prefer `TEST_FISHER_EXACT` (two groups) or a larger sample | vibe-2026-10-31 (E1-S3) |
| A facet `OVERLAY_INDEX_VS_POP` / `OVERLAY_ZSCORE_VS_POP` / `OVERLAY_CHISQ_VS_POP` on a field with a caller-supplied `replace` label binding compared the host's LABELLED values against the population's RAW ones: every relabelled host value warned `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_ZERO` and lost its figure, and `OVERLAY_CHISQ_VS_POP` also counted each raw population category at observed 0 | ONE resolver labels both sides: the population facet runs unlabelled (no bindings, no `AutoLabels` injection) and its overlay field is relabelled by the resolver that labelled the host, so both share the labelled key space the host shows: the figures equal the unlabelled run's, keyed by label, with no spurious `PULSE_OVERLAY_REF_ZERO`. This also fixes a function-driven label table (`LabelTable.Lookup`) whose two raw values share a label: its online collision detection gives the clean label to the first value seen, and with a resolver per side host and population could hand it to different raw values when their value orders differed (a request binding or an `AutoLabels` default alike); the clean label now stays with the source the host gave it. Rows-backed tables were unaffected. A caller `augment` binding on a field that also has a `replace` `AutoLabels` default keeps the population raw like the host | behaviour change (bug fix) | none; a stored layer from a replace-labelled facet overlay moves to the correct figures | vibe-2026-10-31 (E1-S7, E1-S8) |
| `TEST_KS` applied its limiting (asymptotic) p to tie-heavy data with no signal, while the rank tests warned on the same ties | on the asymptotic branch only (n₁·n₂ ≥ 10000, Σw under frequency weights) the row test and `TEST_KS/two_sample_post` add `PULSE_TEST_TIES_DOMINATE` to the result's `warnings` when ≥ 50% of the pooled values are tied — the rank tests' rule and message; weighted, a row of weight w is a tie of w, so the verdict equals the expanded rows' unweighted one. Like the rank tests', it is a per-result string warning only: not lifted onto the envelope, not escalated by `Strict`, no predict counterpart (data-dependent); figures unchanged. The exact branch and `OVERLAY_KS_VS_POP` are unchanged. The code's Message covers the distribution-test case. Skill `op-test-ks` lists it | additive (new warning) | none; on a warned run read the p as conservative, or compare the tied-value shares directly (`TEST_CHISQ` on a tied-vs-not indicator) | vibe-2026-10-31 (E1-S9) |

## Changes from U52 (synth realism)

Not yet released (rides the next alpha). `format_version` stays `"1.1"`; no `.pulse` byte-layout change; a spec using none of the new surface generates byte-identically. Skill: `skills/synth-datetime.md`.

| Old | New | Kind | How to adapt | Unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a synth spec could not declare `datetime` (`unknown field type "datetime"`) | `datetime` is declarable; distribution `uniform_datetime` (`synth.DistUniformDateTime`) draws a local wall-clock time by `hour_weights` / `weekday_weights` in a fixed `time_zone` or a per-row `time_zone_field`, resolves DST per `dst_policy` (`error` default, `earlier`, `later`) and stores the UTC epoch second; codes `PULSE_SYNTH_DST_NONEXISTENT` / `PULSE_SYNTH_DST_AMBIGUOUS` | added | none; adopt when you need timestamps | U52 (E1-S1) |
| `synth from-profile` over a profile holding a `datetime` field failed at schema build | it generates the field from the profile: a document captured by this release carries a `datetime` section and rebuilds as an unzoned `uniform_datetime` (next row); an older document's `numeric` summary still rebuilds as a `normal` over the epoch seconds clamped to the observed range | behaviour change (refusal → generation) | none | U52 (E1-S1, E1-S2) |
| `profile create` summarised a `datetime` column as `numeric` (min / max / mean / std of epoch seconds), so it joined `pairwise` correlations, `conditional` numeric pairs and `--fit-models` targets | the column gets a `datetime` section instead (`synth.DatetimeProfile`: UTC `start` / `end` day, `weekdays` counts Monday first, `hours` counts) and no `numeric` one; it joins no correlation, numeric pair or model. `SpecFromProfile` rebuilds it as `uniform_datetime` with `weekday_weights` (count ÷ that weekday's days in range) and `hour_weights` (the hour counts), no zone | behaviour change | a profile document over a datetime cohort changes shape; one without a datetime column is byte-identical. Re-capture to get the new reconstruction | U52 (E1-S2) |
| synth rule / constraint expressions had no time functions (`trend`, `season`, `between` were plain names) | `trend(t, slope)`, `season(t, "dow"\|"hour"\|"month", weights)` and `between(t, a, b)` read a `date` / `datetime` field on its own clock (the field's zone, the drawn row zone for `time_zone_field`, else UTC); consume no RNG. A field named `trend` / `season` / `between` still reads bare, but calling the function then is refused (`PULSE_SYNTH_RULE_EXPR_INVALID`) | added | none; a spec that does not call them generates byte-identically | U52 (E1-S2) |
| synth rules could not draw; an outcome drawn from a computed probability needed a written `uniform` helper column | `uniform()` and `bernoulli(p)` draw in a rule's `when` / `set_expr` on a counter-based stream per call site (never the field RNG); refused in `constraints[]`, with bad arguments or a `p` outside [0, 1] (`PULSE_SYNTH_RULE_DRAW_INVALID`, new). A field named `uniform` / `bernoulli` still reads bare, but calling the function then is refused (`PULSE_SYNTH_RULE_EXPR_INVALID`) | added | none; a spec that does not call them generates byte-identically | U52 (E1-S3) |
| every spec field was written to the output | `FieldSpec.Output` (`"output": false`) makes a helper: drawn, read and written by rules and constraints, absent from the written schema and records; the other columns are value-identical to the spec with the helper written. A fidelity report gives a model over a helper an `error` and skips a pair or correlation over one with a warning; a spec with no written field is `SERVICE_VALIDATION` | added | none; a spec without `output` is byte-identical. Code reading `FieldSpec` by position, or building `synth.FieldSpec` with unkeyed literals, sees one more field | U52 (E1-S3) |
| a synth `u4` draw outside 0–15 was stored MASKED to its low nibble — a 16 became 0, a 17 became 1, and a negative draw went through Go's implementation-defined float-to-unsigned conversion (a `uniform` with `max: 15.999` wrote its top rounding bucket as 0) | it CLAMPS to 0 / 15 like the `u8`–`u64` arms; in-range draws are unchanged | behaviour change | none for a spec whose `u4` draws stay in 0–15 (every shipped spec fixture regenerates byte-identically); a spec relying on the wrap now gets the end value — narrow the distribution if the clamp mass at 15 or 0 is unwanted | U52 (E1-S4) |
| a synth `categorical_*` that an unconditional rule writes on every row still had to declare a throwaway `weighted_categorical` (`field has empty distribution`) | it may omit `distribution` (and must then omit `params`) when a `set` / `set_expr` with no `when`, not reading the field, determines it; it draws nothing and consumes no RNG. Any other empty distribution stays `SERVICE_VALIDATION`; `params` on such a field is a new `SERVICE_VALIDATION` | relaxed refusal | none; a spec that declared the throwaway sampler is unchanged. Dropping it now consumes one draw less per row, so the other fields' values move | U52 (E1-S4) |
| the `owns_nulls` divergence warning said "the gate accounts for only part of the field's missingness; widen its when" in BOTH directions, so a gate firing on MORE rows than the captured rate was told to widen | a realised rate ABOVE the discarded `null_rate` gets its own sentence ("the gate fires on more rows than the field's captured missingness; narrow its when, check the gate field's generated rate…"); the under-firing wording is unchanged, and both still group as `rule owns_nulls discarded a null_rate it did not replace`. The `--suggest-rules` gating note and stderr footer no longer say the target's own `null_rate` still fires beneath the gate — the candidate carries `owns_nulls`, which discards it | behaviour change | a caller matching the warning text on an over-firing gate matches the new sentence; match ` the nulls of field ` (the stable part) instead | U52 (E1-S4) |
| `profile create --fit-models` regressed each numeric on categorical levels and `set_*` options only; a numeric driver reached a modelled field only through `--residual-correlations` (a churn flag modelled on contract alone), and a cohort with no categorical or `set_*` field had every numeric skipped (`no categorical or set_* candidate predictors in the cohort`) | a numeric also enters the model of every numeric declared AFTER it in the schema whose variance it explains (adjusted r², the same 0.01 floor): `models[].predictors[]` gains `{"kind": "numeric", "field": …, "level": "", "coefficient": …}` (per unit of the field's value). `SpecFromProfile` carries the term onto `Spec.Models`; generation draws each modelled numeric after the modelled numerics it reads (schema order otherwise) and reads the predictor's stored value; a hand-authored spec whose numeric terms form a cycle is `SERVICE_VALIDATION`; the fidelity report's `models` section refits a numeric term as the field's own value. A numeric-only cohort whose numerics relate now gets models; when none relate the old skip warnings are unchanged | behaviour change (profile capture output; generation of a spec carrying a numeric term) | a profile whose numerics relate gains numeric predictors, so its document, its twin and its `residual_correlations` (now ~0 for a pair joined by a numeric predictor) move — re-capture deliberately. A capture where no numeric predictor clears selection is byte-identical, and so is every spec without a numeric term. Declare a cohort's outcome fields after their drivers to read the models in the natural direction. Code switching on `ModelPredictor.Kind` sees `numeric` | U52 (E2-S1) |
| a synth target named by several conditional pairs (two categoricals bidding for one categorical, a categorical and a set option bidding for one numeric, …) went to the FIRST-DECLARED pair — on a profile-derived spec, the earliest field in the schema; options of one `set_*` field were captured and generated independently, so a dependency such as "tv requires broadband" came back as soft noise (5,113 tv-without-broadband rows in a 40,000-row twin of a source with none) | profiles now capture within-set option dependencies, and contested targets go to the strongest association. `conditional.set_set_pairs` gains `set_a == set_b` entries — at most one per option, from the earlier option with the strongest bias-corrected Cramér's V, kept at V ≥ 0.1 — and generation draws them after the set-categorical pairs, so a never-observed combination is generated with probability 0. `resolveConflicts` keeps the pair with the strongest association per target (bias-corrected Cramér's V for a categorical or set-option target, adjusted η² for a numeric one; ties and any numeric pair lacking per-category counts go to the first-declared). New: `Spec.PairArbitration` (`"strength"` default, `"declared"` = the old rule), `synth.PairArbitrationStrength` / `PairArbitrationDeclared`, `CategoricalNumericCategorySpec.N` (`n`, filled by `SpecFromProfile`). A `set_set_pairs` entry naming one field on both sides is now accepted (one OPTION on both sides is `SERVICE_VALIDATION`) | behaviour change (profile capture output; generation of a spec with contested pairs) | a profile of a cohort with a `set_*` field whose options depend on each other gains `set_set_pairs` entries, and any spec whose pairs contest a target generates a different twin — re-capture and regenerate deliberately; set `"pair_arbitration": "declared"` to keep the old arbitration exactly. An emitted spec's numeric pair categories gain `n`. A spec without contested pairs or within-set pairs is byte-identical | U52 (E2-S2) |
| `profile create --fit-models` fitted a `packed_bool` target by least squares and generation read the linear-probability coefficients through the bernoulli step as a probit, attenuating every effect that saturates near 0 or 1 (a churn twin kept 0.42–0.60× the source's churn pseudo-R²) | profiles now capture boolean models as probits: the `models` entry gains `"link": "probit"` (`synth.ModelLinkProbit`, `FieldModel.Link`), its `intercept` / coefficients are probit coefficients, `residual_std` is 1, `r2` is McFadden's pseudo-R² and the in-process residual reservoir is `y − Φ(η)`. `SpecFromProfile` maps it onto the unchanged bernoulli draw (`P(1 \| row) = Φ(intercept + Σ coefficient·x)`), so `Spec.Models` entries for such targets carry rescaled numbers. A fit that does not converge (separation) keeps the linear fit and warns `model for numeric field "x" kept its linear fit: …`; a `link` on a non-bernoulli target is not applied (warned) | behaviour change (profile capture output; generation from a re-captured profile) | re-capture profiles with boolean model targets deliberately — the document, its `residual_correlations` and the twin move (churn pseudo-R² twin/source 0.95–1.06×). Profiles captured before keep their linear fits and generate as before; a profile with no boolean model target is byte-identical. Read a boolean target's coefficients as probit slopes | U52 (E2-S2) |
| the fidelity report scored each marginal with a p-value only (at report sizes a trivial difference rejects), and compared the twin only with the CAPTURED model (`models` could call an effect recovered while the twin's contrast against the source was compressed) | every `fields[]` entry with a `result` gains `effect_size` (`synth.FieldEffectSize`: Cohen's d / Cramér's V with a Cohen 1988 `magnitude`); a report over a spec with an applied model gains `utility.target_rates` (`synth.TargetRateFidelity`: the target's mean per level on source and synthetic, spreads, ε², `verdict`); `SynthOptions.FidelityModel` / `synth from-profile --fidelity-model` refits a supplied request's regressions on both partitions into `utility.model_fits` (`synth.UtilityModelFit`: `pseudo_r2` / `r2`, `fit_ratio`, coefficients); new code `PULSE_SYNTH_FIDELITY_MODEL_INVALID` | added (report keys, option, flag, code) | none; keys are additive `omitempty`, so a reader of the old shape is unaffected — a report's bytes do change (every scored marginal carries `effect_size`). A models-free spec without a supplied model carries no `utility` | U52 (E2-S3) |
| the fidelity report scored `fields[]` marginals for strict numerics and categoricals only — a `packed_bool`, `u4`, `date` or `datetime` column had no entry, a `set_*` option carried rates without an effect size, and nothing said when a structural rule (which reaches generated rows only) caused a difference | `packed_bool` is scored by `TEST_CHISQ` against `_synthetic` (V = \|phi\|), `u4` / `date` (epoch days) / `datetime` (epoch seconds) by `TEST_KS` + Cohen's d; a `datetime` entry gains `shape` (`synth.TimeShapeFidelity`: UTC hour-of-day and weekday TVD); each `set_fields[].options[]` entry gains `effect_size` (V of its 2x2 table); a marginal flagged at an effect of at least `small` on a field a rule writes gains `rule_note` naming the rules (`fields[]` and `set_fields[]`) | added (report keys) | none; keys are additive `omitempty`. A report over a cohort with one of those types gains its entries (code counting `fields[]` sees more); a report over none of them, with no rule-written flag, is byte-identical | U52 (E2-S4) |
| `synth from-profile` always wrote the source rows into the output (tagged `_synthetic=false`), and `constraints[]` was reachable on `synth from-schema` only | `SynthOptions.SyntheticOnly` / `--synthetic-only` writes the generated rows alone, under the source's schema with no `_synthetic` column; the fidelity report is unchanged (built from an in-memory tagged cohort). `--constraints <file>` (a bare `constraints` array, REPLACES, compiled eagerly; refusals name the file in `details.path`) and `--max-rejection-rate` reach the profile path; library callers set `Spec.Constraints` / `MaxRejectionRate` on the `SpecFromProfile` result. Unset, every output and report is byte-identical | additive (option, flags) | none | U52 (E3-S1) |
| a fidelity report said nothing about how close the twin sits to real records — no exact-match count, no distance to closest record | every report written against a source gains a `disclosure` section (`synth.DisclosureFidelity`: `exact_matches` / `exact_match_rate` over every output column and every generated row, `dcr_median` / `dcr_p5` of the Gower distance to closest record over a seeded sample, `sample_rows`) by DEFAULT; `SynthOptions.DisclosureSample` / `--disclosure-sample` caps the sample (0 = 10,000; negative is `SERVICE_VALIDATION`), `SynthOptions.DisableDisclosure` / `--no-disclosure` drops the section. Its working set is charged to `Limits.MaxEstimatedMemory` before generation (`PULSE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, nothing written; the default limit is unlimited). The generated `.pulse` bytes never move | behaviour change (report gains a key) + additive (options, flags) | a reader of the report JSON that refuses unknown keys must accept `disclosure`, or pass `--no-disclosure` / `DisableDisclosure: true` to keep the old document byte for byte; a tight `max_estimated_memory` may now refuse a reported run — lower the sample or switch the section off | U52 (E3-S2) |

## Third-party dependency

- `afero.Fs` is a frozen third-party type in the v1 API (`Options.FS`, the `io` factory). No change; no Pulse-owned filesystem interface replaces it.
